L12: ## NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation
L13: Application Type	NDA
Application Number(s)	210806 and 210807
Priority or Standard	Standard
Submit Date(s)	October 21, 2017
Received Date(s)	October 23, 2017
PDUFA Goal Date	October 23, 2018
Division/Office	DAVP/OAP
Review Completion Date	See DARRTS electronic signature page
Established Name	Doravirine, DOR and doravirine/lamivudine/tenofovir disoproxil fumarate, DOR, 3TC, TDF, fixed dose combination (FDC) tablet
(Proposed) Trade Name	PIFELTRO (DOR), DELSTRIGO (DOR, 3TC, TDF)
Pharmacologic Class	Non-nucleoside reverse transcriptase inhibitor
Code name	MK-1439 (DOR), and MK-1439A (DOR, 3TC, TDF)
Applicant	Merck
Formulation(s)	Doravirine 100 mg tablet and doravirine/lamivudine/tenofovir disoproxil fumarate 100mg/300mg/300 mg fixed dose combination tablet
Dosing Regimen	One tablet taken orally once daily with or without food
Applicant Proposed Indication(s)/Population(s)	Doravirine: In combination with other antiretroviral agents for the treatment of HIV-1 infection in treatment-naïve patients
Doravirine/lamivudine/tenofovir disoproxil fumarate fixed dose combination tablet: is indicated as a complete regimen for the treatment of HIV-1 infection in treatment-naïve patients
Recommendation on Regulatory Action	Approval
Recommended Indication(s)/Population(s) (if applicable)	PIFELTRO™ is indicated in combination with other antiretroviral agents for the treatment of HIV-1 infection in adult patients with no prior antiretroviral treatment history.
DELSTRIGO™ is indicated as a complete regimen for the treatment of HIV-1 infection in adult patients with no prior antiretroviral treatment history

L70: ## 1. Executive Summary Office Level Concurrence
L71: These NDAs for doravirine (DOR) and doravirine/lamivudine/tenofovir disoproxil fumarate (DOR/3TC/TDF) fixed-dose combination tablets for oral use are submitted by Merck. DOR is a new non-nucleoside reverse transcriptase inhibitor (NNRTI), and lamivudine and tenofovir disoproxil fumarate are previously approved nucleos(t)ide reverse transcriptase inhibitors. These NDAs were reviewed by the multi-disciplinary review team. Each discipline has recommended approval of both NDAs, and we concur with those recommendations. DOR will be approved in combination with other antiretroviral agents for the treatment of HIV-1 infection in adult patients with no prior antiretroviral treatment history and DOR/3TC/TDF will be approved as a complete regimen for the treatment of HIV-1 infection in adult patients with no prior antiretroviral treatment history.
L72: The applicant has submitted two Phase 3 adequate and well-controlled trials that provide substantial evidence of efficacy for the indications approved. DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF are safe for the intended use. The DOR development program demonstrated improved safety over protease inhibitor based and efavirenz based comparator regimens for certain pre-specified analyses of fasting lipid laboratory measures (LDL-C and non-HDL-C) and clinical neuropsychiatric endpoints (neuropsychiatric AEs of dizziness, sleep disorders and disturbances, and altered sensorium). The Statistical Review team concurs that appropriate statistical inference testing was conducted for these fasting lipid measures and neuropsychiatric AEs, but recommends confidence intervals and not p values be included in labeling. Labeling will include both as this is consistent with Guidance and precedent (see Section 9.5 for a more detailed discussion). We concur that identified risks can be mitigated through labeling and further evaluated during routine pharmacovigilance. The overall Benefit Risk is favorable. For detailed information supporting the basis for this approval, please refer to the detailed reviews included in this Uni-Review document, the Product Quality Review, and the Benefit Risk Summary.
L73: ## 1.1. Product Introduction
L74: Pifeltro™ (doravirine, or DOR) is a new NNRTI that was developed to use in combination with other ARV agents for the treatment of HIV-1 infection in treatment-naïve patients. Delstrigo™ (DOR/3TC/TDF) is a FDC tablet containing DOR and the NRTIs lamivudine and tenofovir disoproxil fumarate that was developed as a complete regimen for the treatment of HIV-1 infection in treatment-naïve patients. DOR inhibits HIV-1 replication by non-competitive binding of HIV-1 reverse transcriptase. DOR, an NME, will be the sixth NNRTI added to this mechanistic class.
L75: Established name: Doravirine (MK-1439)
L76: Chemical class: New molecular entity
L77: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L78: Pharmacologic class: NNRTI
L79: FDC tablet: DOR/3TC/TDF (MK-1439A)
L80: Trade Name: Pifeltro™ (DOR alone), and Delstrigo™ (DOR/3TC/TDF FDC tablet)
L81: Proposed Indication: DOR is indicated in combination with other antiretroviral agents for the treatment of HIV-1 infection in treatment-naïve patients. DOR/3TC/TDF is indicated as a complete regimen for the treatment of HIV-1 infection in treatment-naïve patients.
L82: Dosage Form: Tablet (DOR 100 mg), and Tablet (DOR 100 mg/3TC 300 mg /TDF 300 mg)
L83: Dose and regimen: One tablet once daily
L84: ## 1.2. Conclusions on the Substantial Evidence of Effectiveness
L85: Data from the two randomized Phase 3 trials included in this application provide substantial evidence of effectiveness to support approval of DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF for the treatment of HIV-1 infection in adult patients with no antiretroviral treatment history. PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD evaluated the effectiveness of DOR-containing treatment in the HIV-1 treatment-naïve population through Week 48. These trials support noninferiority of DOR-containing treatment to the respective active controls, DRV/r- and EFV-containing regimens, using the primary efficacy endpoint of HIV-1 RNA &lt;50 copies/mL at Week 48. In PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, 83.8% and 79.9% had HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL at Week 48 in the DOR and DRV/r arms, respectively: the treatment difference was 3.9% (95.001% CI: -1.6%, 9.4%). In PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, 84.3% and 80.8% had HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL at Week 48 in the DOR/3TC/TDF and EFV/FTC/TDF arms, respectively: the treatment difference was 3.5% (95% CI: -2.0%, 9.0%). These trials adequately establish the effectiveness of DOR-containing treatment in the HIV-1 treatment-naïve population.
L86: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L87: ## 1.3. Benefit-Risk Assessment
L88: ## Benefit-Risk Summary and Assessment
L89: DOR is a new NNRTI indicated in combination with other ARVs for treatment of HIV-1 infection in adult patients with no prior ARV treatment history. DOR/3TC/TDF is a three-drug combination of DOR, 3TC, and TDF (the latter two both NRTIs) indicated as a complete regimen for the treatment of HIV-1 infection in adult patients with no antiretroviral treatment history. Approval of DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF for the stated indications is fully supported by available evidence and analysis of efficacy and safety data.
L90: HIV is a significant public health concern, both globally and domestically. Without effective treatment, HIV leads to progressive destruction of the immune system and therefore is a serious, life-threatening condition. Standard of care HIV treatment is often lifelong and involves the administration of multiple drugs from different mechanistic classes targeting different events in the HIV life cycle. Current US HIV Treatment Guidelines recommend an INSTI plus 2 NRTIs as recommended initial regimens for most people with HIV based on durable virologic efficacy, favorable tolerability and toxicity profiles, and ease of use. NNRTI-containing regimens, such as EFV and RPV, are not recommended as initial regimens for most people with HIV based on considerations including treatment-limiting adverse events (EFV and neuropsychiatric adverse events, RPV and psychiatric adverse events) and lower virologic efficacy in patients with high HIV RNA and low CD4 cell count (RPV). Boosted PI-containing regimens such as DRV/r are not recommended as initial regimens for most people with HIV based on considerations including dyslipidemia. However, currently approved NNRTI- and boosted PI-containing regimens may be recommended initial regimens in certain clinical situations, for example a boosted PI may be preferred when a high genetic barrier to resistance is important for regimen selection.
L91: The HIV treatment armamentarium would benefit from another antiretroviral treatment option that is efficacious, well-tolerated, without clinically relevant DDIs and food restrictions, and has a convenient dosing regimen.
L92: The efficacy of DOR-containing treatment in the HIV-1 treatment-naïve population through Week 48 was established in two randomized Phase 3 trials. These trials support noninferiority of DOR-containing treatment to the respective active controls, DRV/r- and EFV-containing regimens, using the primary efficacy endpoint Week 48 HIV-1 RNA &lt;50 copies/mL. In PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, 83.8% and 79.9% had HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL at Week 48 in the DOR and DRV/r arms, respectively: the treatment difference was 3.9% (95.001% CI: -1.6%, 9.4%). In PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, 84.3% and 80.8% had HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL at Week 48 in the DOR/3TC/TDF and EFV/FTC/TDF arms, respectively: the treatment
L93: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L94: difference was 3.5% (95% CI: -2.0%, 9.0%). Each Phase 3 trial met the pre-specified noninferiority margin. The efficacy results were consistent across analysis populations, methods of missing data imputation, and thresholds of viral suppression.
L95: A goal of the DOR development program was to demonstrate improved safety over DRV/r- and EFV-containing regimens with pre-specified analyses of fasting lipid laboratory and clinical neuropsychiatric endpoints. Both PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD demonstrated statistically significantly changes from baseline in LDL-C and non-HDL-C at Week 48, favoring the DOR-containing treatment, with consistent results between primary and sensitivity analyses. PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD demonstrated statistically significantly lower proportions of subjects that experienced neuropsychiatric AEs of dizziness, sleep disorders and disturbances, and altered sensorium in the DOR-containing arm compared to the EFV-containing arm.
L96: It is noted that the two trials lack an INSTI-containing active control comparison, which is currently recommended in US HIV treatment guidelines as the initial regimen for most people with HIV. This limits comparability of the PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD DOR-containing treatment arm safety results to current standard of care, as it is unknown whether DOR-containing regimens would result in favorable lipid profiles or neuropsychiatric profiles versus INSTI-containing regimens.
L97: The safety data submitted with these NDAs demonstrate a favorable DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF safety profile: no major safety issues unique to DOR were identified in this review. The following adverse reactions occurred in at least five percent of DOR-treated participants in the Phase 3 clinical trials: nausea, fatigue, headache, diarrhea, abdominal pain, abnormal dreams, and dizziness. Grade 1 and 2 total bilirubin elevations occurred more frequently in DOR-containing arms. In general, these elevations were not sustained and not associated with treatment discontinuations or abnormalities in other liver laboratories. Nonclinical toxicology studies identified an imbalance in thyroid adenoma and carcinoma at the highest dose (7 times the DOR AUC exposure at the RHD) in a 2-year rat carcinogenicity study. No thyroid adenomas or carcinomas were reported in the Phase 2 or 3 DOR-containing trials and labeling is proposed to state the clinical significance of this preclinical finding is unclear and the finding was within the range observed in historical control studies. Safety considerations associated with DOR-containing treatment can be adequately described in the label: no risk management beyond standard pharmacovigilance is warranted based on this review.
L98: Co-administration of DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF and drugs that are strong inhibitors of CYP3A may result in increased plasma concentrations of DOR; however, no DOR dose adjustment is needed based on the available safety profiles. Co-administration of DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF with a strong CYP3A inducer is contraindicated because significant decreases in DOR plasma concentrations may occur, which may decrease its effectiveness. Prior to DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF initiation, approximately 4 weeks of a cessation period is needed following administration of a strong CYP3A inducer. If DOR is co-administered with rifabutin, it is recommended to increase DOR dosage to 100 mg twice daily. If DOR/3TC/TDF is co-

## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L100: administered with rifabutin, it is recommended to take one tablet of DOR/3TC/TDF FDC once daily, followed by DOR 100 mg approximately 12 hours after the dose of DOR/3TC/TDF. M9 concentrations, the predominant DOR metabolite which is primarily renally eliminated, are predicted to increase up to 4-fold when DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF is coadministered with rifabutin. No human PK data are available for M9; however, the totality of available data including human DOR exposure data, non-clinical DOR safety margin data, and negative prediction for M9 genetic toxicity support the DOR and rifabutin coadministration recommendations. A PMC is recommended to evaluate the magnitude of increased M9 concentrations when DOR is coadministered with rifabutin at the proposed BID regimen in patients, (b) (4).

L106: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L107: Dimension	Evidence and Uncertainties	Conclusions and Reasons
Benefit	The efficacy of DOR-containing treatment in the HIV treatment-naïve population was established in two randomized Phase 3 trials: PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD.
These trials enrolled 1494 subjects overall: 747 in DOR-containing arms and 747 in comparator arms.
In PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, 83.8% and 79.9% had HIV-1 RNA < 50 copies/mL at Week 48 in the DOR and DRV/r arms, respectively. The treatment difference was 3.9% (95.001% CI: -1.6%, 9.4%).
In PN021-DRIVE AHEAD, 84.3% and 80.8% had HIV-1 RNA < 50 copies/mL at Week 48 in the DOR/3TC/TDF and EFV/FTC/TDF arms, respectively. The treatment difference was 3.5% (95% CI: -2.0%, 9.0%).
The trials support noninferiority of DOR-containing treatment to the active controls. The result is consistent across analysis populations, methods of missing data imputation, and thresholds of viral suppression.
Both PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD demonstrated statistically significantly changes from baseline in LDL-C and non-HDL-C at Week 48, favoring the DOR-containing treatment, with consistent results between primary and sensitivity analyses.
PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD demonstrated statistically significantly lower proportions of subjects that experience AEs of dizziness, sleep disorders and disturbances, and altered sensorium in the DOR-containing arms compared to the active comparator arms.
PN007 provides additional supportive evidence of lower proportions of subjects with NPEs in the DOR arm compared to the EFV arm.
None of the submitted Phase 2 or Phase 3 trials included an INSTI-containing comparator arm.
The efficacy of DOR-containing treatment in the HIV-1 treatment-naïve population through Week 48 was established in two randomized Phase 3 trials. These trials support noninferiority of DOR-containing treatment to the respective active controls, DRV/r- and EFV-containing regimens.
DOR-containing regimens have a favorable lipid profile compared with DRV/r-containing and EFV-containing regimens. DOR/3TC/TDF has a favorable neuropsychiatric profile compared with EFV/FTC/TDF.
The lack of an INSTI-containing active control comparison, currently recommended in US HIV treatment guidelines as the initial regimen for most people with HIV, limits generalizability of the PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD DOR results.

L115: ## 2. Therapeutic Context
L116: ## 2.1. Analysis of Condition
L117: Per the WHO, approximately 37 million people were living with HIV, 1.8 million people were newly infected with HIV, and 1.0 million people died from HIV-related causes globally in 2016. As reported by the CDC, in the US 1.1 million people were living with HIV at the end of 2015, and 39,782 people were newly diagnosed with HIV in 2016. Without treatment, HIV infection leads to progressive destruction of the immune system, resulting in AIDS-defining illnesses and premature death in almost all cases.
L118: Currently, effective cART treatment of HIV-1 infection for treatment-naïve or treatment-experienced patients without history of virologic failure is generally comprised of at least three ARV medications, two of which belong in the NRTI class. The third agent is selected from the INSTI, PI, or NNRTI classes. HIV treatment is not finite but rather is needed lifelong. The goals of HIV treatment are to durably suppress HIV viral load, preserve and restore the immune system, reduce HIV-associated morbidity, and ultimately improve long-term survival. Effective viral load suppression can also provide the public health benefit of decreased HIV transmission.
L119: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L120: Treatment of HIV infection has dramatically improved since the mid-1990s, after the introduction of use of cART. Despite such progress, there is a continued need for development of new ARV drug products and new FDC products. A need for better tolerated regimens also exists. The introduction of FDC drug products has allowed for simpler ARV regimens, increasing the likelihood of adherence and thereby improving treatment outcomes. Availability of new regimens such as FDC products have also allowed patients to be able to switch if they are not tolerating their current regimens or have 'pill fatigue' due to multiple pill regimens. For example, according to a publication by the Antiretroviral Therapy Cohort Collaboration (2013), among the 21,000+ patients in a European and North American cohort who are on their first cART regimen, more than half modified or interrupted their first cART regimen either due to adverse events or toxicities of cART, desire for simplification of the regimen, or due to patient choice. These observations suggest that there is a need for continued development of new products and/or regimens.
L121: If approved, DOR would be a new NNRTI option with a favorable neuropsychiatric toxicity profile compared with EFV and a favorable lipid profile compared to DRV/r and EFV. DOR/3TC/TDF would be a new complete FDC treatment option containing a novel NNRTI plus two NRTIs.
L122: ## 2.2. Analysis of Current Treatment Options
L123: Excluding FDC products or different formulations, 28 individual ARV drugs and two drugs used as pharmacokinetic (PK) enhancers with ARVs are FDA approved and available for the treatment of HIV-1 infection. RTV and cobicistat are not counted as ARVs for treatment of HIV because they are used in therapy as PK enhancers. Standard of care involves the administration of multiple drugs targeting different events in the viral life cycle. Approved drugs belong to seven mechanistic classes: NRTIs, NNRTIs, PIs, INSTIs, CCR5 antagonists, fusion/entry inhibitors, and CD4-directed post-attachment HIV-1 inhibitors. Table 1 below summarizes the antiretroviral drugs that have been approved.

L136: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L137: Table 2 lists recommended initial antiretroviral options for treatment-naïve patients per the current Department of Health and Human Services Guidelines for Use of Antiviral Agents in Adults and Adolescents Living with HIV. EFV- and DRV/r-containing regimens, as stated in these current guidelines, are not recommended as initial regimens for most people with HIV:
L138: ## **EFV**
L139: - “Given the availability of regimens with fewer treatment-limiting adverse events and also with noninferior or superior efficacy, the Panel classifies EFV/TDF/FTC (**BI**) or EFV plus TAF/FTC (**BII**) as Recommended Initial Regimens in Certain Clinical Situations.
- EFV at a reduced dose has not been studied in the U.S. population, in pregnant women, or in patients with TB/HIV coinfection. The Panel **cannot recommend** the use of reduced-dose EFV.”
L140: ## **DRV/r**
L141: - “Darunavir (DRV)-based regimens have been moved to the category of Recommended Initial Regimens in Certain Clinical Situations based on trials showing improved outcomes with INSTI-based regimens when compared with ritonavir-boosted darunavir (DRV/r), in part because of greater tolerability of the former.
- An example of a situation in which a DRV-based regimen may still be preferred is when a high genetic barrier to resistance is particularly important, such as when there is substantial concern regarding a person's adherence or when antiretroviral therapy (ART) should be initiated before resistance test results are available.”
L142: *Reviewer Comment: At the time PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD were designed, DRV/r- and EFV-containing regimens were recommended initial regimens.*
L143: **Table 2: Recommended Initial Regimens for Most People with HIV per Current Department of Health and Human Services Guidelines for Use of Antiviral Agents in Adults and Adolescents Living with HIV**
L144: Recommended Initial Regimens for Most People with HIV
Recommended regimens are those with demonstrated durable virologic efficacy, favorable tolerability and toxicity profiles, and ease of use.
INSTI + 2 NRTIs:
DTG/ABC/3TCa (AI)—if HLA-B*5701 negative
DTG + tenofovirb/FTCa (AI for both TAF/FTC and TDF/FTC)
EVG/c/tenofovirb/FTC (AI for both TAF/FTC and TDF/FTC)
RALc + tenofovirb/FTCa (AI for TDF/FTC, III for TAF/FTC)

Recommended Initial Regimens in Certain Clinical Situations
These regimens are effective and tolerable, but have some disadvantages when compared with the regimens listed above, or have less supporting data from randomized clinical trials. However, in certain clinical situations, one of these regimens may be preferred (see Table 7 for examples).
Boosted PI + 2 NRTIs: (In general, boosted DRV is preferred over boosted ATV)
(DRV/c or DRV/r) + tenofovirb/FTCa (AI for DRV/r and III for DRV/c)
(ATV/c or ATV/r) + tenofovirb/FTCa (BI)
(DRV/c or DRV/r) + ABC/3TCa —if HLA-B*5701—negative (III)
(ATV/c or ATV/r) + ABC/3TCa —if HLA-B*5701—negative and HIV RNA <100,000 copies/mL (CI for ATV/r and III for ATV/c)

NNRTI + 2 NRTIs:
EFV + tenofovirb/FTCa (BI for EFV/TDF/FTC and III for EFV + TAF/FTC)
RPV/tenofovirb/FTCa (BI)—if HIV RNA <100,000 copies/mL and CD4 >200 cells/mm3

INSTI + 2 NRTIs:
RALc + ABC/3TCa (III)—if HLA-B*5701—negative and HIV RNA < 100,000 copies/mL

Regimens to Consider when ABC, TAF, and TDF Cannot be Used:d
DRV/r + RAL (BID) (CI)—if HIV RNA <100,000 copies/mL and CD4 >200 cells/mm3
LPV/r + 3TCa (BID)e (CI)
L145: **Table 2. (continued) Recommended Initial Regimens for Most People with HIV per Current Department of Health and Human Services Guidelines for Use of Antiviral Agents in Adults and Adolescents Living with HIV**
L146: a 3TC may be substituted for FTC, or vice versa, if a non-fixed-dose NRTI combination is desired.
L147: b TAF and TDF are two forms of tenofovir approved by the FDA. TAF has fewer bone and kidney toxicities than TDF, while TDF is associated with lower lipid levels. Safety, cost, and access are among the factors to consider when choosing between these drugs.
L148: c RAL can be given as 400 mg BID or 1200 mg (two 600-mg tablets) once daily.
L149: d Several other NRTI-limiting treatment strategies are under investigation. See the section titled Selected Strategies That Are Under Evaluation and Not Yet Recommended below for discussion regarding these regimens.
L150: e LPV/r plus 3TC is the only boosted PI plus 3TC regimen with published 48-week data in a randomized controlled trial in ART-naïve patients. Limitations of LPV/r plus 3TC include twice-daily dosing, high pill burden, and greater rates of gastrointestinal side effects than other PIs.
L151: **Note:** The following are available as coformulated drugs: ABC/3TC, ATV/c, DRV/c, DTG/ABC/3TC, EFV/TDF/FTC, EVG/c/TAF/FTC, EVG/c/TDF/FTC, LPV/r, RPV/TAF/FTC, RPV/TDF/FTC, TAF/FTC, and TDF/FTC.

L154: ## 3. Regulatory Background
L155: ## 3.1. U.S. Regulatory Actions and Marketing History
L156: DOR is a new molecular entity and not approved currently in the US. DOR/3TC/TDF is an FDC under 505(b) also not approved.
L157: ## 3.2. Summary of Presubmission/Submission Regulatory Activity
L158: **August 11, 2011:** IND 112796 for DOR was submitted in the US for the treatment of HIV-1 infection.
L159: **June 24, 2014:** An end of phase 2 meeting was held. The meeting purpose was to gain feedback of the proposed Phase 3 DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF FDC clinical development program. FDA agreed
L160: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L161: with the proposed clinical development plans consisting of 48-week safety and efficacy data from Phase 3 trials PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, supported by 96-week safety and efficacy data from Phase 2 trial PN007. The Sponsor and FDA came to an agreement in terms of two proposed special safety endpoints, lipid changes from baseline and neuropsychiatric events (NPE). For PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, LDL-C, as opposed to non-HDL-C alone, was agreed on as the primary comparative measure for lipid changes, with non-HDL-C measured either sequentially or in conjunction with LDL-C. Total cholesterol, HDL, and triglycerides were agreed on as acceptable supportive measures. For PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, it was agreed NPEs would be analyzed in a sequential manner in the following order: 1) dizziness, 2) sleep disorders and disturbances, 3) altered sensorium, 4) depression and suicide/self-injury, and 5) psychosis and psychotic disorders. The sequential analysis would stop at the first category that fails to meet a p-value of 0.05. All severities would be included, and the analysis would be done at Week 48.
L162: **March 23, 2015:** IND 124997 for the FDC DOR/3TC/TDF was submitted in the US for the treatment of HIV-1 infection.
L163: **July 29, 2015:** FDA provided written responses to the questions contained in the IND 124997 June 8, 2015 Type C meeting background package. The meeting purpose was to obtain FDA feedback regarding the [REDACTED] (b) (4) in the DOR/3TC/TDF FDC tablet. FDA requested additional information before any agreement could be reached.
L164: **January 17, 2017:** A CMC Type C meeting was scheduled; however, it was cancelled by Merck. Merck stated that the preliminary comments sent by the FDA were sufficiently clear and further discussion was not required at that time. Agreement was reached on the selection of regulatory starting materials in the synthesis of DOR drug substance for the planned NDA submissions.
L165: **May 17, 2017:** A CMC Type C meeting took place. The meeting purpose was to obtain feedback regarding Merck's proposals for the product manufacturing and storage, commercial expiration date of the drug product, and dissolution method for DOR and the FDC product in support for the planned NDA submissions. Briefly, FDA stated the Sponsor's proposed control strategy to [REDACTED] (b) (4) drug product manufacturing and storage, and tablet expiry appeared to be reasonable. However, no agreement was reached regarding the dissolution method. FDA requested a full dissolution method development report.
L166: **August 8, 2017:** A Type B Pre-NDA meeting was conducted. The purpose of the meeting was to discuss the acceptability of the proposed filing strategy for DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF. At this meeting, FDA agreed that the efficacy and safety data from the Phase 3 clinical trials (P018 and P021) along with the Phase 2b data (PN007) are adequate to support the NDA submission. FDA agreed to the proposed content and format as well as plans for integrated analysis of data from the clinical trials supporting safety and efficacy.
L167: INDs 112796 and 124997 have never been placed on clinical hold.
L168: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L169: **October 23, 2017:** NDA 210806 (DOR) and NDA 210807 (DOR/3TC/TDF) were submitted to the FDA.
L170: ## 4. Significant Issues from Other Review Disciplines Pertinent to Clinical Conclusions on Efficacy and Safety
L171: ## 4.1. Office of Scientific Investigations (OSI)
L172: Inspection sites were selected from PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD. A total of six sites, three from PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and three from PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, were selected using the BIMO site selection tool as a resource. Both domestic and foreign sites were selected because this approval would be the first of DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF, and because a substantial amount of the clinical trial experience with DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF has been at foreign sites. The clinical site inspection final reports were all classified as NAI: please refer to the OSI report in DARRTS for complete details (entered 4/4/2018).

L186: ## 5. Nonclinical Pharmacology/Toxicology
L187: ## 5.1. Executive Summary
L188: PIFELTRO is a once daily fixed-dose combination (FDC) tablet containing doravirine (DELSTRIGO; DOR), lamivudine (3TC) and tenofovir disoproxil fumarate (TDF), and is intended for the treatment of HIV-1 infection in adults. DOR (also known as MK-1439) is a potent non-nucleoside reverse transcriptase inhibitor (NNRTI) that is being developed as a single entity to be part of an anti-retroviral therapy (ART), or as part of a fixed dose combination with 3TC and TDF. DOR is the only component of the FDC which has not been previously approved for use by the FDA. 3TC and TDF have been approved for marketing in the U.S. as standalone agents (Epivir®; NDA 020564 and Viread®; NDA 021356) and in multiple FDC products.
L189: The nonclinical safety profile of DOR was evaluated in: safety pharmacology studies in rats, mice and dogs; repeat-dose toxicology studies in mice, rats and dogs for up to 12, 26, and 39 weeks, respectively; 12-week toxicology studies in rats to qualify impurities; phototoxicity studies in mouse fibroblasts and pigmented rats; juvenile, fertility and pre- and post-natal developmental studies in rats; embryo-fetal developmental studies in rats and rabbits; genetic toxicology studies (Ames, *in vitro* chromosomal aberration and *in vivo* rat micronucleus assays); and a 2-year carcinogenicity study in rats, as well as a 6-month study in transgenic mice. In addition, numerous *in vitro* and *in vivo* nonclinical pharmacokinetic studies evaluating the absorption, distribution, metabolism and excretion of DOR have been conducted in mice, rats, rabbits and dogs. Refer to the Pharmacology/Toxicology reviews for NDA-020564 and NDA-021356 for a detailed summary of 3TC and TDF nonclinical data, respectively.
L190: ## Pharmacokinetics
L191: The steady state volume of distribution (V_{ss}) following intravenous infusion of DOR was 2.67 and 0.9 L/kg in rabbits and dogs with half-life (t_{1/2}) values of 2.7, 6.4, 9, and 21.7 hr in mice, rats, rabbits, and dogs, respectively. DOR had low to moderate oral bioavailability (39%-47% in mice, rats, rabbits and dogs) with rapid absorption, reaching maximal plasma concentrations (C_{max}) within 3.5 hours post-dose. DOR had moderate binding to plasma protein (0.24) in all species examined and had wide tissue distribution in the rat, except the brain. The highest concentrations of radioactivity were observed in the alimentary canal, liver, and kidney, due to transit of the dose through the gastrointestinal tract and/or involvement in elimination. DOR did not display affinity for melanin.
L192: The equilibrium blood-to-plasma concentration ratio was independent of the DOR concentration and was approximately 1 across species, except in mice, for which the ratio varied from 0.65 to 0.71. As such, there was no preferential distribution of DOR into blood cells.
L193: DOR was eliminated primarily by oxidative metabolism in nonclinical species. The major metabolic pathway in nonclinical species and humans involved formation of M9, an oxidative
L194: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L195: metabolite resulting from CYP-mediated oxidation. In all species except rats, in which M9 underwent significant glucuronidation, the majority of M9 was eliminated without further modification, predominantly in urine. At pharmacologically relevant DOR exposures, M9 was the major metabolite circulating in mice and humans but not rats, rabbits, or dogs, where M9 was present in trace amounts or not detected.
L196: In a rat pre/post-natal study, DOR was detected in the plasma of neonates on lactation day 14, indicating transfer through milk; lactational transfer on LD 14 was up to 147%.
L197: ## Safety Pharmacology
L198: No significant effects on neurologic (functional observational battery; rats and mice) or respiratory parameters (plethysmography; dogs) were observed following single oral doses of DOR up to 450 mg/kg in rats and mice, and up to 10 mg/kg in dogs. In addition, no significant cardiovascular effects on hemodynamic or electrocardiographic parameters were noted for up to 24 hours post-dose in telemetry-monitored or vagotomized dogs given single oral doses of DOR up to 10 mg/kg. DOR did not significantly inhibit hERG current *in vitro* and the IC50 value was 88 µM (approximately 150 times the unbound DOR levels at Cmax measured at the RHD). No cardiovascular events were observed in clinical trials with DOR.
L199: ## Repeat-Dose Toxicology Studies
L200: Repeat-dose toxicology studies were conducted in mice, rats and dogs with doses up to 450, 450 and 1000 mg/kg/day for up to 3, 6 and 9 months, respectively. The highest dose in rodents was the maximum feasible dose based in maximum feasible concentration and dose volume (b) (4). The limit dose was reached in dogs (1000 mg/kg/day).
L201: In mice, non-adverse effects included mild decreases in weight gain. The NOAEL was the highest dose tested, 450 mg/kg/day (AUC0-24 hr 196 µM·h). In the 6-month rat study, urinary crystals were observed in high dose (450 mg/kg/day) males and females. These crystals were present in the absence of histopathological effects in the urinary bladder or kidney or any adverse effects on urine or serum chemistry parameters. They were later determined to be formed *ex vivo* and a result of supersaturation of DOR in the urine. Additional non-adverse test-article related findings included post-dose salivation (at all dose levels), increased liver weight and various minimal changes in hematologic parameters. The NOAEL was 450 mg/kg/day (AUC0-24 hr 279 µM·hr; male and female combined). In the 9-month dog study, the only findings were mild gastrointestinal effects, post-dose salivation and lacrimation and mild clinical chemistry changes. These effects were not considered adverse due to their intermittent nature and low magnitude. The NOAEL was the highest dose of 1000 mg/kg/day (AUC0-24 hr 673 µM·hr; male and female combined). DOR exposures at the NOAELs in mice, rats and dogs were 5.5, 7.5 and 18 times, respectively, the exposure in humans at the RHD.
L202: ## Genetic Toxicology and Carcinogenicity
L203: DOR was not mutagenic or clastogenic as tested in the Ames assay up to 5000 µg/plate, the *in vitro* chromosomal aberration assay in Chinese hamster ovary cells up to 300 µM, and the *in vivo* rat micronucleus assay up to day 14 at 450 mg/kg/day. A 6-month carcinogenicity
L204: transgenic rasH2 mouse study had no findings. A statistically significant increase in combined female thyroid adenoma and carcinoma was noted at the highest dose of 450 mg/kg/day (7 times the DOR AUC exposure at the RHD) in a 2-year rat carcinogenicity study. The clinical significance of this finding is unclear and the finding was within the range observed in historical control studies.
L205: ## Reproductive Toxicology
L206: DOR exposure was not associated with effects on fertility. Daily oral doses of DOR to rats for 15 days prior to cohabitation, during cohabitation, and through Gestation Day (GD) 7 for the females and until the day prior to scheduled sacrifice for the males (approximately 6 weeks total) had no effects on male or female reproductive performance, on the estrous cycle or sperm, or on embryo/fetal viability. The NOAEL for fertility and early embryonic development in rats was 450 mg/kg/day (AUC_{0-t} 279 \muM\cdothr; AUC from 6-month rat study), the highest dose tested in the study. When compared to human DOR exposure following administration at the RHD, the margin of exposure for DOR is 7.5X.
L207: Developmental toxicity studies were conducted in rats and rabbits. There were no adverse effects on maternal health or embryo-fetal development in rats, and the NOEL/NOAEL was 450 and 300 mg/kg/day, for rats and rabbits, respectively. In rabbits, administration of oral DOR at 300 mg/kg/day to pregnant females during organogenesis resulted in maternal toxicity (body weight loss) and concomitant fetal toxicity (skull malformations). Due to maternal toxicity at this dose, the malformations were not considered test article-related. This finding occurred at exposures, based on AUC (315 \muM\cdothr), approximately 8.5 times the projected clinical exposure of DOR following administration at the RHD.
L208: DOR maternal exposure was not associated with any adverse effects on pre- and postnatal development. DOR at doses up to 450 mg/kg/day in pregnant rats had no maternal or fetal effects on behavior/development. The NOAEL was approximately 9 times higher than clinical exposure following DOR administration at the RHD. In addition, DOR had no clear adverse effects on rats dosed daily from post-natal day (PND) 14 to PND 55, in a juvenile toxicity study. The only significant finding was a dose-related increase in the incidence of MK-1439-treated rats with urine crystals (brown, needle-like, appearing in clusters) observed ex vivo following overnight urine collection in all test article-treated groups in post-natal week (PNW) 8 with increasing frequency at 300 mg/kg/day (highest dose and NOAEL); this finding was not correlated with any other clinical pathology observation or any anatomic pathology finding. The urine crystals were no longer present after a 4-week treatment-free period (PNW 13). The urine crystals were later determined to be formed ex vivo. The NOAEL of 300 mg/kg/day in the study was 9 times the clinical exposure of DOR following administration at the RHD.
L209: ## Other Toxicology
L210: DOR was nonphototoxic, and nonirritating to skin or eye. DOR also showed no potential for sensitization.
L211: ## RECOMMENDATION
L212: The sponsor has provided sufficient nonclinical safety information on Doravirine to support approval for marketing in the U.S as a component in the fixed-dosed combination 3TC/TDF/DOR (DELSTRIGO™). With respect to the FDC of 3TC/TDF/DOR, no specific overlapping toxicity of potential significant clinical concern was identified in animals and combination studies were not conducted.

L220: ## 5.3. Pharmacology
L221: ## Primary pharmacology
L222: ## DOR
L223: DOR, a potent NNRTI of human immunodeficiency virus type 1 (HIV-1), has shown potent activity against wild-type (WT) virus and prevalent K103N and Y181C NNRTI mutants with EC50 values of 12 nM, 21 nM, and 31 nM respectively, in the presence of 100% serum. Additional complete details of the pharmacodynamics of DOR/3TC/TDF can be found in the clinical virology review.
L224: ## Secondary Pharmacology
L225: ## DOR
L226: ## In Vitro Assessment of DOR in the [REDACTED] (b) (4) – Pharmacology Screen/PD001
L227: DOR was evaluated against a large panel of standard enzyme and other receptor assays to
L228: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L229: assess its specificity for off-target activities. In a (b) (4) screen with more than 110 enzymes and receptors. DOR only showed moderate affinity to the 5-hydroxytryptamine (serotonin) receptor 2B (5-HT2B) receptor in a ligand binding assay with an IC50 of 2.5 µM, while in a subsequent cell based functional 5-HT2B assay, no agonist or antagonist activity was observed by monitoring the accumulation of inositol-1-phosphate. Therefore, binding of DOR to 5-HT2B does not impact 5-HT2B receptor function.
L230: ## DOR/3TC/TDF
L231: Due to the low potential for off-target activity and cytotoxicity by each component, no additional secondary pharmacology studies were deemed necessary.
L232: ## Safety Pharmacology
L233: **Effect on hERG Current/TT #10-4716:** A minor effect of MK-1439 on membrane K+ current was observed (IC50= 88 µM) in hERG-transfected CHO cells in this GLP study. This effect is not considered to be of clinical concern.
L234: **Oral Functional Observational Battery Study in Rats/TT #10-6029:** Wistar Han rats were assigned to 4 groups of 10 females and 10 males each that received 0, 5, 75, or 450 mg/kg/day (b) (4) of MK-1439 administered as a
L235: Assessment of toxicity was based on mortality, physical observations, body weights, food consumption, ophthalmic examinations, and clinical and anatomic pathology evaluations. Plasma MK-1439-concentrations in treated and control groups were determined. In addition, a functional observational battery assay was conducted to evaluate potential nervous system effects in male rats after a single oral dose on Study Day 1. The assessment, consisting of home cage, hand-held, and open-field observations, stimulus activity responses, grip-strength, foot splay, latency on the hot plate, and body temperature, was conducted approximately 7 hours (± 1 hour) post-dose (expected Tmax). There were no test article-related FOB findings at all doses tested. The NOEL for nervous system function was ≥450 mg/kg (Cmax of 15.2 µM, approximately 6.5-fold DOR levels at the human Cmax exposure). No significant neurobehavioral effects were observed for up to 24 hours post-dose in an exploratory FOB study in female mice given single oral doses of up to 750 mg/kg (study #TT-10-5262).
L236: **Oral Cardiovascular and Respiratory Telemetry Study in Dogs/TT #10-5606:** Four Beagle dogs (2 males, 2 females) received single doses orally by gavage of the vehicle alone or 0.5, 2, or 10 mg/kg of MK-1439 using an ascending dose administration regimen, beginning with the vehicle (time-matched control) dose. There were at least 3 days between the doses of MK-1439. Arterial blood pressure parameters (systolic [SBP], diastolic [DBP], and mean [MBP] blood pressure), heart rate (HRe), electrocardiograph parameters (PR, QRS, QT and RR intervals), respiratory parameters (rate and depth of respiration) and body temperature were monitored
L237: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L238: for 24 hrs post dose via radiotelemetry. The QT interval was corrected for HR variation (QTci interval) using an individual animal correction method (Miyazaki's correction). The NOEL was &gt;10 mg/kg (C_{max} ~12 (F) and 8 (M) \muM; AUC_{0-24 hr}=220 (F) and 161 (M) \muM\cdothr at 10 mg/kg-taken from study #TT-10-6039; three month toxicity study in dogs). There were no test-article related effects on hemodynamic or electrocardiographic parameters or on body temperature. In addition, there were no test-article related effects on respiratory parameters [respiratory rate (bpm) or depth of respiration (mmHg)].
L239: **Cardiovascular Function in Anesthetized (Vagotomized) Dogs/TT #10-5006:** MK-1439 was administered intravenously during 3 sequential 30-minute periods at 1, 2, and 7 mg/kg in 100% DMSO to determine its effects on cardiovascular function in three anesthetized and vagotomized dogs. Effects were compared to those with 100% DMSO alone determined previously in a separate set of 3 dogs. Heart rate, mean arterial pressure, and electrocardiographic parameters (PR, QRS, and QT/QTc intervals) were monitored predose and throughout the rising dose infusion of the test article. There were no test article-related effects on MAP, HR, QRS, PR or QT/QTc intervals at any dose tested. Maximum average plasma concentrations of MK-1439 measured during the 30-minute infusions of 1, 2, and 7 mg/kg were 3, 6, and 14 \muM, respectively.

L252: ## Distribution Study Comments
L253: - Radioactivity was distributed to most tissues except central nervous system, eye lens and bone.
- Highest concentrations were found in the alimentary canal, bile and urine.
- Blood concentrations were maximal two hours post-dose.
- Radioactivity remained up to 168 hours post-dose.

L262: ## **Excretion and Metabolism of [14C]MK-1439 in Male CD-1 Mouse and Female Dutch Belted Rabbit Following Oral Administration (PK013)**
L263: - The excretion and metabolism of [14C]MK-1439 was characterized in male CD-1 mice and female Dutch belted Rabbits (5 mg/kg) following oral administration of a single 5 mg/kg dose.
L264: - The major parent-related species circulating in mouse plasma were MK-1439 and M9, where levels of M9 were comparable to levels of MK-1439 (approximately 1:1 ratio) at the two time points with quantifiable levels of radioactivity (1 and 6 hr).
- The oxidative metabolite M9 was the major metabolite in rabbit urine, accounting for 19.5% of the dose.
L265: ## M9 Metabolite Comments
L266: - M9 was present in rat and dog plasma (at NOAEL doses) at levels that were respectively 52% and 51% relative to levels observed in plasma from healthy subjects who received multiple once daily 240 mg doses of DOR.
- The unbound fraction of M9 in rat and dog plasma was respectively 2.2- and 2.8-fold higher than in human plasma (Table 9).
- As such, unbound concentrations in the safety species, were approximately similar to the unbound concentrations in human plasma after administration of multiple once-daily administration of 240 mg DOR.
- Exposures of M9 in plasma from patients administered the clinical dose (RHD; 100 mg once a day) were adequately covered in the chronic safety studies.
L267: **Table 9: Fraction Unbound M9 Metabolite in Plasma from Different Species**
L268: Compound	Species	Fraction Unbounda
0.1 \muM	1.0 \muM
M9	Rat	0.18 \pm 0.02	0.20 \pm 0.01
Dog	0.24 \pm 0.01	0.25 \pm 0.01
Human	0.08 \pm 0.003	0.09 \pm 0.004
L269: a Values represent mean \pm SD of 3-6 replicates.
L270: - M9 was adequately assessed in the Ames assay (negative) and predicted to be negative in (Q)SAR analysis conducted by the FDA CDER Chemical Informatics Program.
L271: ## Co-Administration of DOR and Rifabutin
L272: - M9 is the primary metabolite resulting from DOR via CYP3A metabolism in humans
- Co-administration of rifabutin (a CYP3A inducer) with DOR, may result in increased M9 exposure.
- An exposure margin of 4.5X was calculated by the Clinical Pharmacology reviewer. An exposure margin of 10X was calculated by the Sponsor.
- Both margins were based on estimated exposures of M9 in a three-month mouse study (Study TT #12-6013) and estimated human exposures of M9 (variable), after rifabutin co-administration.
- Mouse levels of M9 in the three-month study were estimated from a mass balance study in CD-1 mice, in which plasma exposures of M9 and MK-1439 were equivalent at 1- and 6-hour time-points after a single oral administration of 5 mg/kg MK-1439 (Study PK013).

L284: ## Lactation
L285: ## Oral Placental and Lactational Transfer Study in Rats/TT #15-7130
L286: - MK-1439 was observed in maternal and fetal plasma at 2 and 24 hours post-dose at 5 and 450 mg/kg/day on GD 20, and in maternal plasma and milk at 2 hours post-dose on LD 14.
- The placental transfer on GD 20 was similar at both doses and ranged between 48% and 52%. Lactational transfer on LD 14 was approximately 147% (low dose) and 132% (high dose) at 2 hours post-dose.

L344: ## 5.5.1. General Toxicology
L345: ## Nine-Month Oral Toxicity Study in Dogs / Study No. TT #11-6018
L346: ## Key Study Findings
L347: - The only clear test-article related findings were feces discoloration (in some low- and mid-dose and all high-dose animals due to the presence of unabsorbed MK-1439), post-dose salivation (in a few animals at all dose levels) and lacrimation (in some mid- and high-dose animals).
- No adverse toxicological findings were observed. The NOAEL was 1000 mg/kg (AUC_{0-24 \text{ hr}}=673 \mu\text{M}\cdot\text{hr}; M and F combined at 13 weeks)
L348: Conducting laboratory and location: Merck Research Laboratories, France

L383: ## **Observations and Results: changes from control**
L384: Parameters	Major findings
Mortality	MD: One male was found dead on Study Day 85 without relevant clinical signs observed prior to death. The main histopathology findings were observed in kidneys (pelvis dilatation, papilla necrosis, and mineralization), urinary bladder (dilatation, inflammation, and mineralization), prostate and seminal vesicle (inflammation). Mineralization also observed in heart, aorta, and stomach. Changes correlated with gross observations (kidney discoloration and pelvic dilatation, urinary bladder dilatation and discoloration, prostate discoloration) and supported urinary obstruction as a cause of death (considered to be unrelated to test article). Mineralization was also consistent with uremia secondary to urinary obstruction.
C: One female found dead on Study Day 165, following blood sampling for hematological and serum biochemical examinations, considered to be related to anesthesia accident.
Clinical Signs	HD: Increased sporadic post-dose salivation in all animals starting on study week 3.
Body Weights	Unremarkable
Ophthalmoscopy	Unremarkable
Feed Consumption	Unremarkable
Hematology	MD and HD: + 10% PT and aPTT at 26 weeks; non-adverse
HD: 7% increase in mean platelet volume at 12 weeks (M); non-adverse
HD: 17-33% increase in WBCs, lymphs and neutrophils at 12 and 24 weeks (M)
HD: 4-6% decrease in RBCs/Hg/Hct at 12 and 24 weeks (M). Note: Although statistically significant, change is minimal, within the historical control range and not adverse.
HD: 15% decrease in reticulocytes at 24 weeks (F); non-adverse
Clinical Chemistry	Unremarkable
Urinalysis	HD: Decrease in mean urine volume (-56%) assoc. w/ increased specific gravity and low urine pH in 3 animals (pH=5.5) at week 12 only (F). Note: Animals w/ low pH did not have crystals present. The relationship of this finding to the test-article is uncertain.
HD: One F urine described as “grossly bloody” at 12 weeks. Animal also had significant amount of protein in the 1 ml of urine collected at 24 weeks (in the absence of occult blood).
HD: Microscopic examination of urinary sediments revealed the presence of yellow-brown needle-like crystals in urchin-shaped or bundle-shaped clusters in 4F and 1M at week 12 and in 4 F and 5 M at week 24.Urinary crystals were observed in the absence of histopathological effects on the urinary bladder or kidney and were considered non-adverse.
Based on NMR and LC-MS analyses of urine sediment, the

L388: ## General toxicology; additional studies
L389: ## Three-Month Oral Toxicity Study in Rats /TT #10-6040
L390: Ten males and female Crl:WI(Han) rats per group were dosed with vehicle (b) (4) or 3, 30 or 450 mg/kg/day MK 1439 (b) (4)
L391: Assessment of toxicity was based on mortality, clinical observations, body weights, food consumption, ophthalmic examinations, and clinical and anatomic pathology evaluations. Plasma DOR concentrations were determined in all groups. Test article-related antemortem changes at \ge 30 mg/kg/day were limited to post-dose salivation in both sexes at 450 mg/kg/day, and the presence of urinary crystals in the urine of the majority of females at 450 mg/kg/day, and in a single female at 30 mg/kg/day. The urinary crystals were shown to be formed ex vivo in the exploratory ten-day oral-dose urine examination study in female rats (TT #14-1095; reviewed in additional studies). The NOEL for this study was 450 mg/kg/day (AUC_{0-24 \text{ hr}}: 253 \mu\text{M}\cdot\text{hr}; approximately 6.5-fold above the DOR exposure at the RHD).
L392: ## Three-Month Oral Toxicity Study in Dogs /TT #10-6039
L393: Three female and male Beagle dogs per group received the vehicle, or 1, 10 or 1000 mg/kg/day MK-1439 in 10% polysorbate 80 in deionized water. Assessment of toxicity was based on mortality, clinical observations, body weights, food consumption, ophthalmic and electrocardiographic examinations, and clinical and anatomic pathology evaluations. Plasma DOR concentrations were determined in all samples. Discoloration of the feces (likely from the non-absorbed MK-1439) was observed the high dose in both sexes. The NOEL for this study was 1000 mg/kg/day (AUC_{0-24 \text{ hr}}: 683 \mu\text{M}\cdot\text{hr}, approximately 18-fold above the DOR exposure at the RHD).
L394: ## MK-1439 Three-Month Oral Range-Finding and Toxicokinetic Study in CD1 Mice/TT #12-6013
L395: MK-1439 was administered once daily, by oral gavage, to male and female CD1 mice daily for approximately 3 months at up to 450 mg/kg/day. Assessment of toxicity was based on mortality, clinical observations, body weights, and clinical and anatomic pathology evaluations.
L396: Plasma MK-1439 concentrations were determined in MK-1439-treated groups from the toxicokinetic arm.
L397: Antemortem test article-related findings were limited to mild decreases in body weight gain in both sexes at 450 mg/kg/day (compared to controls, -16% in females in Study Week 13 and -22% to -37% in males from Study Week 8 onward). This was not considered adverse since the changes were relatively small. AUC increased less than dose proportionally, indicating saturation. Although M9 levels were not directly measured in this study, DOR and M9 levels were anticipated to be similar in CD-1 mice, based on a mass balance study (1:1 ratio). The NOAEL was 450 mg/kg/day (AUC_{0-24 hr} = 196 \mu M.hr/mL), approximately 5.5 times the exposure at the RHD.

L422: ## **5.5.3. Carcinogenicity**
L423: ## **Six-Month Oral Carcinogenicity Study in rasH2 Transgenic Mice /TT #13-6005**
L424: Jic:CB6F1-TgrasH2@Jcl mice were assigned to five groups of 25 females and 25 males each that received 10, 60, and 300 mg/kg/day of DOR or control article only (two control groups of 25 mice/sex/group). The amount of HPMCAS administered to the control groups (1200 mg/kg/day) was equivalent to the amount of polymer present in the high-dose group formulation. In addition, a positive control group of 10 females and 10 males was given urethane in 0.9% sodium chloride at 1000 mg/kg, by the intraperitoneal route, once a day, on Study Days 1, 3, and 5. Assessment of toxicity or carcinogenicity was based on mortality, clinical observations (including palpation for presence of masses except in the positive control group), body weights, and anatomic pathology evaluations. DOR concentrations in plasma samples from DOR-treated and control animals were determined.
L425: There were no test article-related unscheduled deaths and no test article-related findings in clinical observations or body weights. There was no test article-related increase in mortality and no evidence of a carcinogenic potential in any sex up to 300 mg/kg/day. The incidence of adenomas of the Harderian gland was 2/25 in males of the 300- mg/kg/day group. None were present in males of the low- and mid-dose or the concurrent control groups.
L426: The FDA Statistical Reviewer's tumor analysis showed a statistically significant increasing trend for adenoma in the Harderian glands in male mice (p = 0.0343). The pairwise comparisons did not show statistically significant increased tumor incidence in any treated group compared to the combined control. Per the final Applicant report, similar or higher incidences of this tumor type have been found in 5/34 vehicle-treated male rasH2 historical control groups. As such, there were no test article related neoplastic or non-neoplastic findings in this study at up to 300 mg/kg/day (AUC_{0-24 \text{ hr}}: 228 \mu\text{M}\cdot\text{hr}; approximately 6-fold above the DOR exposure at
L427: the RHD) after 6 months of daily oral administration of DOR.
L428: ## Two-Year Oral Carcinogenicity Study in Rats with a Six-Month Toxicokinetic Evaluation/ TT-136029
L429: The carcinogenicity potential of MK-1439 was tested in an oral 2-year rat carcinogenicity study. Wistar Han rats were administered 3, 30, or 450 mg/kg/day of MK-1439 provided as a
L430: oral gavage for 104 weeks. Two identical control groups received suspended in vehicle equivalent to that in the formulation administered to the 450-mg/kg/day MK-1439 dose group.
L431: There were no variations of tumor incidences considered related to administration of MK-1439. A statistically significant increase of Leydig cell tumors was observed in high dose males and an increased incidence of combined parafollicular adenoma and parafollicular carcinoma in the thyroid was observed in high dose females, by the FDA statistical reviewer, Hepei Chen, M.D., M.S. However, these numbers were within the range or close to those observed in historical control animals (as outlined below.
L432: According to historical control data for 104 week studies in Wistar Han male rats ( ), the range of interstitial cell adenoma in male testes was between 0.89 and 6.67%. Seven percent of males (3/43) at the high dose were found with Leydig cell tumors in the current carcinogenicity study, which is just slightly above the historical control maximum (6.67%).
L433: In the current carcinogenicity study, the rate of parafollicular cell adenoma in the high dose females was 13.5%, which was within the historical control range of 1.22% to 22% ( ; supplier). Likewise, the rate of parafollicular cell carcinoma was 2%, which was also within the historical control range of 1.33% and 8.93% ( supplier). The rate of parafollicular cell adenoma (13.5%) and carcinoma (2%) was also within the range of historical control data submitted by the Sponsor from studies conducted in their own facilities; i.e. 0-16% for adenomas and 0-2% for carcinomas. The thyroid findings were observed at 450 mg/kg/day (AUC_{0-24 \text{ hr}}: 279 \mu\text{M}\cdot\text{hr}; approximately 7.5-fold above the DOR exposure at the RHD) after 2 years of daily oral administration of DOR.
L434: The Executive Carcinogenicity Assessment Committee concurred that the combined incidence of drug related thyroid parafollicular cell adenoma and carcinoma in females was increased at 450 mg/kg/day. As such, the label reflects this decision and the historical control observation.

L503: ## **MK-1439 Oral Toxicity and Toxicokinetic Study in Juvenile Rats/TT #15-7140**
L504: Male and female Wistar Han rats were dosed with 10, 45 or 300 mg/kg/day MK-1439 from post-natal day (PND) 14 to PND 55. An interim necropsy was conducted on PND 56-58 and a final necropsy was conducted after a 4-week recovery period.
L505: The only significant finding was a dose-related increase in the incidence of MK-1439-treated rats with urine crystals (brown, needle-like, appearing in clusters) observed ex vivo following overnight urine collection in all test article-treated groups in post-natal week (PNW) 8 with increasing frequency at 300 mg/kg/day; this finding was not correlated with any other clinical
L506: pathology observation or any anatomic pathology finding. The urine crystals were no longer present after a 4-week treatment-free period (PNW 13) and in another study were found to be formed *ex vivo*. The NOAEL in this study was 300 mg/kg/day, with an AUC0-24 value of 333 \muM\cdoth (males and females combined).
L507: ## 5.5.5. Other Toxicology Studies
L508: ## Exploratory Ten-Day Oral-Dose Urine Examination Study in Female Rats/Study # TT 14-1095
L509: The purpose of this study was to investigate urine crystal formation *in vivo* and *ex vivo* using various urine collection methods in female rats orally dosed with MK-1439 daily for 10 days. Sixteen female Crl:WI(Han) rats received 450 mg/kg/day of MK-1439 (base compound)
L510: formulated as a
L511: (b) (4)
L512: (b) (4)
L513: and
L514: at a dose volume of 10 mL/kg. Control animals were not included. Assessment of toxicity was based on mortality, clinical observations, body weights, and gross examinations of urinary bladder and kidney. Overnight urine collections via metabolism caging (*ex vivo* collection) were performed on all rats on Study Days 3 and 9. Urine collections via cystocentesis (*in vivo* collection) were performed on all rats on Study Days 4 and 10. Urine chemistry and microscopic examinations were performed to evaluate crystal formation. Drug concentrations in plasma, urine supernatant, and urine sediment were measured. Urine crystal precipitation was only seen following *ex vivo* overnight metabolism cage collection (9/16 urine samples on day 3 and 16/16 urine samples on day 9). Crystals were not present after *in vivo* urine collection (cystocentesis), indicating crystal formation due to changes in external environment (temperature, volume decrease, protein precipitation etc.). Also, the concentration of MK-1439 in urine of overnight cystocentesis collection were 6-10 times fold higher than the measured solubility in FaSSIF or *in vitro* urine, indicating facilitation of solubility by urine proteins.
L515: ## Phototoxicity, Irritation and Sensitization Studies
L516: In a local lymph node assay conducted in mice, DOR at 5-25% administered by topical application to the dorsum of each mouse ear for three consecutive days, was not considered a dermal sensitizer since there was no test-article related increase in the stimulation index compared to the vehicle control group (Study TT #13-7859). DOR was a non-irritant in a bovine corneal opacity and permeability test in which five pig corneas were dosed with a 20% suspension of DOR (Study TT #13-7857). DOR was also non-irritating to skin in the MatTek EpiDerm MTT Viability Assay in which tissue samples were treated with 100 mg DOR for up to 24 hours (Study TT #13-7858). Lastly, there were no skin reactions or ocular observations (confirmed by histopathology of the eye tissues) indicative of phototoxicity in a three-day oral gavage phototoxicity study in male pigmented rats dosed with up to 450 mg/kg DOR and exposed to UV radiation thereafter (Study TT #11-9011).

L527: **Three-Month Oral Toxicity Study in Rats with a Micronucleus Assay/TT #16-6010:** The purpose of this study was to determine the potential toxicity profile of a new DOR batch lot and to determine the potential toxicity profile and micronucleus induction potential of Tenofovir Disoproxil Fumarate Degradate G (also known as (b) (4)) when each test article formulation is individually administered orally once daily to rats for approximately 3 months. Based on the absence of findings, the NOELs were \ge 30 mg/kg/day and \ge 0.3 mg/kg/day, for DOR and Tenofovir Disoproxil Fumarate Degradate G, respectively.

L533: ## 6. Clinical Pharmacology
L534: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L535: The Office of Clinical Pharmacology has reviewed the clinical pharmacology information contained in NDA 210806 and NDA 210807. These NDAs are considered approvable from a clinical pharmacology perspective. The key review issues with specific recommendations and comments are summarized below:
L536: Review Issues	Recommendations and Comments
Pivotal or Supportive evidence of effectiveness	The primary evidence of effectiveness is provided by two Phase 3 trials: DRIVE-FORWARD (P018) and DRIVE-AHEAD (P021). The supportive evidence for efficacy derives from a Phase 2 dose-range trial (P007).
General dosing instructions	The proposed dosage regimen of DOR 100 mg and DOR/3TC/TDF FDC tablet 100 mg/300 mg/300 mg taken orally once daily with food or without food is supported by the efficacy, safety, and PK data from the clinical trials submitted in this NDA. The recommended oral dose regimen is acceptable from a clinical pharmacology perspective.
Dosing in patient subgroups (intrinsic and extrinsic factors)	
No dose adjustments are recommended for DOR based on the following intrinsic factors:
patients with mild or moderate hepatic impairment
patients with mild, moderate, or severe renal impairment (for DOR)
(b) (4)
sex
(b) (4)
race/ethnicity
DOR/3TC/TDF is not recommended in patients with estimated creatinine clearance less than 50 mL/min because of the restrictions for 3TC and TDF.

Based on a bioavailability study and supportive dissolution data, the two formulations are not substantially different.
DOR/3TC/TDF FDC to-be-marketed formulation was used in the Phase 3 trial, P021.

L550: ## 6.2.2. General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
L551: ## General Dosing
L552: The Applicant's proposed dosage regimen of DOR 100 mg and DOR/3TC/TDF FDC tablet at 100 mg/300 mg/300 mg taken orally once daily with food or without food is supported by the efficacy, safety, and PK data from the clinical trials submitted in this NDA, and the recommended oral dose regimen is acceptable.
L553: ## Therapeutic Individualization
L554: ## Intrinsic factors
L555: ## - *Hepatic impairment*
L556: The review team concurs with the Applicant's recommendation that no dosage adjustment is required for DOR for patients with mild or moderate hepatic impairment. No dosage adjustment is required for patients with any degree of hepatic impairment for 3TC and TDF. Therefore, the recommendation of no dosage adjustment for patients with mild or moderate hepatic impairment is also applied to DOR/3TC/TDF FDC. DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF has not been studied in subjects with severe hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh score C).
L557: ## - *Renal impairment*
L558: The review team concurs with the Applicant's recommendation that no dosage adjustment of DOR is required in patients with mild, moderate, or severe renal impairment. DOR has not been adequately studied in patients with end-stage renal disease and has not been studied in dialysis patients.
L559: The review team also concurs with the Applicant's recommendation that DOR/3TC/TDF is not recommended in patients with estimated creatinine clearance less than 50 mL/min, because DOR/3TC/TDF is a fixed-dose combination tablet and the dosage of 3TC and TDF, both components of DOR/3TC/TDF cannot be altered.
L560: ## - *Other intrinsic factors:*
L561: The review team concurs with the Applicant's recommendation that no dosage adjustment of DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF is required based on [REDACTED] (b) (4) sex, [REDACTED] (b) (4) or race/ethnicity. Safety and efficacy of DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF have not been established in pediatric patients less than 18 years of age.
L562: ## Extrinsic factors
L563: The review team reviewed the relevant drug interaction studies and the proposed management strategy for clinically important DDIs is generally acceptable. The agreed upon recommendations for clinical management of specific drugs are summarized below:
L564: - Coadministration of DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF FDC with a CYP3A inducer decreases DOR plasma concentrations, which may reduce DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF FDC efficacy. DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF FDC is contraindicated with drugs that are strong cytochrome P450 (CYP)3A enzyme inducers as following (may not be all-inclusive):the anticonvulsants carbamazepine, oxcarbazepine, phenobarbital, phenytoin
- the androgen receptor inhibitor enzalutamide
- the antimycobacterials rifampin, rifapentine
- the cytotoxic agent mitotane
- St. John's wort (*Hypericum perforatum*)
L565: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L566: - If DOR is coadministered with rifabutin, increase DOR dosage to 100 mg twice daily (approximately 12 hours apart). If DOR/3TC/TDF FDC is co-administered with rifabutin, take one tablet of DOR/3TC/TDF FDC once daily, followed by DOR 100 mg approximately 12 hours after the dose of DOR/3TC/TDF FDC.
- Coadministration of DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF FDC and drugs that are inhibitors of CYP3A may result in increased plasma concentrations of DOR. However, no dose adjustment is needed based on the available safety profiles.
- Prior to initiation of DOR, at least 4 weeks of a cessation period is needed following administration of a moderate or strong CYP3A inducer.
- No dose adjustment is needed when DOR is coadministered with multiple CYP3A inhibitors.
L567: ## Outstanding Issues
L568: The exposure of the M9 metabolite in humans has not been measured and reported to FDA. Furthermore, the exposure of M9 has never been evaluated when DOR is administered as 100 mg BID and coadministered with rifabutin. A PMC will be issued to address this uncertainty.
L569: ## 6.3. Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
L570: ## 6.3.1. General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
L571: Pharmacology
Mechanism of Action	DOR: a non-nucleoside reverse transcriptase inhibitor [NNRTI];
3TC and TDF: nucleoside analogue reverse transcriptase inhibitors
Active Moieties	DOR: active, 75% of circulating radioactivity in plasma;
3TC: Intracellularly, 3TC is phosphorylated to its active 5'-triphosphate metabolite, lamivudine triphosphate (3TC-TP);
Tenofovir: Tenofovir DF requires initial diester hydrolysis for conversion to tenofovir and subsequent phosphorylations by cellular enzymes to form active tenofovir diphosphate.
QT Prolongation	DOR: At a dose of 1200 mg, which provides approximately 4 times the peak concentration observed following the maximum approved dose, DOR does not prolong the QT interval to any clinically relevant extent.
3TC: not available;
TDF: not available
General Information
Bioanalysis	Validated HPLC/MS/MS methods were used to determine the concentrations of DOR, 3TC, tenofovir (TFV), and coadministered drug in human plasma, urine or feces

L577: ## 6.3.2. Clinical Pharmacology Questions
L578: ## Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive evidence of effectiveness?
L579: The primary evidence of efficacy of doravirine was provided by two pivotal Phase 3 trials (DRIVE-FORWARD [P018] and DRIVE-AHEAD [P021]) in antiretroviral treatment-naïve, HIV-1 infected subjects. The exposure-response (E-R) analysis based on PK and efficacy data from the Phase 3 trials provided supportive evidence of effectiveness of doravirine.
L580: ## Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?
L581: Yes, the proposed dosing regimen is appropriate for the treatment of HIV-1 infection in adult patients who have not received prior antiretroviral treatment. The proposed regimen for DOR is 100 mg taken once daily with or without food, in combination with other antiretroviral agents. The proposed regimen for the fixed dose combination is one DOR/3TC/TDF 100 mg/300 mg/300 mg combination tablet taken orally once daily with or without food. The proposed doses for 3TC and TDF in the fixed dose combination are the approved doses for 3TC and TDF.
L582: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L583: The relationships between DOR exposure and efficacy endpoints were evaluated in Phase 2 and Phase 3 studies separately due to different methods for endpoint derivation between the Phase 2 and Phase 3 trials. In the 2 pivotal trials, P018 and P021, the primary endpoint was the proportion of subjects with HIV-1 RNA &lt;50 copies/mL at Week 48. In the supportive Phase 2b trial (P007), the primary endpoint was the proportion of subjects with HIV-1 RNA &lt;40 copies/mL at Week 24. No clinically meaningful association was found between DOR exposure and efficacy endpoints over the range of exposure achieved between 25 to 200 mg doses. E-R relationships for efficacy in Phase 3 study suggest the steady state DOR AUC_{0-24} and C24 values achieved following the proposed 100 mg QD dose are associated with high levels of antiretroviral activity and low rate of virologic failure, which provides additional evidence of effectiveness for the proposed dosing regimen of 100 mg QD (Figure 1).
L584: **Figure 1: Predicted and Observed Proportion of Subjects Achieving HIV-1 RNA &lt;50 copies/mL or Virologic Failure (VF) as a Function of DOR Steady State C24 Quartiles Following Administration of 100 mg once-daily DOR with Truvada (P018 and P021)**
L585: Note: SADR50: Proportion of subjects achieving HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL – snapshot Approach. OADR50: Proportion of subjects achieving HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL – observed failure approach. VF: Virologic failure. The solid line represents mean predicted exposure-response relationship. Shaded area represents the 95% confidence interval of the prediction over the 5th to 95th percentile of exposures. Markers represent the observed endpoint frequency by C24 quantile.
L586: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L587: Source: Reviewer's Analysis based on Dataset "mk1439-er-18-21-20170622.xpt" to confirm Figure 4 in Applicant's ER report
L588: Results of safety analyses for the Phase 2b and Phase 3 trials indicate that the 100-mg QD dose of DOR is generally well-tolerated. No safety findings of concern have been identified in the clinical program. Nevertheless, given the incidence of neuropsychiatric AEs or unfavorable changes from baseline in fasting lipids for other HIV-1 antiretroviral treatments, the DOR exposure-response (E-R) relationship for these endpoints was investigated. There was no association between the incidence of neuropsychiatric AEs or fasting LDL-C levels and DOR exposure over the 25 mg to 200 mg QD dose range evaluated in the Phase 2b and Phase 3 trials. Evaluation of the E-R relationship for lipid profiles suggested a positive trend between DOR PK and change in LDL-C from baseline at week 48, although this relationship was not statistically significant. Predicted relationships between DOR PK and change in lipids indicate that lower DOR exposure was associated with a larger decrease in lipids from baseline. However, this relationship is not considered clinically meaningful.
L589: ## **Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors?**
L590: ## **Renal Impairment**
L591: No dose adjustment of DOR is required in patients with mild, moderate, or severe renal impairment. Renal excretion of DOR is minor, with approximately 6% of the administered dose excreted unchanged in urine. Study 051 compared 8 subjects with severe renal impairment (baseline estimated glomerular filtration rate [eGFR] &lt; 30 mL/min/1.73 m2) to 8 healthy subjects without renal impairment, as shown in the following table, the single dose exposure of DOR was 43% higher in subjects with severe renal impairment.
L592: **Table 19. DOR Pharmacokinetics in Renally Impaired versus Healthy Patients, Study 051**
L593: DOR PK Parameter (100 mg single dose)	Mean (%CV)	%GLSM Ratio (90% CI)
Severe Renal Impairment	Normal Renal function
AUCinf (\muM\cdothr)	77.8 (63.4%)	45.7 (31.9%)	1.43 (1.00, 2.04)
Cmax (nM)	1780 (42.0%)	1920 (26.7%)	0.83 (0.61, 1.15)
C24 (nM)	1070 (47.7%)	702 (33.2%)	1.38 (0.99, 1.92)
L594: In the population PK analysis, eGFR was not identified as a significant covariate. Compared with HIV-1 infected subjects with normal renal function, DOR steady-state AUC0-24 and C24 were 20% and 69% higher, respectively, in patients with moderate renal impairment, with smaller changes in patients with mild renal impairment. Although no patients with severe renal impairment and only 6 subjects with moderate renal impairment were included in the Phase 2/3 trials, the magnitude of the increase of DOR concentrations was not considered clinically significant.
L595: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L596: DOR has not been studied in patients with end-stage renal disease or in patients undergoing dialysis.
L597: DOR/3TC/TDF FDC is not recommended in patients with estimated creatinine clearance less than 50 mL/min, because the dosage regimen for 3TC and TDF need to be modified in patients with estimated creatinine clearance below 50 mL/min or in patients with ESRD who require dialysis, but the dosing interval or dose cannot be modified for a component of the FDC.
L598: ## ***Hepatic Impairment***
L599: No dose adjustment of DOR is required in patients with mild (Child-Pugh Class A) or moderate (Child-Pugh Class B) hepatic impairment.
L600: DOR is primarily metabolized and eliminated by the liver. A single dose hepatic impairment study (Study P019) shows that there was no clinically relevant difference in the pharmacokinetics of DOR in subjects with moderate hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh score B, n=8) as compared to healthy subjects without hepatic impairment (n=8).
L601: **Table 20. DOR Pharmacokinetics in Hepatically Impaired versus Healthy Patients, Study P019**

(100 mg single dose)	Mean (%CV)	%GLSM Ratio (90% CI)
Moderate Hepatic Impairment	Normal Hepatic function
