L603: DOR has not been studied in subjects with severe hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh score C).
L604: ## ***Geriatric***
L605: No clinically relevant differences in the pharmacokinetics of DOR have been identified in subjects at least 65 years of age compared to subjects less than 65 years of age in a Phase 1 trial (Study P09) or in a population pharmacokinetic analysis. Phase 2/3 trials of DOR did not include sufficient numbers of subjects aged 65 and over (N=7) to determine whether they respond differently from younger adult subjects.
L606: ## ***Gender***
L607: No clinically relevant pharmacokinetic differences have been identified between men and women for DOR. In Study P09, the AUC0-\infty and Cmax of MK-1439 were 20% and 42% higher, respectively, for female as compared to male subjects. However, the difference is not expected to be clinically significant. In addition, in the population PK analysis, no influence of gender on the PK of DOR was identified as part of the covariate analysis.
L608: ## ***Body Weight***
L609: Body weight (36.6 to 195.5 kg) was found to have a statistically significant effect on DOR clearance and volume of distribution. However, the effects of body weight on CL and volume of distribution were modest (&lt;25%).
L610: ## ***Pediatric***
L611: The pharmacokinetics and dosing recommendations of DOR in patients younger than 18 years of age have not been established.
L612: ## ***Race***
L613: No clinically relevant racial differences in the pharmacokinetics of DOR have been identified based on a population PK analysis of DOR in healthy and HIV-1-infected subjects. The majority of individuals in the population PK analysis were white (65%), with black and Asian individuals representing 22% and 7% of the analysis population, respectively, and other (including multiracial, and Native American) individuals representing ~6% of the analysis population. Formal testing of the effect of this covariate indicated that race did not have a statistically significant effect on the PK of DOR.
L614: ## ***Are there clinically relevant food-drug or drug-drug interactions, and what is the appropriate management strategy?***
L615: ## ***Food Effect***
L616: DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF may be dosed without regard to food. The effect of a standard high-fat meal on the PK of DOR from the oral tablet (Study P037) and the combination DOR/3TC/TDF film-coated tablet (Study P029) was formally assessed in food effect trials in healthy subjects. Food increases DOR AUC_{0-inf} and C24 by 16% and 36%, respectively, and did not remarkably change Cmax. Administration of DOR/3TC/TDF with food increased DOR AUC_{0-inf} and C24 by 10% and 26%, respectively, but had no significant effect on Cmax. Increases to C24 with food are likely due to slightly delayed Tmax. 3TC AUC_{0-inf} and Cmax decreased by 7% and 19% respectively, while tenofovir AUC_{0-inf} increased by 27% and Cmax decreased by 12%. The magnitude of the food effect is not considered clinically significant for any of the three components. In the Phase 2/Phase 3 trials, DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF were dosed without regard to food. Therefore, DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF may be dosed without regard to food.
L617: ## ***Drug-Drug Interactions***
L618: **DOR as a victim drug:** As shown from in vitro and in vivo studies, DOR is primarily metabolized by CYP3A, and drugs that induce or inhibit CYP3A may affect the clearance of DOR. Co-administration of DOR and drugs that induce CYP3A result in decreased plasma concentrations of DOR. Co-administration of DOR and drugs that inhibit CYP3A result in increased plasma concentrations of DOR.
L619: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L620: Although an in vitro study indicated that DOR may be a P-gp substrate, the in vivo drug interaction study with ledipasvir/sofosbuvir indicated that ledipasvir, a P-gp inhibitor, has no effect on DOR exposures. Therefore, the in vivo study confirms that the potential of DOR as a P-gp substrate may not be clinically significant.
L621: The effects of co-administration with other drugs on the exposure (C_{max}, AUC, and C_{24}) of DOR are summarized in Table 21. CYP3A inhibitors ketoconazole and ritonavir increase DOR AUC by about 3-fold. CYP3A inducers rifampin, rifabutin and efavirenz reduce DOR AUC by 32% to 88%. A single DOR 100 mg dose was administered in these studies unless otherwise noted.

L630: No dose adjustment is necessary for coadministration of DOR and a strong CYP3A inhibitor. In Phase 2/3 trials, 12 subjects used a strong non-topical CYP3A inhibitor during treatment with DOR and no significant safety concerns were identified. In addition, DOR up to 750 mg for 10 days was studied in healthy volunteers with no safety concerns identified. PBPK analysis indicated that adding a strong CYP3A inhibitor on top of another strong CYP3A inhibitor would not significantly increase DOR concentrations further. Therefore, no dose adjustment is needed even if DOR is coadministered with multiple CYP3A inhibitors.
L631: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L632: DOR should be contraindicated with strong CYP3A inducers, because the strong CYP3A inducer, rifampin reduced DOR AUC by 88% and Cmin by 97%. FDA's PBPK analysis indicates that prior to initiation of DOR, at least 4 weeks of a cessation period may be needed following administration of a strong CYP3A inducer. If DOR is coadministered with rifabutin, DOR should be increased from 100 mg once daily to twice daily, because rifabutin decreases DOR AUC by 50%. Nonparametric superposition (NPS) analysis has been conducted to predict DOR steady-state exposure following DOR 100 mg BID when coadministered with rifabutin. Based on NPS projections, 100 mg BID DOR coadministered with 300 mg QD rifabutin achieves similar C24, AUC0-24, and Cmax values to 100 mg QD DOR in the absence of a CYP3A inducer. However, coadministration of DOR with rifabutin may increase DOR metabolite (M9) exposures. To evaluate the maximum effect of induction, PBPK analysis was conducted to evaluate the effect of rifampin on M9. The analysis shows that a strong CYP3A inducer may increase M9 AUC and Cmax by up to 2-fold and 4-fold, respectively. By increasing DOR dosing frequency from once daily to twice daily when coadministered with a moderate CYP3A inducer (rifabutin), M9 AUC is expected to increase approximately 4-fold, at maximum, by assuming a linear relationship between parent drug and production of M9 via CYP3A. The maximum levels of M9 after coadministration of DOR with rifabutin are anticipated to be 20 µM or less. CYP3A inducers were not included in the Phase 2/3 trials. The supporting safety data for M9 are summarized as follows:
L633: - Multiple doses of up to 750 mg QD (n=6) for 10 days provided an exposure multiple of ~3.7-fold relative to the steady-state AUC following administration of 100 mg QD.
- Single doses of up to 1200 mg (n=6) provided an exposure multiple of 6.5-fold relative to the steady-state AUC following administration of 100 mg QD.
- Non-clinical safety margin is more than 6.5-fold with coadministration of rifabutin (NOAEL AUC=196 µM.hr).
- M9 was evaluated by the CDER/OTS/OCP/DARS Chemical Informatics Program for genetic toxicity using (quantitative) structure-activity relationship [(Q)SAR] models. M9 is predicted to be overall negative for the bacterial mutagenicity, mouse lymphoma, *in vitro* chromosome aberrations, and *in vivo* micronucleus assays based on the application of expert knowledge.
L634: Although there are only very limited human safety data (n=6) from multiple doses of 750 mg QD (n=6) for 10 days, which may provide the similar M9 exposures for coadministration of DOR and rifabutin, nonclinical data and (Q)SAR assessment for M9 indicate that there is likely not a significant safety concern over coadministration of DOR and rifabutin.
L635: In the efavirenz DDI study, following multiple-dose efavirenz pretreatment relative to no pretreatment, doravirine multiple-dose Day 14 AUC, Cmax and Cmin were reduced by approximately 32%, 14% and 50%, respectively. A PBPK analysis using SimCyp V16 was conducted by the reviewer and the simulation indicates that at least 4 weeks of a cessation period is needed following a strong CYP3A inducer before initiation of doravirine therapy. By week 4 following discontinuation of rifampin treatment (600 mg QD for 2 weeks), doravirine AUC can still be reduced by 19%. If the rifampin dosage regimen is increased from 600 mg QD
L636: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807

L670: ## 7.2. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L671: ## 7.2.1. PN007
L672: Trial PN007 was a two-part, Phase 2 dose-ranging trial to compare the safety and efficacy of various doses of doravirine (DOR) to efavirenz (EFV) 600 mg in HIV-1-infected, treatment-naïve subjects.
L673: In Part 1 of the trial, subjects were randomized in a 1:1:1:1:1 ratio to one of five treatment arms: DOR 25 mg, DOR 50 mg, DOR 100 mg, DOR 200 mg, and EFV 600 mg, all taken QD. Based on results from Part 1, a single dose of DOR was chosen for Part 2. Subjects receiving DOR at any dose in Part 1 switched to the chosen dose, DOR 100 mg, for Part 2 of the trial. Subjects randomized to EFV 600 mg in Part 1 continued to receive EFV 600 mg in Part 2. Part 2 of the trial enrolled additional subjects and randomized them in a 1:1 ratio to DOR 100 mg and EFV 600 mg. Along with the randomized study drug, subjects received Truvada QD (FTC/TDF 200/300 mg). Randomization was stratified by screening HIV-1 RNA (\le 100,000 copies/mL, &gt; 100,000 copies/mL).
L674: The efficacy endpoints for Part 1 include the proportion of subjects that achieve HIV-1 RNA &lt; 40 copies/mL at Week 24 and the proportion of subjects that achieve HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL at Week 24. The efficacy endpoints for Part 2 include the proportion of subjects that achieve HIV-1 RNA &lt; 40 copies and HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL, both measured at Weeks 24, 48, and 96. HIV-1 RNA was quantified using the Abbott RealTime HIV-1 Assay, which has a lower limit of quantification (LLOQ) of 40 copies/mL. In addition, Tier 1 safety endpoints for Part 2 include the proportion of subjects that experience at least one neuropsychiatric event by Weeks 8 and 24. The trial was not designed to conduct statistical testing on the primary efficacy endpoint. However, it did pre-specify the Tier 1 safety endpoints in Part 2 for statistical testing.
L675: All the aforementioned endpoints were assessed by calculating the difference in proportions between the DOR arm and the EFV arm. The 95% confidence interval (CI) for the difference in proportion was calculated using Miettinen's and Nurminen's method to adjust for the randomization strata. In addition, a p-value was calculated for both safety endpoints. The safety endpoints were tested in sequential order, with testing of the Week 24 safety endpoint being dependent on rejecting the null hypothesis for the Week 8 safety endpoint. The testing of the Tier 1 safety endpoints is considered acceptable due to the pre-specification and the sequential order of the testing of the endpoints.
L676: Subject dispositions and baseline characteristics were relatively balanced across treatment arms, considering the limited sample sizes for each arm.
L677: Given the similarity of the results and to be consistent with the other trials in the NDA submissions, only the results for the proportion of subjects that achieve HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL are presented. Table 24 contains the proportion of subjects that achieve HIV-1 RNA &lt;

L679: 50 copies/mL at Week 24 in Part 1, as measured by the FDA-defined snapshot algorithm. The observed proportions are similar across the DOR arms and higher than the proportion in the EFV arm.
L680: Table 25 contains the proportion of subjects that achieve HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL at Week 24 in Part 1/2. The proportions are similar between DOR 100 mg and EFV 600 mg.

L687: The results generated from PN007 suggest that doravirine has similar efficacy across treatment doses and that DOR 100 mg has a similar level of efficacy to EFV 600 mg. In addition, a somewhat higher proportion of discontinuations due to AE were observed in the EFV arms than in the DOR arms, although the occurrence of such discontinuations was low. Results from the PN007 Tier 1 safety endpoint analysis are discussed in Section 9.5 (subsection on Neuropsychiatric Analysis).
L688: ## Dose Selection
L689: As discussed in Section 6.3.2, Protocol 007 efficacy and safety data did not distinguish among the DOR doses tested. The DOR 100 mg dose allows for adequate DOR exposures in the setting of moderate CYP3A inducers, allows for a safety margin in the setting of moderate CYP3A inhibitors. In addition, E-R relationships for efficacy in Phase 3 suggest the steady state DOR AUC0-24 and C24 values achieved following the proposed 100 mg QD dose are associated with high levels of antiretroviral activity and low rate of virologic failure, which provides additional evidence of effectiveness for the proposed dosing regimen of 100 mg QD.
L690: ## 7.2.2. PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD
L691: ## Trial Design and Endpoints
L692: Both PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD are active-controlled noninferiority (NI) trials. In this setting, it is acceptable to use NI trials to support the efficacy of DOR. Several drugs, including the active controls in both trials, have been approved for the treatment of HIV-1 infection in treatment-naïve patients and have “sufficiently well-described effects” of efficacy (HIV-1 Guidance for Industry, 2015). The NI trials also allow subjects randomized to the control treatment to receive an active treatment for HIV. Both trials utilized a NI margin of 10%. The use of a 10% NI margin is consistent with the recommendation outlined in the HIV-1 Guidance for Industry (2015).
L693: Trial PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD was a multicenter, randomized, double-blind, active-controlled, NI trial that evaluated the efficacy, safety, and pharmacokinetics of DOR compared to darunavir boosted with ritonavir (DRV+r) in HIV-1-infected, treatment-naïve adults. Either Truvada (FTC/TDF 200/300 mg) or Epzicom/Kivexa (ABC/3TC 600/300 mg) was also administered to subjects, based on the judgment of the study investigator, as an NRTI background therapy.
L694: Subjects were randomized in a 1:1 ratio to receive either DOR 100 mg QD or DRV+r 800/100 mg QD, along with the chosen NRTI background therapy. The randomized treatment was blinded but the NRTI background therapy was not blinded. Randomization was stratified by NRTI background therapy and HIV-1 RNA at screening (\le or &gt; 100,000 copies/mL). Subjects in the base study received blinded study treatment for 96 weeks. Such subjects were then eligible to enter the open-label extension to receive DOR 100 mg QD, along with either FTC or 3TC and TDF or TAF. At the end of study treatment, subjects were followed for 14 days. The trial schematic is shown in Figure 2.
L695: **Figure 2: Trial Schematic for PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD**
L696: 1 Virologic failure or relapse is confirmed with 2 consecutive measurements of HIV-1 -RNA at least one week apart
L697: Source: PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD Week 48 Clinical Study Report, Figure 9-1, page 44
L698: PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD was a multicenter, randomized, double-blind, active-controlled, NI trial that evaluated the efficacy and safety of MK-1439A, a fixed dose combination (FDC) of doravirine, lamivudine, and tenofovir disoproxil fumarate (DOR/3TC/TDF), compared to Atripla, a FDC of efavirenz, emtricitabine, and tenofovir disoproxil fumarate (EFV/FTC/TDF), in HIV-1-infected, treatment-naïve adults.
L699: Subjects were randomized in a 1:1 ratio to receive DOR/3TC/TDF 100/300/300 mg QD or EFV/FTC/TDF 600/200/300 mg QD in a blinded fashion. Randomization was stratified by HIV-1 RNA at screening (\le or &gt; 100,000 copies/mL) and HBV and/or HCV coinfection (yes or no). Subjects in the base study will receive blinded study treatment for 96 weeks. Such subjects are then eligible to enter the open-label extension where all will receive DOR/3TC/TDF 100/300/300 mg QD. At the end of study treatment, subjects will be followed for 14 days. The trial schematic is shown in Figure 3.
L700: Figure 3: Trial Schematic for PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD
L701: 1 Virologic failure or relapse is confirmed with 2 consecutive measurements of HIV-1 –RNA at least one week apart
L702: Source: PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD Week 48 Clinical Study Report, Figure 9-1, page 55
L703: Study visits included a screening assessment, a baseline assessment on Day 1, and visits at Week 4, 8, 16, 24, and every 12 weeks until the unblinding visit at Week 96. PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD included an additional visit at Week 2. Every study visit except for the Week 2 visit in trial PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD included HIV-1 RNA measurements. Every study visit included measurements of safety laboratory tests, including LDL-C and non-HDL-C. CD4+ T-cell counts were measured at screening, baseline, and Weeks 8, 24, 48, 72, and 96.
L704: For both confirmatory trials, the primary efficacy endpoint was the proportion of subjects that achieve HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL at Week 48. The Abbott RealTime HIV-1 Assay, with a LLOQ of 40 copies/mL, was used to quantify HIV-1 RNA. The primary efficacy endpoint is considered appropriate for the subject population, HIV-1 treatment-naïve patients, per the HIV-1 Guidance for Industry (2015). In accordance with the FDA-defined snapshot algorithm, the outcomes for the primary efficacy endpoint are defined as:
L705: - HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL
- HIV-1 RNA \ge 50 copies/mL
- Study drug discontinuation due to AE or death
- Study drug discontinuation due to reasons others than AE or death
- Missing RNA during Week 48 window but still on study drug
L706: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L707: A secondary analysis utilized the observed failure approach. Under the observed failure approach, subjects with an observed HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL at Week 48 were considered successes. Subjects with an observed HIV-1 RNA \ge 50 copies/mL at Week 48 or who discontinued from the study drug due to lack of efficacy prior to Week 48 were considered failures. However, in contrast to the snapshot algorithm, subjects with other types of discontinuations or missing data at Week 48 were excluded from this analysis. This approach is equivalent to the missing equals excluded (M = E) method of data imputation.
L708: Secondary efficacy endpoints include the proportion of subjects at Week 48 that achieve HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL under the observed failure approach as well as the proportions of subjects at Week 48 that achieve HIV-1 RNA &lt; 40 copies/mL and HIV-1 RNA &lt; 200 copies/mL, both under the snapshot algorithm and the observed failure approaches. Additionally, the change from baseline in CD4+ T-cell count at Week 48 was a secondary efficacy endpoint.
L709: Both PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD pre-specified safety endpoints for statistical testing. Please see Section 9.3 for further explanation of the statistical analysis of the safety endpoints.
L710: ## Key Inclusion and Exclusion Criteria
L711: The inclusion and exclusion criteria were identical for PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, except for additional criteria for PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD for absence of ABC resistance, and additional criteria for PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD relating to hemoglobin levels and decompensated cirrhosis.
L712: ## Key Inclusion Criteria
L713: - Both PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEADAt least 18 years of age
- HIV-1 infected
- ARV treatment-naïve
- At screening, alkaline phosphatase \le 3.0 \times ULN, AST and ALT \le 5.0 \times ULN, and creatinine clearance \ge 50 mL/min by the Cockcroft-Gault equation
- Clinically stable with no sign of active infection
- Unlikely to become pregnant or to impregnate a partner (either not of reproductive potential or agrees to use of acceptable contraception methods)
- PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD onlyAt screening, have hemoglobin \ge 9.0 g/dL (if female) or \ge 10.0 g/dL (if male)
L714: ## Key Exclusion Criteria
L715: - Both PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEADUse of illicit drugs or recent history of abuse of illicit drugs or alcohol

- Documented or known resistance to DOR, EFV, FTC, 3TC, and/or TDF

## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L717: - Use of systemic immunosuppressive therapy or immune modulators within 30 days prior to study treatment, or anticipated use during the study
- Current diagnosis of acute hepatitis
- Pregnant or breastfeeding, or expecting to become pregnant during the study
- PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD onlyDocumented or known resistance to ABC
- PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD onlyEvidence of decompensated or advanced liver disease, or liver cirrhosis with a Child-Pugh Class C score or a Pugh-Turcotte score &gt;9
L718: ## Statistical Analysis Plan
L719: The Applicant seeks to present evidence of efficacy by demonstrating that DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF are noninferior to DRV+r and EFV/FTC/TDF, respectively, in treatment-naïve, HIV-1-infected subjects. Noninferiority was assessed in each trial using the primary efficacy endpoint, the proportion of subjects with HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL at Week 48. The difference in proportions between the treatment arms was calculated and summarized using a 95% CI. The Cochran-Mantel-Haenszel method was used to calculate the 95% CI to account for the randomization strata. If the lower bound of the 95% CI was greater than -10%, in accordance with the pre-specified NI margin of 10%, then the results concluded noninferiority of the DOR-containing treatment to the active control.
L720: The primary efficacy analysis defined the outcomes for the primary efficacy endpoint according to the FDA-defined snapshot algorithm. It also defined the analysis population as the full analysis set (FAS), which included all randomized and treated subjects with baseline data. Sensitivity analyses included using the observed failure method to assess the effect of missing data and using the per-protocol (PP) set as the analysis population for the primary efficacy analysis. The PP set is a subset of the FAS that excludes subjects that commit “important deviations from the protocol that could substantially affect or confound the results of the primary efficacy endpoint(s)” (PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD Study Report). Such deviations are categorized as:
L721: - Discontinuation for reasons not related to treatment
- Major protocol deviations having the potential to affect efficacy
- Noncompliance with study medication
L722: Per the applicant, the detailed criteria for excluding subjects from the PP set was “fully defined and documented in a memo before the Week 48 database lock and unblinding” (PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD Study Report).
L723: Several subjects in both trials were placed into the incorrect screening HIV-1 RNA stratum. PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD randomized 17 subjects, eight in the DOR arm and nine in the DRV+r arm, into a treatment arm using the opposite screening HIV-1 RNA stratum. PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD randomized 41 subjects, 26 in the DOR/3TC/TDF arm and 15 in the EFV/FTC/TDF arm, into a treatment arm using the opposite screening HIV-1 RNA stratum. An example of such an occurrence would be a subject with screening HIV-1 RNA &lt; 100,000 copies/mL that was
L724: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L725: randomized along with subjects with screening HIV-1 RNA \ge 100,000 copies/mL. The applicant used subjects' assigned randomization strata in its primary efficacy analysis. The Cochran-Mantel-Haenszel method of calculating 95% CI accounts for randomization strata based on the size of each stratum. In addition, placing subjects in an incorrect randomization stratum may introduce bias into the produced estimates. Therefore, randomizing subjects using the incorrect stratum may impact the calculated 95% CI used to determine noninferiority. A sensitivity analysis of the primary efficacy endpoint using the correct screening HIV-1 RNA stratum is presented in Section 7.2.3 and Table 27.
L726: Both confirmatory trials conducted both efficacy- and safety-related interim analyses. In PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, the external Data Monitoring Committee (eDMC) conducted a pre-specified efficacy-related interim analysis to assess futility of the trial once 340 randomized subjects reached the Week 24 visit or discontinued prior to it. The applicant stated that no adjustments for Type I error are necessary because the efficacy-related interim analysis only assessed futility. Nevertheless, the applicant allocated an alpha level of 0.00001 to the interim analyses. The protocol for PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD did not pre-specify any efficacy-related interim analysis.
L727: A review of the statistical analysis plan for safety-related endpoints can be found in Section 9.
L728: ## Protocol Amendments
L729: Protocol amendments were reviewed and overall did not markedly impact the main trial design or choice of primary endpoint, enhance patient safety or further clarify trial procedures, or alter permitted concomitant medications. Both Phase 3 protocols added an open-label extension whereby subjects could receive active DOR-containing treatment after completing the 96-week, double-blind base study.
L730: ## 7.2.3. Study Results
L731: ## Compliance with Good Clinical Practices
L732: The Applicant states the clinical trials were conducted in substantial conformance with ICH GCP requirements and applicable country and/or local statutes and regulations regarding ethical committee review, informed consent, and the protection of human subjects participating in biomedical research. The Phase 3 trials were conducted according to FDA requirements, under INDs 112796 and 124997. Additionally, FDA clinical site inspections did not reveal evidence of GCP non-compliance at the inspected sites, as noted in Section 4.1.

L734: See Section 15.2 for details on the financial disclosure.
L735: ## Subject Disposition
L736: Figure 4 depicts the subject disposition of all screened subjects in PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD. The statistical review confirmed the numbers reported in the figure.

L743: Table 26 contains the percentages of study discontinuation through Week 48 by treatment arm for both trials. In both trials, the percentages for study discontinuations are above 14%. Although the upper bounds of the 95% CIs for the treatment difference in discontinuations due to AE or death round to 0.0%, the upper bound for PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD was slightly less than zero. The DOR/3TC/TDF arm in PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD had a higher percentage of discontinuations due to lack of efficacy.

L747: It is important to note that some subjects achieved a primary efficacy outcome of HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL within the Week 48 window prior to their study discontinuation, also within the Week 48 window. L748: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L749: As noted above, the PP population in both Phase 3 trials was used as the secondary population for analysis of key efficacy endpoints, and consisted of subjects in the full analysis set population who had no important protocol deviations that could have substantially affected or confounded the results for the key efficacy endpoints.
L750: In PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD there were more protocol deviations among DRV/r recipients than DOR recipients; however, the efficacy results in the full analysis set population and the PP population were very similar. Consequently, these discrepancies did not affect the outcome or conclusions. In PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD rates of protocol deviations were similar among EFV recipients and DOR recipients, as were the efficacy results in the full analysis set population and the PP population: the protocol deviations did not affect the outcome or conclusions.
L751: ## Demographic Characteristics
L752: Table 27 contains information regarding demographic characteristics for each treatment arm for both trials. Within each trial, the demographic characteristics appear to be well-balanced between the two treatment arms. Across trials, PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD had a much smaller percentage of white subjects and a slightly smaller percentage of black subjects compared to PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD. About 105 subjects in PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD indicated “multiple” for race, indicating that the subjects identified with multiple races. In addition, PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD has a higher percentage of subjects with Hispanic ethnicity. These occurrences could be because a larger percentage of subjects in PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD enrolled in Latin America and

L758: ## **Other Baseline Characteristics (e.g., disease characteristics, important concomitant drugs)**
L759: Table 28 contains information regarding baseline disease characteristics for each treatment arm for both trials. In both trials, the DOR-containing arm had a slightly higher average (mean and median) baseline CD4 cell count than the active control arm. In PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, the DOR arm had a slightly higher percentage of subjects with baseline CD4 cell counts greater than 200 cells/mm3 than the DRV+r arm, 89% compared to 82.5%. In PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, the EFV/FTC/TDF arm had a slightly higher percentage of subjects with Clade B subtype than the DOR/3TC/TDF arm, 69.5% compared to 63.7%. Despite these differences, the baseline disease characteristics appear to be well-balanced between treatment arms within both trials. Across trials, PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD had a slightly higher percentage of subjects with a history of AIDS than PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD.

L771: PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD also misclassified 12 subjects in the DOR/3TC/TDF arm and 13 subjects in the EFV/FTC/TDF arm into the wrong hepatitis randomization strata. A total of 21 subjects randomized as Hepatitis B or C positive were later identified through laboratory testing as Hepatitis B and C negative, while four subjects randomized as Hepatitis B and C negative were later identified as Hepatitis B and/or C positive. However, the primary efficacy analysis did not adjust for the hepatitis randomization strata. As a result, the misclassifications for hepatitis status did not affect the primary efficacy analysis.
L772: Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use Study
Medication Diary Cards were used to document study treatment compliance in both Phase 3 trials, and the cards were reviewed at each scheduled visit. Reported compliance was similar for the DOR and active control treatment groups in each trial. In PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, 95% of DOR subjects and 96% of DRV/R subjects reported full compliance with all study drugs ≥90% of the time. In PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, 93% of DOR/3TC/TDF subjects and 88% of EFV/FTC/TDF subjects reported full compliance with all study drugs ≥90% of the time.
L773: Most subjects received concomitant medications (~80-87%), with similar proportions of subjects receiving concomitant medications in the DOR and active controls in the two Phase 3 trials.
L774: ## Efficacy Results
L775: The reviewer successfully replicated the applicant's efficacy analyses and produced results that are consistent with the applicant's results. In addition, the reviewer conducted additional analyses in order to assess efficacy further. The major points of the efficacy analyses are presented below.
L776: ## Efficacy Results – Primary Endpoint
L777: Table 30 summarizes the primary efficacy analysis: the proportion of subjects in the FAS with HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL at Week 48, defined using the snapshot algorithm. In PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, 83.8% of subjects in the DOR arm and 79.9% in the DRV+r arm achieved HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL at Week 48. The treatment difference was 3.9% with a 95.001% CI of (-1.6%, 9.4%). In PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, 84.3% of subjects in the DOR/3TC/TDF arm and 80.8% of subjects in the EFV/FTC/TDF arm achieved HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL at Week 48. The treatment difference was 3.5% with a 95% CI of (-2.0%, 9.0%). For both trials, the lower bound of the confidence interval for the treatment difference was above the pre-specified margin of -10%. Thus, the results conclude that DOR is noninferior to DRV+r and DOR/3TC/TDF was
L778: noninferior to EFV/FTC/TDF. Because the 95% CI from either trial did not exclude zero, the results do not support a conclusion of superiority.

L782: Table 31 summarizes the proportion of subjects in the PP population with HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL at Week 48. The treatment difference was 2.1% with a 95.001% CI of (-2.7%, 6.9%). As in the FAS, the lower bound of the confidence interval for the treatment difference was above the pre-specified margin of -10% for both trials. The results in the PP population support the previous conclusion of noninferiority of DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF to the active controls, particularly in the presence of minimal protocol violations.

L790: Source: statistical reviewer created
L791: To explore the impact of missing data on the primary efficacy analysis, the applicant repeated the primary efficacy analysis using the observed failure approach. Table 32 summarizes the results of the primary efficacy analysis under the observed failure approach. In PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, the observed treatment difference was 1.9% with a 95.001% CI of (-3.1%, 6.8%). In PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, the observed treatment difference was -0.2% with a 95% CI of (-4.9%, 4.6%). While the observed treatment differences decrease in both trials and even changes direction in PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, the lower bound of the 95% CI is greater than the NI margin of -10% for both trials. This result suggests that exclusion of missing data does not affect the noninferiority of DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF compared to DRV+r and EFV/FTC/TDF, respectively.

L795: As stated previously, several subjects in both trials were randomized using the incorrect screening HIV-1 RNA stratum. In order to evaluate whether the incorrect assignments influenced the results of noninferiority, the statistical reviewer repeated the primary efficacy analysis using the correct screening HIV-1 RNA stratum for all subjects as a sensitivity analysis. Using the correct screening HIV-1 RNA stratum does not change the primary efficacy endpoint result for subjects. However, it may affect the estimated treatment difference and its corresponding 95% CI because both account for the size of the randomization strata.
L796: Based on the results presented in Table 33, the incorrect assignments regarding screening HIV-1 RNA do not appear to alter the results of noninferiority of DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF to DRV+r and EFV/FTC/TDF, respectively. For both trials, the treatment difference and 95% CI change very little, if at all, when using the correct screening HIV-1 RNA strata.

L808: ## **Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints**
L809: The secondary endpoint analyses conducted were to evaluate the sensitivity of the noninferiority finding to the choice of threshold for viral suppression. All the analyses showed consistent results and support the conclusion of noninferiority reached based on the results of the primary efficacy analysis. The specifics of the analyses are shown below.
L810: ## *HIV-1 RNA &lt; 40 copies/mL at Week 48*
L811: Table 35 summarizes the results of the analysis of the secondary endpoint: HIV-1 RNA &lt; 40 copies/mL at Week 48 in the FAS. The number of subjects with discontinuations and missing RNA data remain the same as in the primary efficacy analysis.
L812: In PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, 83.3% of subjects in the DOR arm and 79.1% in the DRV+r arm achieved HIV-1 RNA &lt; 40 copies/mL at Week 48. The treatment difference was 4.2% with a 95% CI of (-1.4%, 9.7%). In PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, 83.8% of subjects in the DOR/3TC/TDF arm and 79.7% of subjects in the EFV/FTC/TDF arm achieved HIV-1 RNA &lt; 40 copies/mL at Week 48. The treatment difference was 3.3% with a 95% CI of (-2.0%, 8.6%).
L813: For both trials, the lower bound of the confidence interval for the treatment difference was above the pre-specified margin of -10%. The endpoint was also analyzed in two sensitivity analyses: one that used the PP population rather than the FAS (not shown) and one that used the observed failure approach rather than the snapshot algorithm (not shown). All three analyses of the secondary efficacy endpoint of HIV-1 RNA &lt; 40 copies/mL produced consistent and similar results. The findings support the conclusion of noninferiority reached based on the results of the primary efficacy analysis.

L878: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L879: between the two confirmatory trials. In PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, the proportion of subjects with HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL at Week 48 was higher for the DOR arm than the DRV+r arm. However, in PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, the proportion subjects with HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL was lower in the DOR/3TC/TDF arm than the EFV/FTC/TDF arm.
L880: The treatment difference in PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD among subjects with baseline CD4+ T-cell count \le 200 cells/mm^3 is powered by the treatment difference among such subjects who also have assigned screening HIV-1 RNA \le 100,000 copies/mL. Of subjects with baseline CD4+ T-cell count \le 200 cells/mm^3 and assigned screening HIV-1 RNA \le 100,000 copies/mL, 63.2% (12/19) in the DOR/3TC/TDF arm achieved HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL at Week 48 compared to 86.4% (19/22) in the EFV/FTC/TDF arm. The treatment difference was -12.1%, with a 95% CI of (-31.1%, 6.9%). Among subjects with baseline CD4+ T-cell count \le 200 cells/mm^3 in PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, a higher proportion of subjects in the DOR arm achieved HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL regardless of assigned screening HIV-1 RNA strata. The observed treatment difference using the actual screening HIV-1 RNA strata was consistent with the treatment difference using the assigned screening HIV-1 RNA strata.
L881: The Breslow-Day test to assess the treatment effect of the DOR-containing treatment compared to the active control between baseline CD4+ T-cell count \le 200 cells/mm^3 and &gt; 200 cells/mm^3 produced p-values of 0.167 in PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and 0.043 in PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD. However, the small sample sizes and the inconsistent treatment differences for subjects with baseline CD4+ T-cell count \le 200 cells/mm^3 between the two trials add complexity to the interpretation of the findings. While the analysis is considered exploratory and the testing is not adjusted for multiple comparisons, the efficacy of DOR according to baseline CD4+ T-cell count may warrant further exploration in future trials to determine if any consistent trends exist.
L882: DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF Section 14 labeling will include Phase 3 trial virologic outcome, defined as the proportion of subjects with HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL, by baseline demographic and disease state characteristics including CD4+ T-cell count.
L883: ## *CD4+ T-cell Count*
L884: For the Week 48 change from baseline in CD4+ T-cell count, the statistical reviewer obtained results consistent with the applicant's results. None of the evaluated measures of disease characteristics had a treatment difference with a corresponding 95% CI that excluded zero. Thus, there is no evidence to suggest a differential treatment effect of DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF on Week 48 change from baseline in CD4+ T-cell count compared to DRV+r or EFV/FTC/TDF, respectively.
L885: ## 7.3. Statistical Issues Related to Review of Effectiveness
L886: While the efficacy analysis was straightforward for both trials, there were some statistical issues present in the analyses for this application. The issues were previously discussed in the review and will therefore only be summarized in this section.
L887: ## Subjects Randomized Using Incorrect Screening HIV-1 RNA Strata
L888: PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD randomized 17 and 41 subjects, respectively, into a treatment arm using the incorrect screening HIV-1 RNA stratum. Because the primary efficacy analysis adjusted for randomization strata, the incorrect strata assignments could have affected the calculated 95% CI used to determine noninferiority. However, sensitivity analyses that used subjects' correct screening HIV-1 strata produced results almost identical to the original primary efficacy analyses. This finding suggests that the observed results are robust to the misclassification of screening HIV-1 RNA.
L889: ## Calculation and Interpretation of Subgroup Analyses
L890: ## *Subgroup Analysis Using Snapshot Algorithm*
L891: The applicant presented subgroup analyses using the observed failure approach, rather than using the FDA-defined snapshot algorithm. The statistical reviewer conducted subgroup analyses using the snapshot algorithm.
L892: ## *Interpretation of Subgroup Analyses*
L893: DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF Section 14 labeling will include the virologic outcome data by certain baseline and demographic categories. The interpretation of the subgroup analyses is problematic. The analyses are not controlled for multiple comparisons, which can cause inflated Type I error. The analyses are also not powered for statistical inference due to limited sample size, which can cause wider confidence intervals. Therefore, the subgroup analyses are considered exploratory.
L894: ## *Adjustment for Randomization Strata*
L895: Several subgroups had limited sample sizes that produced randomization strata too small for adjustment. Thus, only unadjusted results are available for those subgroups. Additionally, several subgroups had zero subjects with screening HIV-1 RNA &gt; 100,000 copies/mL and who used ABC/3TC as the NRTI background therapy, which may affect the generalizability of the results.
L896: ## *Subgroup Analysis Conclusions*
L897: Considering the statistical issues regarding subgroup analyses, there may be need for further exploration of the efficacy of DOR and/or DOR/3TC/TDF in the previously noted subgroups within future trials.
L898: ## 7.4. Summary and Conclusions
L899: These NDA submissions contained data from three clinical trials: Phase 3 trials PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD and a dose-ranging Phase 2 trial, PN007. For both confirmatory trials, the primary efficacy endpoint was HIV-1 RNA &lt; 50 copies/mL at Week 48, measured using the FDA-defined snapshot algorithm. The purpose of PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD was to demonstrate the noninferiority of DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF to DRV+r and EFV/FTC/TDF, respectively, using a NI margin of -10%.
L900: As shown in sections above, both Phase 3 trials showed NI and support efficacy in the HIV-1 treatment-naïve population. In PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, 83.8% (321/383) of subjects in the DOR arm achieved the primary efficacy endpoint compared to 79.9% (306/383) of subjects in the DRV+r arm. The observed treatment difference was 3.9% with a 95.001% CI of (-1.6%, 9.4%). In PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, 84.3% (307/364) of subjects in the DOR/3TC/TDF arm achieved the primary efficacy endpoint compared to 80.8% (294/364) of subjects in EFV/FTC/TDF arm. The observed treatment difference was 3.5% with a 95% CI of (-2.0%, 9.0%). The lower bounds of both 95% CIs are greater than -10%. These data will be included in Section 14 of the DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF labels.
L901: The applicant and the statistical reviewer conducted additional analyses across different analysis populations, time points, and thresholds of viral suppression. Such analyses produced similar results to the primary efficacy analyses, further supporting the conclusion of noninferiority. Sensitivity analyses that sought to correct the incorrect assignment of randomization strata produced almost identical results to the primary efficacy analyses, indicating that the incorrect assignments do not alter the results.
L902: DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF Section 14 labeling will include Phase 3 trial virologic outcome data by the following baseline demographic subgroups, both for general consistency with other HIV labels and to inform patients and prescribers of virologic outcomes in these clinically relevant categories: gender, race, ethnicity, baseline HIV-1 RNA, baseline CD4, viral subtype. Numeric differences noted for certain Phase 3 trial subgroups were based on limited sample size and were not adjusted for multiple comparisons. Therefore, no definitive conclusions can be made. Conducting similar subgroup analyses in future DOR-containing trials may identify consistent trends.
L903: The efficacy results in the Phase 3 trials are supported by the efficacy results in PN007. The results generated from PN007 indicate that DOR has similar efficacy across treatment doses. The results also support a conclusion of similar efficacy of DOR 100 mg to EFV 600 mg, as the two treatment arms had an almost equal proportion of subjects that achieved viral suppression.
L904: Based on the statistical evidence gathered from the two confirmatory trials, PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, as well as supportive evidence from the dose-ranging trial PN007, these NDAs provides adequate evidence of the efficacy of the following:
L905: - Doravirine 100 mg, to be used in combination with other antiretroviral agents for the treatment of HIV-1 infection in treatment-naïve patients.
- The fixed dose combination of doravirine 100 mg, lamivudine 300 mg, and tenofovir disoproxil fumarate 300 mg as a complete regimen for the treatment of HIV-1 infection in treatment-naïve patients.
L906: ## 8. Clinical Virology Review
L907: ## 8.1. Nonclinical Virology
L908: ## 8.1.1. Mechanism of Action
L909: Doravirine (DOR) is a non-nucleoside reverse transcriptase inhibitor (NNRTI) of human immunodeficiency virus type 1 (HIV-1). HIV-1 reverse transcriptase (RT) is essential for HIV-1 replication as it converts the linear, single-stranded RNA genome into linear, double-stranded DNA using its RT and RNase H activities.
L910: The sponsor conducted numerous structural, biochemical and virologic studies to support the mechanism of action. Co-crystallization of DOR and HIV-1 RT and the resultant X-ray structures showed that DOR binds to the classic NNRTI pocket of reverse transcriptase (data not shown, Study Report PD001mk1439, page 3). To examine the inhibition activity of wildtype (WT) and variant RTs with NNRTI resistance-associated substitutions K103N or Y181C, the sponsor produced the full-length recombinant HIV-1 RT proteins in *Escherichia coli* and quantified the incorporation of ruthenium-conjugated-dUMP into the minus strand DNA in the presence or in the absence of added drug using an electrochemiluminescence RT biochemical assay. The inhibitory concentration at 50% (IC_{50}) of DOR for RNA-dependent DNA polymerization of WT RT (12.2\pm 2.0 nM, n=3) was similar to NNRTI mutant RTs expressing K103N (9.7\pm 1.4 nM, n=3), and Y181C (9.7\pm 0.9 nM, n=3) resistance substitutions. The IC_{50} value of DOR for WT RT (12.2 nM) was higher than the corresponding IC_{50} values for WT RT with efavirenz (0.42 nM), etravirine (0.64 nM), and rilpivirine (1.1 nM).
L911: The sponsor evaluated the inhibitory activity of DOR (assayed at concentrations from 1 to 100 \muM) with purified human DNA polymerases \alpha, \beta, and \gamma activities in biochemical assays using gapped fish sperm DNA as a template. Aphidicolin was used as a control inhibitor for DNA polymerase \alpha, and ddATP as a control inhibitor for polymerases \beta and \gamma. DOR inhibited DNA polymerases \alpha, \beta, and \gamma with an IC_{50} value &gt;100 \muM. Therefore, the selectivity index of DOR for HIV-1 RT (IC_{50} value = 12.2 nM, Study Report PD001mk1439, page 4) compared to cellular DNA polymerases (&gt; 100 \muM) is &gt;8,197-fold.
L912: ## 8.1.2. Antiviral Activity in Cell Culture
L913: The antiviral activity of DOR was tested using the HIV-1 isolate R8 grown in MT4-gag-GFP T lymphoid cells, in which the expression of the green fluorescent protein (GFP) reporter is dependent on the production of HIV-1 tat and rev (Study Report PD002mk1439). MT4-gag-GFP cells were infected with HIV-1 (R8 or variants carrying K103N, Y181C, or K103N/Y181C substitutions) and the inhibitors added. Efavirenz, etravirine, and rilpivirine were included for comparison. A combination of efavirenz, indinavir and an in-house integrase strand transfer inhibitor (INSTI) was used a positive control. HIV-1-infected cells were quantified at 48 and 72 hours post infection by counting the number of green cells. The DOR EC50 values extrapolated to 100% normal human serum were 12±4.4 nM (n=61), 21±6.8 nM (n=45), 31±10 nM (n=44), and 33±4.2 nM (n=7) against WT virus, K103N, Y181C, and K103N/Y181C mutant viruses (Table 41).

L924: ## 8.1.3. Antiviral Activity in Cell Culture in the Presence of Serum and Serum Proteins
L925: To determine if serum proteins affect the antiviral activity of DOR, the antiviral activity of DOR was determined against the HIV-1 R8 isolate in MT4 cells in the presence of 50% normal human serum compared to 10% normal human serum (Table 43). MT4 cells were infected with HIV-1 (R8) and incubated at 37°C for 24 hours. The cells were treated with serial dilutions of DOR or efavirenz as a control and incubated for another 72 hours. Cells were tested for HIV-1 growth by measuring p24 levels in the medium. The EC95 values of DOR against WT HIV-1 increased 1.8- to 3.2-fold in the presence of 50% normal human serum compared to 10% normal human serum. In comparison, the EC95 values of efavirenz increased from 5.8- to 9.9-fold, etravirine EC95 values increased 6.8- to 12.4-fold, and RPV EC95 values increased 18.5- to 31.5-fold, consistent with these being highly protein bound. Thus, the adjusted antiviral activity of DOR in clinically relevant levels of human serum is comparable to those of currently FDA approved antiretroviral drugs, efavirenz, etravirine, and rilpivirine.

L931: ## 8.1.4. Cytotoxicity, Mitochondrial Toxicity and Therapeutic Index
L932: The sponsor tested the cytotoxicity of DOR in stationary and activated human peripheral blood mononuclear cells (phytohemagglutinin P-treated PBMCs), CD4+ T helper cells
L933: (phytohemagglutinin P-treated), human monocytes (treated first with interferon \gamma and then with phorbol myristate acetate), human monocyte-derived macrophages, and in proliferating cells such as MT4 (human T cell leukemia cells), SupT1 (human T cell lymphoblastic lymphoma cells), and HL60 (human acute myeloid leukemia cells) cell lines (Study Report PD004mk1439). Cell viability was tested using AlamarBlue™ Cell Viability Reagent (Invitrogen) following incubation of the cells with DOR or with a known cytotoxic control compound (L-001898133-001N003) for ~3 days.
L934: DOR had no cytotoxicity up to a concentration of 100 \muM in stationary or in activated PBMCs, CD4+ T cells, monocytes, and macrophages as well as in proliferating MT4, SupT1, and HL60 cell lines treated for ~72 hours. The EC50 values were determined with infected cells treated with DOR for 48 or 72 hours. DOR displayed no more cytotoxicity than its dissolvent DMSO in cell cultures. A therapeutic index of &gt; 8,333 was calculated from the cytotoxicity value (CC50) and antiviral activity value (EC50) in MT4 cells (therapeutic index = &gt;100,000 nM/12 nM).
L935: ## 8.1.5. Combination Antiviral Activity in Cell Culture
L936: The combination antiviral activity relationships of DOR with each of 18 FDA-approved anti-HIV-1 drugs (NNRTI: delavirdine, efavirenz, etravirine, nevirapine, and rilpivirine; NRTI: abacavir, didanosine, emtricitabine, lamivudine, stavudine, tenofovir disoproxil fumarate, zalcitabine, zidovudine; CCR5 coreceptor antagonist: maraviroc; gp41 fusion inhibitor: enfuvirtide; INSTI, raltegravir; PI: darunavir, indinavir) were tested by [REDACTED](b) (4) (PD006mk1439). The known antagonistic combination of ribavirin and stavudine was used as a positive control for antagonism.
L937: All the combinations were tested three times in CEM-SS cells (a human T-lymphoblastoid cell line) with HIV-1IIIB, except for the combination of DOR with maraviroc, where HIV-1Ba-L was evaluated in MAGI-CCR5 (HeLa cells expressing high levels of CD4 and CCR5 coreceptor and the HIV-1 LTR driving \beta-galactosidase expression) cells. HIV-1-infected cells and uninfected cells were treated with DOR starting at 1,000 nM and seven serial half-log dilutions. Each dilution of DOR was tested in combination with each of the FDA-approved anti-HIV-1 drugs starting at 10x the EC50 value and four additional half log dilutions. Drug-treated CEM-SS and MAGI-R5 cells were incubated for 6 days and 2 days, respectively. Cell viability and \beta-galactosidase activity of drug-treated cells were tested using the MTS dye (CEM-SS cells) and Gal-Screen® assay (MAGI-CCR5 cells), respectively. The combination antiviral relationships were analyzed using the MacSynergy II program and the Bliss Independence Model. The combinations of DOR with any of the 18 FDA-approved anti-HIV-1 drugs tested did not show antagonistic activity on their combined antiviral activities (mean antagonism volume values &lt; -50) (Table 44).

L943: ## 8.1.6. Resistance Development in Cell Culture
L944: DOR-resistant HIV-1 (subtype B, isolate R8) variants were selected in Sup T1 cells infected at low and at high MOIs using fixed doses of DOR (10x, 50x and 250x EC95) (Study Report PD003mk1439). At high MOIs and 10x DOR EC95 value drug concentration, a single F227C substitution was detected in emerging HIV-1 resistant variants.
L945: At low MOIs with escalating DOR concentrations (1x to 50x EC95), the sponsor selected HIV-1 variants carrying known NNRTI resistance-associated substitutions, V106A, V108I, and F227L, as well as a rare H221Y substitution. Other known NNRTI resistance-associated substitutions (L100I and K101E) were selected in parallel experiments with efavirenz treatment of HIV-1 subtype B cultures.
L946: In addition, resistance selection was performed with HIV-1 subtypes A, B and C viruses. MT4 cells carrying an HIV-1 LTR-GFP fusion gene (MT4-GFP for subtype B) and MT4-GFP stably expressing the CCR5 gene (MT4-GFP/CCR5 for subtypes A and C) were infected at low MOIs and used to select DOR-resistant HIV-1 variants. V106A was the first substitution selected at low DOR concentrations in HIV-1 subtype B resistant variants. With increasing DOR concentrations, HIV-1 with double resistance-associated substitutions, V106A/F227L (FC values &gt;500) and V106A/L234I (FC value = 150) and with V106A/F227L/L234I triple substitutions emerged. Minor substitutions included, V108I, followed by emergence of V106A and L234I. In parallel efavirenz-treated cultures, HIV-1 variants carrying the single (L100I) followed by double (L100I/K103N, L100I/V179D or triple (L100I/V179D/P225H or M230L) resistance-associated substitutions were selected. HIV-1 variants carrying K103N, followed by L100I/K103N, were also selected by efavirenz treatment. L100I, K103N and V179D were the predominant efavirenz resistance-associated substitutions identified in the subtype B studies.
L947: In DOR selection of HIV-1 subtype A variants in MT4-GFP/CCR5 cells, V106A/M was selected followed by selection of double resistance-associated substitutions (V106A(M)/F227L(C/V)). Efavirenz selected HIV-1 variants carrying L100I with secondary emergence of L100I/Y188H/C or V179D and V106M followed by secondary selection of Y188C or L100I.
L948: In HIV-1 subtype C studies, DOR treatment selected HIV-1 variants carrying V106A followed by secondary emergence of F227I or V106M followed by emergence of F227C. Efavirenz selected HIV-1 L100I and V106M variants with secondary substitutions of Y188C or F227C.
L949: In summary, the predominant emergent HIV-1 DOR resistance-associated substitutions selected in cell culture were: RT V106A/I/M, V108I, H221Y, F227C/L/V, M230I, L234I, P236L, and Y318F. Based on the X-ray structure of DOR/RT complex, 3 residues (V106, V108, and F227) are in close proximity to DOR and, thus, may play an important role in the interactions between DOR and RT. The V106A and V108I mutant viruses conferred approximately 12- and 4-fold reductions in susceptibility to DOR, respectively. Emergence of additional substitutions likely further reduces the susceptibility of the mutant viruses to DOR. In the resistance selection with subtype A, B, and C viruses using MT4-GFP cells, viruses containing substitutions at V106 and F227 account for the majority of mutants in the breakthrough viruses under low MOI conditions. The double mutant virus containing substitutions V106A/F227L confers &gt;500-fold resistance to DOR.
L950: To determine EC50 values of DOR against circulating HIV-1 variants, a PhenoSense GT™ assay (performed by [REDACTED](b) (4)) was used. The PR and RT genes from HIV-1 present in
L951: patient plasma samples were amplified and inserted into a resistance test vector, which contains a luciferase gene. HIV-1 stocks carrying the patient specimen-derived sequences were generated and tested for susceptibility to DOR, efavirenz, etravirine, and rilpivirine. Data were analyzed by plotting inhibition of luciferase activity versus \log_{10} drug concentration and EC_{50} values were calculated by nonlinear least squares and bootstrapping (Study Report PD002mk1439).
L952: The sponsor highlighted the single Y188L and the triple (V106A/G190A/F227L) resistance-associated substitutions, which conferred &gt;100-fold decreases in susceptibility to DOR (Figure 10, panel A). However, another single substitution (G190S) and two double resistance-associated substitutions, L100I/K103N and K103N/P225H, conferred ~12-fold, ~15-fold, and ~12-fold reductions in susceptibility to DOR, respectively. K103N/Y181C also conferred 7-fold decreased susceptibility to DOR. In contrast, efavirenz susceptibility was decreased by &gt;100-fold by multiple NNRTI resistance-associated substitutions. Single (Y188L, G190S), double (L100I/K103N, K103N/G190A, K103N/P225H) and triple (K103N/Y181C/G190A, K101E/Y181C/G190A, and V106A/G190A/F227L) resistance-associated substitutions conferred &gt;100-fold reduction in susceptibility to efavirenz (Figure 10, panel B). Similar patterns in the relative frequencies and magnitude of DOR and efavirenz resistance phenotypes were observed using a panel of clinical NNRTI resistance-associated mutant viruses (below).
L953: **Figure 10: Comparison of the Mutant Profiles between DOR and Efavirenz in a Single Cycle**

L958: ## 8.1.7. Resistance and Cross-Resistance in a Panel of 96 Clinical NNRTI Resistance-Associated Mutant Viruses
L959: The susceptibilities of a panel of 96 clinical NNRTI resistance-associated mutant viruses to DOR and efavirenz, etravirine, and rilpivirine were examined by (b) (4) (summarized in Table 45 and Table 75). The sponsor analyzed the data using an arbitrary 10-fold cutoff for drug resistance to all the NNRTIs including DOR (Study Report PD002mk1439, page 12). However, we reanalyzed the data using various cutoffs (0&lt;5, ≥5-30, &gt;30) for DOR susceptibility
L960: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L961: and using the (b) (4) cutoffs for EFV (\ge 3-fold), ETR (\ge 2.9-fold), and RPV (\ge 2.0-fold). In the FDA analyses, DOR showed antiviral activity with EC_{50}-fold change &lt;5 against 62 of the 96 (65%) clinical HIV-1 isolates encoding RT NNRTI resistance-associated substitutions including the most commonly found K103N. Of the remaining 34 of the 96 (35%) clinical HIV-1 isolates with \ge 5-fold reduced susceptibility to DOR, 33 (97%), 23 (68%), and 27 (79%) had \ge 5-fold reduced susceptibility to efavirenz, etravirine, and rilpivirine, respectively.
L962: Overall, in the panel of 96 mutant viruses, 78 mutant viruses (81%) were resistant to efavirenz (\ge 3-fold reduction in susceptibility). Of these, 57 (73%) had &lt;5-fold reduction in susceptibility to DOR. Of the 63 viruses with \ge 10-fold shift in EC_{50} value with efavirenz, 17 (27%) were reduced in susceptibility to DOR (\ge 10 fold).
L963: Forty-one percent (n=39) of the 96 HIV-1 isolates encoding NNRTI resistance-associated substitutions were resistant to etravirine (\ge 3-fold reduction in susceptibility) and, of these, 59% (n=23) were also resistant to DOR (\ge 5-fold reduced susceptibility). Of the 15 mutant viruses that had \ge 10 reduction in susceptibility to etravirine, eight (53%) were also resistant to DOR.
L964: Almost half (47%; n=45) of the mutant viruses were reduced in susceptibility to rilpivirine (\ge 2-fold reduction) and of these 60% (n=27) were also reduced in susceptibility to DOR. Of the RPV-resistant viruses with a \ge 10-fold shift, thirteen (13/19; 68%) also conferred resistance to DOR.
L965: The common NNRTI resistance-associated substitutions (V106M, V108I, V179D, Y188C, Y188H, P236L) conferred less than 5-fold change in decreased susceptibility to DOR. Notably, however, the resistance-associated substitutions V106M and V108I emerged in RT of DOR-resistant HIV-1 variants selected in cell culture resistance selection experiments (Study Report PD003mk1439).
L966: Furthermore, the HIV-1 RT single amino acid substitutions V106A, Y181V, Y181C, G190S and Y188L had \ge 5-fold reduced susceptibility to DOR. The greatest reductions in susceptibility to DOR were in HIV-1 variants encoding V106A (7.1-28.0 FC values) or Y188L (95- to &gt;116 FC values). HIV-1 variants encoding a single NNRTI resistance-associated substitution, V106A or G190S, were cross-resistant to DOR and efavirenz; whereas, HIV-1 encoding the single NNRTI resistance-associated substitution of Y181V, Y181C, or Y188L were cross-resistant to DOR and efavirenz, etravirine, and rilpivirine.
L967: HIV-1 variants encoding a combination of K103N with L100I, Y181C, P225H, and/or V108I conferred reductions in susceptibility to DOR in the range of 2.0-27-fold change (Table 45). HIV-1 clinical isolates encoding a combination of Y181C with K101E, K103N, K103R, V108I, V108V/I, V179D and/or G190S conferred 2.0-18-fold decreased susceptibility to DOR. The highest levels of reduced susceptibility to DOR were observed in HIV-1 clinical isolates encoding a combination of Y188L + K103N (&gt;124-FC), Y188L + V106I (&gt;110-FC), and E138K + Y181C + M230L (&gt;111-FC). HIV-1 variants encoding these combinations of NNRTIs resistance-associated substitutions were cross-resistant to efavirenz, etravirine, and rilpivirine. HIV-1 isolates
L968: encoding the combination of V106A + G190A + F227 were highly resistant to DOR (&gt;106 FC values) and were cross resistant to efavirenz.

L973: ## 8.2. Clinical Virology
L974: ## 8.2.1. Methodology
L975: Protocol-defined virologic failure was defined by the sponsor in both Protocol 018 and Protocol 021 as:
L976: - Rebounder: A subject with confirmed (2 consecutive measures at least 1 week apart) HIV-1 RNA ≥50 copies/mL after initial response of HIV-1 RNA &lt;50 copies/mL at any time during the study;
L977: OR
L978: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L979: - Non-responder: A subject with confirmed (2 consecutive measures at least 1 week apart) HIV-1 RNA \ge200 copies/mL at Week 24 or Week 36 OR with confirmed (2 consecutive measures at least 1 week apart) HIV-1 RNA \ge50 copies/mL at Week 48.
L980: However, for the FDA resistance analyses, subjects were considered virologic failures if they had confirmed HIV-1 RNA \ge400 copies/mL after response of HIV-1 RNA &lt;50 copies/mL at any time during the study OR if they discontinued with HIV-1 RNA \ge400 copies/mL at or after Week 4.
L981: Testing for viral genotypic and phenotypic resistance to DOR and the ARVs used in each trial [DRV+r and the NRTIs used in the Trial 018 (FTC, TDF, 3TC, ABC); EFV, FTC, and TDF in Trial 021] was performed for subjects with protocol-defined virologic failure and for subjects who discontinued the trial for any reason. Testing was done on the sample from the viral failure confirmation visit or, if not available, from the early discontinuation visit, if the subject's HIV-1 RNA level was &gt;400 copies/mL. In some instances, samples from subjects with HIV-1 level &lt;400 copies/mL were mistakenly sent for resistance testing, or subjects with HIV-1 &gt;400 copies/mL did not have samples collected for such testing, due to site error. Post-baseline genotypic resistance to DOR was defined by the sponsor as any of the following substitutions in the reverse transcriptase (RT) gene: L100I, K101E, V106A, V106I, V106M, V108I, E138K, Y188L, G190A, G190S, H221Y, P225H, F227C, F227L, F227V, M230I, M230L, L234I, P236L, and Y318F.
L982: ## 8.2.2. Resistance Analyses in Treatment-Naïve Clinical Trials
L983: ## CLINICAL TRIAL 018
L984: In Trial 018, 21 subjects (21/383; 5.5%) in the DOR arm and 16 subjects (16/383; 4.1%) in the DRV/r arm were identified as meeting FDA virologic failure criteria by Week 48. Thus, the rates of virologic failure were similar between the DOR and DRV+r treatment groups. Of the 21 virologic failure subjects in the DOR arm, 7 had post-baseline resistance data with 2 of them having DOR genotypic resistance emergence (Table 46 and Table 47). Subject 103285 had NNRTI resistance-associated substitution E138G emerge as a mixture with other RT substitutions (V60I R83K, K173T, V245E, V293I) after viral load rebound at Week 48 and Subject 105159 had NNRTI resistance-associated substitutions V90I/V, V106I, H221Y, F227C and lamivudine/emtricitabine resistance substitution, M184V, emerge after discontinuation due to non-compliance at Week 24. The presence of the E138E/G mixture and other substitutions in Subject [REDACTED](b)(6) did not confer decreased susceptibility to DOR (FC= 0.98). However, in Subject [REDACTED](b)(6), the V90I/V, V106I, H221Y and F227C substitutions conferred &gt;97-fold decreased susceptibility to DOR and the M184V conferred resistance to emtricitabine (&gt;58-fold).
L985: In the DRV/r arm, 9 out of 16 subjects had post-baseline resistance data, but none of them showed genotypic or phenotypic resistance emergence to DRV/r, 3TC, FTC, ABC or TDF.

L999: ## CLINICAL TRIAL 021
L1000: In Trial 021, rates of virologic failure were comparable in the DOR and EFV treatment groups: 22 subjects (22/364; 6%) and 21 subjects (21/364; 5.8%), respectively, were identified as meeting FDA virologic failure criteria by Week 48 (confirmed &gt;400 copies/mL at discontinuation or at virologic failure timepoint \geWeek 4). Of the 22 virologic failure subjects in the DOR arm, 20 had post-baseline resistance data with 9 of them having evidence of DOR genotypic resistance emergence and 6 having phenotypic reduced susceptibility to DOR (Table 45 and Table 46):
L1001: - Subject (b) (6) had emergent E138G as a mixture at Week 36 with no decrease in DOR susceptibility (fold-change = 0.79).
- Subject (b) (6) had emergent Y188L at Week 48 with corresponding &gt;182-fold decrease in DOR susceptibility, emergent M184V with corresponding &gt;102-fold resistance to 3TC and emergent M41L with no change in TDF susceptibility.
- Subject (b) (6) had emergent Y318F as a mixture at Week 48 with no decrease in DOR susceptibility (fold-change = 0.35)
- Subject (b) (6) had emergent V90V/G, V106I, F227C at Week 24 with &gt;105-fold decreased DOR susceptibility and 3.1-fold change in 3TC susceptibility.
- Subject (b) (6) had emergent A98G, V106V/I, H221H/Y, P225P/L, and F227C at Week 24 with &gt;110-fold decrease in DOR susceptibility and emergent M184V with a decrease in 3TC susceptibility of &gt;116-fold
- Subject (b) (6) had emergent P225P/S and A355A/T mixtures at Week 48 with a 1.8-fold change in DOR susceptibility.
- Subject (b) (6) had emergent A98A/G, P225L and F227C with &gt;176-fold decrease in DOR susceptibility and emergent M184V with &gt;104-fold decrease in 3TC susceptibility.
- Subject (b) (6) had emergent A98A/G, V106A, P225H, and Y318Y/F with &gt;211-fold decrease in DOR susceptibility and emergent K65R with a 1.6-fold decrease in TDF susceptibility and 12-fold decrease in 3TC susceptibility.
- Subject (b) (6) had emergent V106M, V108V/I, and F227F/C/R with &gt;103-fold decrease in DOR susceptibility and emergent K65R/K mixture and M184V with &gt;97-fold decrease in 3TC susceptibility.

L1015: Of the 21 virologic failure subjects in the EFV arm, 20 had post-baseline resistance data with 12 of them having EFV phenotypic and/or genotypic resistance to EFV (Table 47 and Table 48). Of these 12 subjects, 10 had emergent K103N substitution, 1 had emergent K65R as a mixture with
L1016: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L1017: 0.8-fold change in TDF susceptibility, and 5 had emergent M184V and FTC resistance. In addition, 2 other subjects in the EFV arm had either emergent K103Q or baseline K101Q with no corresponding decrease in EFV susceptibility (1 to 1.5-fold).
L1018: In Trial 021, emergence of M184V or I was comparable between the DOR and EFV arms emerging in 42-44% of virologic failures (Table 46). The emergence of thymidine analog mutation substitutions (TAMS: M41L, D67N, or K70E) was also similar between the arms: one subject had emergent M41L in the DOR arm and one subject had emergent D67N/K70E in the EFV arm. Two subjects (22%) in the DOR arm had emergent K65R compared to 1 subject (8%) in the EFV arm. The predominant emergent NNRTI substitution in the EFV arm was the K103N substitution, which emerged in 83% (10/12) of the virologic failure subjects.
L1019: For Trials 018 and 021, rates of virologic failure to DOR were similar in both trials and were comparable with virologic failure rates in the comparator arms (Table 48). We also note that in Trial 018, 7/21 (33%) of the virologic failures had baseline viral loads &gt;100,000 in the DOR arm compared to 3/15 (20%) in the DRV/r arm and in Trial 021, 9/22 (41%) of the DOR virologic failures had baseline viral loads &gt;100,000 compared to 7/21 (33%) in the EFV arm.
L1020: However, the number of virologic failures with resistance data was higher in Trial 021 compared to Trial 018: (&gt;90% compared to 33-56%) (Table 48). In the DOR arms of both trials, there were 11 total virologic failures with evidence of resistance emergence; 2 subjects in Trial 018 and 9 subjects in Trial 021. Seven of the 11 virologic failures with evidence of genotypic resistance emergence to DOR had decreased susceptibility to DOR (&gt;97-fold to &gt;211-fold).

L1026: In Trials 018 and 021 combined, the NNRTI resistance substitutions that emerged in more than one of the DOR virologic failures included V90I/G, A98G, V106I/A/M, E138G, H221Y, P225H/L/S, F227C/R, and the novel Y318F substitution (Table 49). Two other NNRTI substitutions that emerged were V108I and Y188L. The A98G, V106I/A/M, P225H/L/S, F227C/R and Y318F substitutions emerged most frequently in the virologic failures.
L1027: Substitutions at V106, V108I, H221Y, F227 and Y318F were also selected in cell culture resistance selection experiments. The presence of the Y188L substitution conferred 95- to &gt;116-fold change in DOR susceptibility in cell culture. The other substitutions individually conferred a 3- to 10-fold change in DOR susceptibility in cell culture. However, in the virologic failures from the clinical trials, multiple substitutions emerged in combination which conferred &gt;90-fold decrease in DOR susceptibility. The data indicate that the combination of multiple NNRTI substitutions each conferring &lt;10-fold decreased DOR susceptibility can result in higher level DOR resistance of &gt;90-fold.

L1032: ## 8.2.3. Phenotypic Data
L1033: There were no screening/baseline phenotypic data for DOR of the responder subjects in Trial 018. In Trial 021, there was only screening/baseline phenotypic data for 23 responder subjects (6 of whom were in the DOR arm). The fold-change in DOR susceptibility for these subjects ranged from 0.29 to 3.69 with a median of 0.97 and mean of 1.05.
L1034: For the 20 virologic failures in Trial 021, the phenotypes ranged from 0.35 to &gt;211 with a median of 1.3. However, the mean and median fold-change in susceptibility to DOR of the 9 virologic failures with evidence of DOR genotypic and/or phenotypic resistance from Trial 021 was considerably higher at 90 and &gt;103, respectively (range 0.35 to &gt;211). Three of the 9 virologic failures who had only amino acid mixtures of NNRTI resistance substitutions showed doravirine phenotypic fold-changes of &lt;2-fold. However, the mean and median fold-change in susceptibility to DOR of the other 6 virologic failures with DOR phenotypic resistance was &gt;148 and &gt;143, respectively (range &gt;103 to &gt;211). All 6 of these virologic failures with resistance to DOR were also cross-resistant to EFV (&gt;3-fold) (Table 50).
L1035: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L1036: The mean and median fold-change in DOR susceptibility was &gt;141 and &gt;110, respectively, (range &gt;97 to &gt;211) from the 7 virologic failures with DOR genotypic and phenotypic resistance from both Trials 018 and 021. Given the large range in DOR phenotypic susceptibilities of the small number of virologic failures, there are insufficient clinical phenotypic data to determine a definitive phenotypic breakpoint for DOR resistance. The shifts in susceptibility were hundred-fold or greater for the virologic failures with genotypic changes at NNRTI resistance substitutions (V90I/G, A98G, V106I/A/M, E138G, H221Y, P225H/L/S, F227C/R) whereas the shifts were &lt;2-fold for the virologic failures with amino acid mixtures at NNRTI resistance sites.
L1037: Of the virologic failures with decreased susceptibility to DOR, 6/7 (86%) had resistance to EFV, 3/7 (43%) had decreased susceptibility to ETR, and 4/7 (57%) had decreased susceptibility to RPV.
