L1048: ## 8.2.4. Discrepancy in Resistance Data between Trials 018 and 021
L1049: We noted above that there were fewer DOR virologic failures with resistance data and resistance emergence in Trial 018 than in Trial 021. An examination of the virologic failures in Trial 018 showed that several subjects did not have post-baseline resistance testing performed even though viral loads were &gt;400 copies/mL. A request was sent to the sponsor for additional resistance information on 6 subjects: [REDACTED] (Week 60), [REDACTED] (Week 36 or 48), [REDACTED] (Week 24), [REDACTED] (Week 8), [REDACTED] (Week 16), and [REDACTED] (Week 8). The sponsor's response stated that Protocol 021 was the trial included in the original Investigational New Drug application for DOR/3TC/TDF (MK-1439A, IND 124,997), which was submitted on March 23, 2015. In the Agency's Study May Proceed letter dated April 24, 2015, the Agency requested that Merck revise Protocol 021 to include collection of samples at every visit for potential resistance testing if virologic failure occurs and is confirmed. Protocol 021 was amended accordingly, prior to initiation of the trial. However, since Protocol 018 was already ongoing and partially enrolled at that time, this change was not applied to Protocol 018. Therefore, per protocol, resistance samples were not collected at each study visit in Protocol 018 as they were in Protocol 021, which explains the discrepancy in the number of subjects with post-baseline resistance data between the 2 trials. No resistance samples were collected or available for Subject [REDACTED]. The resistance sample from Week 24 for Subject [REDACTED] was sent to [REDACTED] and analyzed after the database lock. This subject was sensitive to all drugs in the PhenoSense® assay and GenoSure® assay with no recognized resistance associated substitutions in RT or PR. In addition, as stated earlier, samples from subjects with HIV-1 level &lt;400 copies/mL were mistakenly sent for resistance
L1050: testing, or subjects with HIV-1 &gt;400 copies/mL did not have samples collected for such testing, due to investigational site error.
L1051: Thus, the lack of collection of resistance samples for multiple subjects in Trial 018 appears to be the explanation for the lower number of subjects with resistance data and resistance emergence in this trial.
L1052: ## 8.2.5. Summary of Virologic Failures in Phase 2 Trial 007
L1053: The DOR resistance emergence data from Phase 2 Trial 007, showing emergence of NNRTI resistance substitutions V106I, E138G and F227C, support the data obtained in the two Phase 3 trials.
L1054: There were 3 virologic failure subjects from Trial 007 summarized below:
L1055: - Subject (b) (6) was randomized to the doravirine 25 mg group. At screening and baseline, HIV-1 RNA was 93,083 and 47,556 copies/mL and CD4+ cell count was 128 and 110 cells/mm3, respectively. HIV-1 RNA was &lt;40 copies/mL at Week 16. At Week 24, HIV-1 RNA was 498 copies/mL. The subject was determined to have protocol-defined virologic failure (PDVF) (rebound) at Week 36 and Week 72 (time of resistance test). At Week 36, HIV-1 RNA was 17,814 copies/mL (VF confirmation). The subject was discontinued from study medication at Week 60 (last laboratory visit was at Week 72) due to non-compliance with study medication (the subject did not return his study medication and completed diaries). On resistance testing, the following genotypic resistance substitutions were found at Week 36: K101K/E and P236P/L. No genotypic resistance substitutions were found for emtricitabine or tenofovir. No evidence of phenotypic decrease in susceptibility to any study medication was noted.
- Subject (b) (6) was randomized to the doravirine 25 mg group. At screening and baseline, HIV-1 RNA was 95,653 and 123,695 copies/mL, and CD4+ cell count was 613 and 716 cells/mm3, respectively. HIV-1 RNA was &lt;40 copies/mL at Weeks 8, 12, 16, and 24. At Week 36, HIV-1 RNA was 663 copies/mL and approximately 1 month later (also recorded as Week 36) was &lt;40 copies/mL. At Week 48, HIV-1 RNA was 44 copies/mL. The subject was determined to have PDVF (rebound) at Week 60 (time of resistance test). At Week 60, HIV-1 RNA was 533 copies/mL (VF confirmation). The subject completed the trial and reported being 100% compliant with study medication. On resistance testing, the following genotypic resistance substitutions were found: V106V/I and F227C and M184V (emtricitabine-associated substitution). No genotypic resistance substitutions were found for tenofovir. There was an approximately 66-fold decrease in susceptibility to doravirine and a greater than 91-fold decrease in susceptibility to emtricitabine noted with no evidence of phenotypic decrease in susceptibility to tenofovir.
L1056: - Subject (b) (4) was randomized to the doravirine 100 mg group. At screening and baseline, HIV-1 RNA was 209,326 and 111,344 copies/mL, and CD4+ cell count was 320 and 304 cells/mm3, respectively. A V179D genotypic resistance substitution was found at screening. The subject was determined to have PDVF (non-response) at Week 24 (time of resistance test). At Week 24, HIV-1 RNA was 46,213 copies/mL (VF confirmation). The subject was discontinued from study medication at Week 24 due to non-compliance with study medication. On resistance testing (at study medication discontinuation; Week 24), the following genotypic resistance substitutions were found: V179D and E138E/G and A62V (emtricitabine- and tenofovir-associated substitution). No evidence of phenotypic decrease in susceptibility to doravirine, emtricitabine, or tenofovir was noted.
L1057: ## 8.2.6. Clinical Virology Summary
L1058: - DOR is an NNRTI with nM activity against HIV-1 (WT, K103N, Y181C) (12-33 nM) across clades.
- The NNRTI substitutions V106A/I/M, V108I, H221Y, F227L/C, M230I, L234I, P236L and novel NNRTI substitution Y318F were selected in cell culture selection experiments with DOR.
- DOR-resistant isolates are cross-resistant with the other NNRTIs.
- There were 11 virologic failures from Clinical Trials 018 and 021 who had emergent genotypic and phenotypic resistance.
- Multiple resistance-associated substitutions emerged and conferred high-level DOR resistance (&gt;100-fold).
- A98G, V106I/A/M, P225H/L/S, F227C/R and Y318F emerged most frequently in the virologic failures.
- Most DOR-resistant failures were cross-resistant to efavirenz and about half were resistance to etravirine and rilpivirine.
L1059: ## 8.2.7. Post-Marketing Commitment
L1060: There is one post-marketing commitment (PMC) for the sponsor to analyze the phenotype of Y318F in cell culture.
L1061: - Assess the phenotypic susceptibility in cell culture of DOR and approved nonnucleoside reverse transcriptase inhibitors (NNRTIs) against Y318F alone and in combination with the following substitutions, which have been associated with DOR and/or other NNRTIs: K103N; Y181C; K103N/Y181C; L100I; L100I/K103N; V106A; P225H; V106A/P225H; H221Y; V106M; F227C; V108I.

The criteria for FDA resistance analysis subsets are subjects with confirmed HIV-1 RNA \ge 400 copies/mL after response of HIV-1 RNA &lt;50 copies/mL at any time during the study or subjects who discontinued with evaluable HIV-1 RNA (\ge 400 copies/mL) at or after Week 4.
L1066: The FDA-negotiated labeling in Section 12.4 is shown below:
L1067: ## **Mechanism of Action**
L1068: Doravirine is a pyridinone non-nucleoside reverse transcriptase inhibitor of HIV-1 and inhibits HIV-1 replication by non-competitive inhibition of HIV-1 reverse transcriptase (RT). Doravirine does not inhibit the human cellular DNA polymerases \alpha, \beta, and mitochondrial DNA polymerase \gamma.
L1069: ## **Antiviral Activity in Cell Culture**
L1070: Doravirine exhibited an EC_{50} value of 12.0 \pm 4.4 nM against wild-type laboratory strains of HIV-1 when tested in the presence of 100% normal human serum (NHS) using MT4-GFP reporter cells. Doravirine demonstrated antiviral activity against a broad panel of primary HIV-1 isolates (A, A1, AE, AG, B, BF, C, D, G, H) with EC_{50} values ranging from 0.6 nM to 10.0 nM.
L1071: ## **Antiviral Activity in Combination with other HIV Antiviral Agents**
L1072: The antiviral activity of doravirine in cell culture was not antagonistic when combined with the NNRTIs delavirdine, efavirenz, etravirine, nevirapine, or rilpivirine; the NRTIs abacavir, didanosine, emtricitabine, lamivudine, stavudine, tenofovir DF, zidovudine; the PIs darunavir or indinavir; the gp41 fusion inhibitor enfuvirtide; the CCR5 co-receptor antagonist maraviroc; or the integrase strand transfer inhibitor raltegravir.
L1073: ## **Resistance**
L1074: ## ***In Cell Culture***
L1075: Doravirine-resistant strains were selected in cell culture starting from wild-type HIV-1 of different origins and subtypes, as well as NNRTI-resistant HIV-1. Observed emergent amino acid substitutions in RT included: V106A, V106M, V106I, V108I, H221Y, F227C, F227I, F227L, F227V, M230I, L234I, P236L, and Y318F.
L1076: ## ***In Clinical Trials***
L1077: In the doravirine treatment arms of the DRIVE-FORWARD and DRIVE-AHEAD trials (n=747), 11 subjects showed the emergence of doravirine-associated resistance substitutions in their HIV among 28 subjects (39%) in the resistance analysis subset (subjects with HIV-1 RNA greater than 400 copies per mL at virologic failure or early study discontinuation and having resistance data). Emergent doravirine resistance-associated substitutions in RT included one or more of the following: A98G, V106I, V106A, V106M/T, V108I, E138G/K, Y188L, H221Y, P225H, F227C, F227C/R, and Y318Y/F. Seven of 11 subjects with emergent doravirine-associated resistance

## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L1079: substitutions showed doravirine phenotypic resistance and all of them had at least a 100-fold reduction in doravirine susceptibility (range &gt;97- to &gt;211-fold reduction in doravirine susceptibility). The other 4 virologic failures who had only amino acid mixtures of NNRTI resistance substitutions showed doravirine phenotypic fold-changes of less than 2-fold. Of the 28 subjects in the resistance analysis subset, 8 subjects (29%) developed genotypic and/or phenotypic resistance to the other drugs (abacavir, lamivudine, emtricitabine, or TDF) in the regimens of the DRIVE-FORWARD and DRIVE-AHEAD trials. The resistance-associated substitutions that emerged were RT M41L (n=1), A62V (n=1), K65R (n=2), T69T/A (n=1) and M184V (n=5).

L1081: ## Cross-Resistance
L1082: A panel of 96 diverse clinical isolates containing NNRTI-associated substitutions was evaluated for susceptibility to doravirine. Clinical isolates containing the Y188L substitution alone or in combination with K103N or V106I, V106A in combination with G190A and F227L, or E138K in combination with Y181C and M230L showed greater than 100-fold reduced susceptibility to doravirine.
L1083: Cross-resistance has been observed among NNRTIs. Treatment-emergent doravirine resistance-associated substitutions can confer cross resistance to efavirenz, rilpivirine, nevirapine, and etravirine. Of the 7 virologic failures who developed doravirine phenotypic resistance, all had phenotypic resistance to nevirapine, 6 had phenotypic resistance to EFV, 4 had phenotypic resistance to rilpivirine, and 3 had partial resistance to etravirine based on the Monogram PhenoSense assay.

L1092: Cross-resistance has been observed among NNRTIs. Treatment-emergent doravirine resistance associated substitutions can confer cross resistance to efavirenz, rilpivirine, nevirapine, and etravirine. Of the 6 virologic failures who developed doravirine phenotypic resistance, all had phenotypic resistance to EFV and nevirapine, 4 had phenotypic resistance to rilpivirine, and 3 had partial resistance to etravirine based on the Monogram PhenoSense assay.
L1093: ## 9. Review of Safety
L1094: The benefit risk assessment for both DOR as a single agent and the DOR/3TC/TDF FDC was established through 1) 48 week data from the two Phase 3 trials PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, 2) 96 week data from the supporting Phase 2 trial PN007, 3) relative bioavailability studies showing similar DOR, TDF, and 3TC PK when administered individually or as part of DOR/3TC/TDF (PN014, PN015, and PN026), and 4) Safety Update Report (SUR) data.
L1095: ## 9.1. Safety Review Approach
L1096: Data from two Phase 3 trials in treatment-naïve HIV-1 infected subjects (PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD) were analyzed both individually and pooled for the DOR-containing arms to support safety for DOR 100 mg QD and for DOR/3TC/TDF FDC tablet QD. In general, the safety review was analyzed by trial, except in situations where pooling the data might help identify less common serious signals (discontinuations due to AEs and biliary SAEs). Pooling of the Phase 3 trials for the DOR-containing arms is appropriate because the trial designs were similar with generally similar inclusion and exclusion criteria, except that PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD had an extra inclusion criterion for hemoglobin above a certain threshold and an extra exclusion criterion for advanced or decompensated liver disease. In addition to a complete independent analysis of safety for the Phase 3 trials, selected safety analyses from the Phase 2 trial PN007 are included in this review when applicable.
L1097: Clinical trial data were independently analyzed in JMP, JMP Clinical, MAED, and JReview. All the safety assessments and conclusions are those of the FDA reviewer unless otherwise specified. Certain safety tables exported from JReview include calculated percentages with decimal places; therefore, some percentages may be different compared to labeled safety information which rounds to whole numbers.
L1098: The Applicant submitted a SUR four months after the original NDA submission providing approximately 10 and 5 months' additional safety data for PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD (data cutoff date 7/14/2017) and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD (data cutoff date 8/21/2017), respectively: no new PN007 safety data were included. Approximately five months' additional safety data were also provided for the ongoing trials PN024, PN028, and PN030 (data cutoff date 8/21/2017):
L1099: - PN024: Phase 3 trial evaluating a switch from a boosted PI, NNRTI, or integrase inhibitor based regimen to DOR/3TC/TDF for 48 weeks in virologically-suppressed subjects with HIV-1 infection. Subjects randomized 2:1 to an immediate or delayed (Week 24) switch.
- PN028: Phase 2b trial evaluating a switch from EFV/FTC/TDF to DOR/3TC/TDF in virologically-suppressed, HIV-1 infected subjects for a 36 week total duration. Subjects randomized 1:1 to an immediate or deferred (Week 36) switch.
- PN030: Phase 2 trial evaluating the safety and efficacy of DOR/3TC/TDF in antiretroviral treatment-naïve subjects with HIV-1 infection with selected NNRTI-transmitted resistance mutations.
L1100: Deaths, SAEs, and discontinuations due to AEs reported in the SUR are included in the relevant safety sections.
L1101: ## Statistical Testing of Pre-Specified Safety Outcomes
L1102: Both PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD measured safety-related endpoints, some of which were pre-specified for statistical hypothesis testing. For PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, the primary safety endpoints were changes in LDL-C and non-HDL-C from baseline at Week 48, both included in the testing hierarchy. Secondary safety endpoints include changes in total cholesterol, triglycerides, and HDL-C from baseline at Week 48 and discontinuations from the trial due to AE by Week 48.
L1103: For PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, the primary safety endpoints were the proportion of subjects that experience AEs by Week 48 in the categories of dizziness, sleep disorders and disturbances, and altered sensorium. All three were included in the testing hierarchy.
L1104: Key secondary safety endpoints for PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD were changes in LDL-C and non-HDL-C from baseline at Week 48, also included in the testing hierarchy along with the primary safety endpoints. Other secondary safety endpoints include:
L1105: - Proportion of subjects that experience AEs in the categories of depression and suicide/self-injury as well as psychosis and psychotic disorders by Week 48
- Changes in total cholesterol, triglycerides, and HDL-C from baseline at Week 48
- Discontinuations from the trial due to adverse event by Week 48
L1106: PN007 also pre-specified the proportion of subjects that experience at least one neuropsychiatric AE by Weeks 8 and 24 as testable endpoints.
L1107: Both confirmatory trials conducted both safety-related interim analyses. In PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, the external Data Monitoring Committee (eDMC) conducted a pre-specified safety-related interim analysis to assess the incidence of neuropsychiatric AEs in the DOR and DRV+r treatment groups. The applicant stated that no adjustments for Type I error for the safety-related endpoints are necessary because the safety-related interim analysis would not determine termination of the study. Nevertheless, the applicant allocated an alpha level of 0.00001 to the interim analyses. The eDMC for PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD conducted three safety reviews, which are considered interim analyses. Once again, the applicant stated that results of the interim analyses would not determine termination of the study. Nevertheless, the applicant allocated an alpha level of 0.00001 for each interim analysis, for a total allocation of 0.00003 for safety-related interim analyses.
L1108: Treatment differences in the changes from baseline for the lipid endpoints were modeled separately using an analysis of covariance model (ANCOVA) that adjusted for actual treatment and baseline lipid value. A conclusion of statistical significance (superiority) was reached if the
L1109: p-value generated by the ANCOVA model was less than 0.04999 in PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and 0.04997 in PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, respectively.
L1110: The 95% CIs and p-values for the proportion of subjects that experienced the neuropsychiatric AEs were calculated using Miettinen and Nurminen's method. For each pre-specified category, a conclusion of statistical significance (superiority) was reached if the p-value generated by Miettinen and Nurminen's method was less than 0.04997.
L1111: The significance levels were adjusted for interim analyses conducted during the course of the trials.
L1112: In PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, the lipid hypotheses were tested sequentially in order to avoid multiplicity. The LDL-C hypothesis was tested first and the non-HDL-C hypothesis was tested only if superiority was found for the LDL-C hypothesis.
L1113: In PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, the efficacy and safety hypotheses were tested sequentially and the testing of a hypothesis depended on rejecting the null hypotheses for all preceding hypotheses. According to the trial protocol for PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, the hypotheses were tested in the following order:
L1114: - Primary efficacy hypothesis of NI of DOR/3TC/TDF to EFV/FTC/TDF
- Primary safety hypothesis for dizziness
- Primary safety hypothesis for sleep disorders and disturbances
- Primary safety hypothesis for altered sensorium
- Primary safety hypothesis for LDL-C
- Primary safety hypothesis for non-HDL-C
- Secondary efficacy hypothesis of superiority of DOR/3TC/TDF to EFV/FTC/TDF
L1115: The safety endpoints that were tested were all pre-specified for statistical testing in the trial protocols for PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD. In addition, the sequential order of testing the endpoints provides control of the Type I error rate. Therefore, it is considered appropriate from a statistical perspective to test the safety endpoints related to the AEs of dizziness, sleep disorders and disturbances, and altered sensorium as well as LDL-C and non-HDL-C.
L1116: ## 9.2. Review of the Safety Database
L1117: ## Overall Exposure
L1118: A total of 1657 subjects have received at least 1 dose of DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF, including 855 HIV-1 infected subjects with treatment initiated at the proposed 100 mg daily dose in either the Phase 3 trials (PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD) or in the supportive Phase 2 trial (PN007). See Table 52.

L1124: ## Relevant characteristics of the safety population
L1125: The studied populations adequately reflect the types of patients for which the Applicant is seeking an indication for DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF, namely treatment-naïve HIV-1 infected adults. The safety population includes males and females, multiple races, Hispanic and non-Hispanic ethnicities, 29 nationalities, and ages ranging from 18 to 70 years old.
L1126: Please refer to Section 7.2.3 for a description of the demographics of the subjects enrolled in the Phase 3 trials. Safety analyses by demographic subgroup are shown in Section 9.6.
L1127: ## Adequacy of the safety database
L1128: Overall, the safety database of over 1500 subjects is comprehensive and adequate to assess the safety of DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF for the proposed indication, dosage regimen, duration, and patient populations. The 855 subjects in the safety database who received the intended DOR dose for 24 to 48 weeks, exceeds the 300 to 500 subjects, referenced in the FDA Guidance for Industry on *HIV-1 Infection: Developing Antiretroviral Drugs* (https://www.fda.gov/downloads/drugs/guidancecomplianceregulatoryinformation/guidances/ucm355128.pdf), recommended for initial approval in treatment-naïve HIV-1 infected adult patients.
L1129: ## 9.3. Adequacy of Applicant's Clinical Safety Assessments
L1130: ## Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
L1131: Data integrity and quality of the initial submission in combination with subsequent submissions were adequate to perform the safety review for DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF. The OCS Jump Start service also analyzed data fitness and found no major issues that would preclude performing a safety review. We did note missing ECG data, missing ALT values from most subjects enrolled in Australia, and missing MedDRA coding for several AEs; however, these issues were resolved early in the review and did not impact our overall safety assessment.
L1132: ## Categorization of Adverse Events
L1133: The Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities (MedDRA) versions 19.0 (PN007) and 19.1 (PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, ISS) were used for AE coding. Other than issues identified in the Sponsor's TEAE definition and those concerning Merck's AE toxicity grading scale (discussed below), there were no identified issues with respect to recording, coding, and categorizing AEs. The Applicant categorized SAEs in accordance with standard, regulatory definitions and grouped AEs using the standard MedDRA hierarchy. The Applicant's translations of verbatim terms to preferred terms for the events reported in PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, and PN007 were reviewed and found to be acceptable. Laboratory toxicities were graded according to the DAIDS Table for Grading the Severity of Adult and Pediatric Adverse Events Version 1.0, December, 2004; Clarification August 2009.
L1134: **TEAEs** were defined as any AE that occurred from the time the first dose of study treatment was taken until 14 days following cessation of study treatment. Cessation of study treatment relied on review of treatment diaries and, using the Sponsor's definition, AEs occurring &gt;14 days after treatment diaries were last reviewed were counted as "post-study", even if the subject was presumed to still be on treatment.
L1135: - For example, two deaths that occurred &gt;14 days after the last study visit were counted as "post-study" and not included as TEAEs, even though the two subjects were presumed to still be taking study medication at the time of death.
L1136: This safety review utilizes a modified TEAE definition that defines the last day of study
L1137: treatment as the earliest of the following: 1) the day of death, 2) the last day of study treatment if confirmed by the subject at a discontinuation study visit, or 3) the day the subject would have run out of study treatment if fully compliant after the last study visit. Using this modified method adds a total of 22 TEAEs: 3 to PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, 12 to PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, and 7 to PN007. Thus, while some numbers from our safety analyses may be slightly different than the numbers provided in the Applicant's analyses, the differences are very small and do not change the overall safety conclusions.
L1138: Merck's AE toxicity grading scale uses a three-line grading table with the following toxicity grades:
L1139: - Mild (Grade 1): awareness of signs or symptoms, but easily tolerated
- Moderate (Grade 2): discomfort enough to cause interference with usual activity
- Severe (Grade 3): incapacitating with inability to work or do usual activity
L1140: All AEs in the reviewed trials were graded using this same scale, and so are comparable within the trials; however, Merck's AE grading scale has fewer toxicity grades than the toxicity grading scales used by most other companies, which have 4-5 toxicity grades. Consequently, AEs of a certain toxicity grade in these DOR-containing trials are not comparable to AEs of the same toxicity grade reported in trials performed by other companies.
L1141: ## Routine Clinical Tests
L1142: Routine clinical evaluations for safety included medical history taking for assessment of symptoms of adverse events, vital sign measurements and physical examinations for assessment of signs of adverse events, and laboratory evaluations. In the Phase 2 study PN007, ECGs were also performed at screening, Week 24, and Week 48. In the pivotal trials PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, key evaluations were performed at baseline, Week 2 (PN018 only), and Weeks 4, 8, 16, 24, and every 12 weeks thereafter, with a final follow-up visit 14 days post-study. Follow-up visits were also scheduled for subjects who discontinued treatment prematurely due to adverse events.
L1143: ## 9.4. Safety Results
L1144: Each Safety Results subsection presents the Phase 3 population results: events included in PN007 or the SUR are also discussed briefly in each subsection, as appropriate. For all analyses, subjects who experienced the same TEAE on more than one occasion are counted only once, at the highest toxicity grade reported. When a "total" value is included for a column, it represents the total number of subjects included in the analysis, rather than the total number of events. Safety analysis of AEs leading to discontinuation were defined as any AE that began within the Week 48 treatment period and led discontinuation, regardless of whether the discontinuation occurred within or outside the Week 48 treatment period. This definition is different from the statistical definition in Table 26 which only includes subjects who discontinued within the Week 48 treatment period.
L1145: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L1146: An overall summary of the Phase 3 TEAEs through 48 weeks is presented in Table 53. Reports of any AE and drug-related AEs occurred more frequently in the EFV/FTC/TDF group (91% and 63%, respectively) compared with the DOR, DRV/r, and DOR/3TC/TDF groups (78-83% and 31-32%, respectively). In addition, more AEs leading to discontinuation were reported in the EFV/FTC/TDF group (7%) compared with the DOR, DRV/r, and DOR/3TC/TDF groups (2-3%). Differences between treatment groups will be assessed more critically in the safety subsections to follow.

L1152: ## Deaths
L1153: Five deaths total were reported among randomized and treated subjects in the two Phase 3 studies in the initial NDA submission, and no deaths were reported in the supportive Phase 2 study PN007. PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD reported one death in the DOR group. PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD reported four deaths using the modified TEAE definition: one in the DOR/3TC/TDF group and three in the EFV/FTC/TDF group. Brief summaries for each of the cases are provided below: all deaths were considered unrelated to study medication.
L1154: ## PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD
L1155: - Subject (b) (6) (DOR plus TDF/FTC, 'Death' Day 222) was a 41-year-old man with medical history including HIV-1, hypertension, headaches, non-epileptic seizures, pulmonary tuberculosis, active tobacco use. Baseline CD4 412 cells/mm3, HIV-1 RNA 45,801 copies/mL. On Day 222 while at home watching TV, he reportedly felt dizzy,
L1156: collapsed, and died. Ongoing AEs at the time of death, all considered unrelated by the investigator, were four months of mild diarrhea, cough, and leg cramps (CPK normal throughout). No autopsy performed. At last study visit (Day 173) CD4 500 cells/mm3, HIV-1 RNA 73 copies/mL, and abnormal laboratory values were mildly low Hgb (12.4 mg/dL), WBC (3.1 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L}), neutrophil (1.28 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L}), and eosinophil (0.01 \times 10^3/\mu\text{L}). *Of note, a more detailed description of this case than in the NDA narrative was provided under IND 112796 SDN 148, which was the source of some of the details presented.*
L1157: ## **PN021, DRIVE-AHEAD**
L1158: - **Subject** (b) (6) (DOR/3TC/TDF, 'Death' Day 87) was a 48-year-old man with medical history including HIV-1, bronchitis, gastritis. Baseline CD4 134 cells/mm3, HIV-1 RNA 189,820 copies/mL. At last study visit (Day 58), undetectable HIV-1 RNA, CD4 109 cells/mm3, Grade 1 elevated alkaline phosphatase, AST, ALT, and decreased magnesium (1.0 mEq/L). The investigator was concerned about the CD4 drop and wanted the subject to start trimethoprim sulfamethoxazole prophylaxis, but the subject was on vacation and unable to return to clinic. The reported last dose of drug was Day 71, although the subject was presumed to still be on study medication at the time of death Day 87. An autopsy was not done, and no other details of the death are known.
- **Subject** (b) (6) (EFV/FTC/TDF, 'Death' Day 58) was a 28-year-old man with medical history including HIV-1, allergic dermatitis, psychotic disorder. Baseline CD4 363 cells/mm3, HIV-1 RNA 10,375 copies/mL. On Day 58 found unconscious at home with a needle nearby and a venipuncture site in his left upper limb. He was pronounced dead in the ER: no autopsy was done.
- **Subject** (b) (6) (EFV/FTC/TDF, 'Completed suicide' Day 337) was a 32-year-old man with medical history including HIV-1, AIDS, hydrocephalus, cerebral palsy, hemiplegia, ventriculo-peritoneal shunt, epilepsy. Baseline CD4 182 cells/mm3, HIV-1 RNA 648,359 copies/mL. The subject committed suicide Day 337. The subject had experienced traumatic events (friend's death and job loss) prior to the suicide.
- **Subject** (b) (6) (EFV/FTC/TDF, 'Injury' Day 384) was a 40-year-old man with medical history including HIV-1, syphilis. Baseline CD4 199 cells/mm3, HIV-1 RNA 223,262 copies/mL. On Day 384, he died of injuries sustained in a road accident.
L1159: In the SUR five additional Phase 3 deaths were reported, two occurring in DOR-treated subjects (PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD), two occurring in DRV/r-treated subjects (PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD), and one occurring in an EFV-treated subject (PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD).
L1160: - One DOR-treated subject (b) (6) was a 43-year-old man with medical history including HIV-1, diabetes, hypertension who died Day 653 from complications of leptospirosis; one DOR-treated subject (b) (6) was a 59-year-old man with medical history including HIV-1, AIDS, hypertension who died Day 683 from myocardial infarction.
- Causes of death in the comparator arms were pulmonary embolism (Day 451, DRV/r), Hodgkin's disease (Day 755, DRV/r), and 'natural causes' (Day 494, EFV).
L1161: In the ongoing trials PN024, PN028, and PN030 one death was reported in the PN024 immediate switch group. This subject was found dead at home on Day 166: cause of death was
L1162: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L1163: unknown; however, overdosing on illicit drugs could not be excluded. No autopsy information was provided, and a death certificate was not available. The subject received the last dose of study medication Day 84. The investigator considered the death unrelated to study medication.
L1164: Reviewer Comment: The three deaths in the subjects on EFV/FTC/TDF seem to be clearly unrelated to treatment, with the possible exception of the suicide which may have been exacerbated by EFV/FTC/TDF use. The two deaths in the subjects on DOR unfortunately do not have enough information to determine a cause of death, given the lack of autopsy. Subject [REDACTED](b) (6) may have died of an acute stroke or heart attack, which would fit with his cardiac risk factors of hypertension, smoking, and HIV infection. Of note, DOR did not have a noticeable effect on ECGs in PN007, nor did a supratherapeutic dose of DOR lead to QT prolongation in the thorough QT study. Subject [REDACTED](b) (6), whose CD4 count dropped shortly before death despite an undetectable viral load, may have had an undiagnosed infection. However, there is no clear pattern with these deaths that point to a specific safety concern, and the mortality rate of subjects on DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF is similar to the mortality rates of the subjects on the active comparators: the additional SUR deaths in DOR-treated subjects do not alter this assessment.
L1165: ## Serious Adverse Events
L1166: SAEs were seen in about 4-5% of subjects receiving DOR in both PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD through Week 48, which was similar to the rates in the active comparator groups. The only SAE seen in more than one subject receiving DOR in a single trial was anogenital warts, which was seen in two DOR subjects in PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD. SAEs summarized by Body System Organ Class are shown in Table 54 for PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, and in Table 55 for PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD. The most frequently reported Body System Organ Class with SAEs was infections and infestations, with approximately 2% of subjects in both the DOR groups and the active comparator groups having SAEs in this class. This is similar to other HIV development programs and is likely due to an HIV-infected population that may be more susceptible to infections due to either immunosuppression or high risk behaviors.

L1173: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L1174: Data Source: PN021 subset of the ISS ADSL and ADAE datasets
L1175: Only one DOR-recipient in each study had an SAE that was judged to be related to study treatment by the investigator. Causality assessment of both these SAEs are confounded by timing and comorbidities, and neither is convincing for a causal association. In the first case, the SAEs of nausea and vomiting occurred three days after a single dose of study medication, and in the setting of preexisting acute kidney injury of unclear etiology. In the second case, the SAEs of asthenia, nightmare, and insomnia occurred eight months after initiating study treatment, and in the setting of preexisting depression. These two cases are described below:
L1176: ## **PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD**
L1177: - **Subject (b) (6) (DOR + TDF/FTC, SAE nausea and vomiting)** was a 49-year-old man with medical history including HIV-1. Baseline CD4 544 cells/mm3, HIV-1 RNA 663 copies/mL. The subject received one study drug dose because he was found to have acute kidney injury on predose labs (creatinine 2.76 mg/dL), and thus discontinued for safety reasons. On Day 4 developed nausea and vomiting, judged related to study medication, and Day 6 was hospitalized for continued nausea and vomiting, acute kidney injury, and hypovolemia. These AEs resolved within a few days except for acute kidney injury, classified as resolving at the Day 9 discontinuation visit (creatinine 1.88 mg/dL).
L1178: ## **PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD**
L1179: - **Subject (b) (6) (DOR/3TC/TDF, SAEs asthenia, nightmare, insomnia)** was a 38-year-old woman with medical history including HIV-1, depression, renal disorder, hypertension, and hypercholesterolemia. Baseline CD4 442 cells/mm3, HIV-1 RNA 13,356 copies/mL. On Day 265 experienced severe asthenia, nightmare, and insomnia, judged related to study medication. Creatinine increased from baseline 0.78 mg/dL to 1.00 on Day 169, associated with moderate renal disorder and vitamin D deficiency. The investigator assessed asthenia and vitamin D deficiency as caused by renal insufficiency worsened by TDF use, and on Day 272 the subject discontinued study medication.
L1180: Similar to the Phase 3 trials, no PN007 SAEs were reported in more than one subject in the same treatment arm. Compared to the Phase 3 trials, more subjects overall in PN007 had SAEs [10% (11/108) in subjects initially randomized to DOR 100 mg, and 12% (13/108) in subjects initially randomized to EFV]; however, PN007 data were for 96 weeks of treatment rather than 48 weeks in the Phase 3 trials, which likely explains this discrepancy. No SAEs in PN007 DOR-treated subjects were judged related to study medication.
L1181: In the SUR no additional Phase 3 drug-related SAEs were reported. In the ongoing trials PN024, PN028, and PN030, drug-related SAEs were reported in four PN024 DOR-treated subjects in the immediate switch group: these cases report AEs which are proposed for labeling (depression, asymptomatic increased lipase, asymptomatic increased AST/ALT) or lack information to assess causality (pancreatitis).
L1182: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L1183: *Reviewer Comment: Overall, SAEs in the Phase 3 trials, PN007, and the SUR do not raise significant safety concerns as there were no trends or clustering of SAEs identified in individual trials.*
L1184: ## Dropouts and/or Discontinuations Due to Adverse Effects
L1185: Rates of discontinuation due to AEs were low in the Phase 3 trials, with 1.6% of subjects on DOR versus 3.1% of subjects on DRV/r discontinuing treatment due to AEs in PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, and 3.0% of subjects on DOR/3TC/TDF versus 6.6% of subjects on EFV/FTC/TDF discontinuing treatment due to AEs in PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD. Within the combined DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF treatment groups from the two trials, no AEs leading to discontinuation were reported in more than one subject except for nausea/vomiting, abdominal pain (or abdominal pain upper), fatigue/asthenia, rash, and renal disorders (see Table 56). However, even for these five types of AEs, rates of discontinuation were &lt;1% in the combined DOR groups, and similar to or less than rates of discontinuations for these AEs in the active comparator groups. In addition, with the exception of rash described further under Section 9.5 (subsection on Rash and Hypersensitivity Reactions), the day of onset for these five AEs among subjects who discontinued treatment did not follow a consistent pattern.

L1195: PN007 DOR-treated subjects who discontinued due to AEs discontinued for similar AEs. Within

In the ongoing trials PN024, PN028, PN030 14 subjects discontinued due to AEs, all in PN024: ascites/alcoholic hepatitis, increased ALT (2), hepatic enzyme increased, decreased appetite, hepatitis, fatigue/sleep disorder, rash, myalgia/generalized edema/rash/renal pain, memory impairment, pancreatitis (SAE discussed above), depression (SAE discussed above), amylase/lipase increased (SAE discussed above), TB pleurisy, endocarditis.
L1198: *Reviewer Comment: Fatigue/asthenia, nausea, abdominal pain, and rash are recommended to be included in the DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF Section 6 labeling. Hallucinations, a component of psychosis and psychotic disorders, and depression and suicide are also both recommended for inclusion in the label as part of the proposed PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD NPE assessment. Other AEs leading to discontinuation in DOR treatment groups have likely alternative etiologies based on timing or comorbidities.*
L1199: ## Significant Adverse Events
L1200: Significant AE analysis in the Phase 3 trials and PN007 evaluated the following AE terms classified as Designated Medical Events (DME) which potentially reflect serious medical conditions that may be related to drugs: acute pancreatitis, acute respiratory failure, agranulocytosis, anaphylaxis or anaphylactoid reaction, aplastic anemia, blindness, bone marrow depression, deafness, disseminated intravascular coagulation, hemolytic anemia, liver failure, liver necrosis, liver transplant, pancytopenia, renal failure, seizure, Stevens-Johnson syndrome, torsades de pointes, toxic epidermal necrolysis, thrombotic thrombocytopenic purpura, and ventricular fibrillation. Potential DMEs were analyzed both by SMQ analysis and by preferred term.
L1201: Among 133 TEAEs that could be loosely classified as one of the above terms by a narrow SMQ search in all treatment groups, 71% were mild in severity and 85% were considered not related to study treatment. None of these terms was seen disproportionately in the DOR groups. The following AEs occurring in DOR-treated subjects are described in more detail.
L1202: - **PN007 Subject**(b) (6) (DOR 100 mg, 'Deafness') experienced an SAE of moderate acute hearing loss Day 218 associated with dizziness. Inpatient diagnostic work up included an MRI, audiologist evaluation, EEG, and did not identify an etiology. Study treatment was not interrupted, no additional treatment was administered, and the subject fully recovered in two weeks. The subject completed the study Day 672 and the event was not considered related to study treatment by the investigator.
L1203: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L1204: - **PN007 Subject**(b) (6) (DOR 100 mg, 'Epilepsy') with a history of epilepsy experienced two AEs of moderate epileptic seizure, one on Day 222 and one on Day 290, each lasting 5 minutes and considered not related to study treatment. Study treatment was not interrupted for either. The subject was later lost to follow up with last day of confirmed treatment on Day 590.
L1205: *Reviewer Comment: The frequency and descriptions of these AEs, including resolution without study treatment discontinuation, do not raise concern for a causal relationship with DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF. Routine postmarketing pharmacovigilance is recommended for continued assessment of potential DOR safety signals.*

L1227: *Reviewer Comment: Merck's toxicity grading scale differs from the scales used by most other companies in that it only has 3 grades as compared to 4-5. In addition, Merck's toxicity scale consists of three sentences defining mild, moderate, and severe for all AEs, whereas other toxicity grading scales contain 15 pages of detailed grading criteria separated by specific AE. Due to these differences, I suspect that some AEs that would have been grade 2 by a different scale were counted as grade 1 by Merck's scale. Evidence of this is seen when looking at the grade 2 or higher ADRs with an incidence \ge 2.0\% for DRV/r. In PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, diarrhea is the only ADR that met these criteria in the DRV/r group, whereas in the DRV/r label in a trial in a similar population, there were seven ADRs that met these criteria in the DRV/r group (abdominal pain, diarrhea, nausea, vomiting, anorexia, headache, and rash). For this reason, and also to provide a more useful label for clinicians, the ADR table should include ADRs of all grades reported in \ge 5\% of any treatment group.*
L1228: ## Laboratory Findings
L1229: A summary of the Grade 1-4 laboratory abnormalities reported during Week 0 to 48 of the Phase 3 trials is shown below. Table 61 shows the chemistry laboratory toxicities, and Table 62 shows the hematology laboratory toxicities for PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD. Laboratory findings recommended for DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF labeling are denoted by shading, and those with &gt;2\% increase in DOR-containing arms versus comparator arms are bolded. For most parameters, laboratory findings are similar between the DOR and comparator groups, and Grade 3 and 4 laboratories are uncommon. Laboratory analyses did not reveal any imbalances apart from Grade 1 and 2 bilirubin elevations in DOR-treated subjects. In addition, fewer graded fasting lipid elevations occurred in DOR-treated subjects versus comparators. Please see Section 9.5 (subsections on Lipid Analysis and Hepatobiliary Analysis) for further analyses regarding differences in graded total bilirubin and fasting lipid abnormalities between treatment groups. The label inclusion rationale for liver enzymes, creatinine, creatine kinase, and lipase is to provide general consistency with other approved HIV product labels.

L1239: ## Vital Signs
L1240: Vital signs were assessed at least every 12 weeks in Phase 3 trials, and more frequently at study initiation. There was a trend towards a small increase in weight over time in DOR and comparator treatment groups, consistent with known data about the effect of HIV treatment. No clinically relevant pattern of changes in other vital signs (systolic and diastolic blood
L1241: pressure, pulse, respiratory rate, temperature) were identified in DOR or comparator treatment groups across the Phase 3 trials or in the supportive Phase 2 trial.
L1242: ## Electrocardiograms (ECGs)
L1243: ECGs were only done at baseline in PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD. In the supportive Phase 2 study PN007, ECGs were done at screening, Week 24, and Week 48. There were no ECG abnormalities that increased in frequency between baseline, Week 24, and Week 48. There were also no clinically significant trends over time or dose response in ECG parameters including HR, QT, QTcF, QTcB, QRS interval, and PR interval.
L1244: ## QT
L1245: A thorough QT study (PN017) was conducted in 44 healthy subjects randomized to single doses of placebo, 400 mg moxifloxacin, or 1200 mg DOR (12X the indicated dose). The QT-IRT team from the Division of Cardiovascular and Renal Products reviewed study PN017 and concluded that no significant QTc prolongation effect of 1200 mg DOR was detected.
L1246: ## Immunogenicity
L1247: Because DOR is a small molecule, immunogenicity issues were not anticipated and not specifically addressed during the clinical trials. Similarly, both TDF and 3TC, the other components of DOR/3TC/TDF, are small molecules and immunogenicity issues are not anticipated.
L1248: ## 9.5. Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
L1249: The following adverse events and laboratory abnormalities of special interest were reviewed because these were either prespecified by the Sponsor as specific safety analyses, because these issues were identified as safety issues with other NNRTIs, or because they were identified by numerical imbalances in Section 9.4 analyses:
L1250: - Neuropsychiatric events
- Changes in fasting lipid values
- Hepatobiliary analysis
- Rash and hypersensitivity reactions
- Alopecia
L1251: ## Neuropsychiatric Analysis
L1252: A detailed neuropsychiatric analysis was performed to evaluate the potential for neuropsychiatric adverse reactions from DOR because neuropsychiatric events (NPE) have been identified as a safety signal for other NNRTIs, particularly with EFV. This section summarizes the following analyses: 1) PN007 pre-specified NPE endpoints; 2) PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD pre-specified NPE endpoints; 3) review of additional PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD and PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD NPEs. The results from these analyses support inclusion of PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD NPE Week 48 analysis in DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF labeling.
L1253: ## Review of PN007 Pre-Specified Neuropsychiatric Events
L1254: The Phase 2 trial PN007 pre-specified the proportion of subjects experiencing at least one NPE by Weeks 8 and 24 for statistical testing of superiority as one of the primary objectives reviewed in Section 7.2.1. The following selected MedDRA preferred terms were pooled and categorized as a NPE (defined as CNS event in the protocol): depression, nightmare, confusional state, suicidal ideation, nervous system disorder, psychotic disorder, abnormal dreams, suicide attempt, acute psychosis, delirium, depressed level of consciousness, hallucination, hallucination auditory, hallucination visual, completed suicide, suicidal behavior, major depression, depressed mood, depressive symptom, insomnia, disturbance in attention, somnolence, dizziness, or concentration impaired.
L1255: Table 63 presents the proportion of subjects that experienced at least one NPE by Weeks 8 and Week 24 in Part 2 of PN007. The observed treatment difference is -20.4% for both endpoints, which favors DOR 100 mg. The p-values for both endpoints are less than 0.05, indicating statistically significantly lower proportions of subjects in the DOR 100 mg arm that experience at least one NPE by Weeks 8 and 24, compared to the EFV 600 mg arm. These data were used to help inform the pre-specified PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD NPE analyses.

L1259: ## Review of PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD Pre-Specified Neuropsychiatric Events
L1260: PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD included NPEs as a pre-specified co-primary endpoint with the hypothesis that DOR/3TC/TDF is superior to EFV/FTC/TDF as measured by the subject proportion with all-cause and all grade NPEs by Week 48 (superiority tested within category, sequentially, in the order indicated below):
L1261: - Dizziness
- Sleep Disorders and Disturbances
- Altered Sensorium
L1262: NPEs of Depression and Suicide/Self-Injury and Psychosis and Psychotic Disorders were predefined secondary endpoints. Section 9.1 provides PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD pre-specified NPE analysis statistical methodology. All five NPE categories were defined by MedDRA groupings and included DAVP EOP2 meeting input (see Appendix Table 147). NPEs were elicited by open-
L1263: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L1264: ended questioning to mirror clinical practice (NDA 210807 Response to FDA Information Request, received 1/9/2018).
L1265: PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD NPE analyses were conducted in the FAS, per subjects' actual treatment. Nevertheless, all subjects received his/her randomized treatment. Please see Table 25 and Table 26 for PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD demographics and baseline disease characteristics of the FAS.
L1266: As shown in Table 64, the proportion of subjects that experienced each of the Week 48 endpoints was lower in the DOR/3TC/TDF arm compared to the EFV/FTC/TDF arm. For the pre-specified categories of dizziness, sleep disorders and disturbances, and altered sensorium, the analyses produced two-sided p-values less than 0.04997. Therefore, a statistically significantly lower proportion of subjects experienced dizziness, sleep disorders and disturbances, and altered sensorium in the DOR/3TC/TDF arm compared to the EFV/FTC/TDF arm. In addition, a lower proportion of subjects experienced depression and suicide/self-injury and psychosis and psychotic disorders in the DOR/3TC/TDF arm compared to the EFV/FTC/TDF arm.

L1308: ## **PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD**
L1309: For completeness, similar Week 48 analyses were performed for PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD using the same PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD predefined NPE category MedDRA PTs. Neuropsychiatric AEs were not a pre-specified analysis for PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD. Overall there was an approximate 10% increase in overall NPEs in the PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD DOR arm (24%) compared with the PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD DOR arm (13%), with similar trends generally observed in the individual NPE categories: NPEs overall and by individual category were similar between the PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD DOR- and DRV/r-containing arms as expected (Table 68). We hypothesize the increased PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD DOR arm NPEs is because of increased AE reporting in the EFV-containing protocol.

L1314: ## Neuropsychiatric AE Analysis Conclusion
L1315: The detailed neuropsychiatric analyses support inclusion of PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD NPE Week 48 data in DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF labeling. The Phase 2 PN007 trial NPE data do not substantively add to the NPE information presented from PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, and therefore these data are not recommended for labeling.
L1316: It is recommended to include subject percentages with one or more NPE overall and for each NPE category, and to include the treatment difference and CI information in labeling for the NPE categories prespecified for statistical testing (i.e., Dizziness, Sleep Disorders and Disturbances, and Altered Sensorium). A decision was reached with the Division Director and signatory authority to include p-values in labeling for the three NPE categories pre-specified for statistical testing based on the following rationale which is also considered acceptable by the Clinical Reviewer:
L1317: - the three NPE categories (Dizziness, Sleep Disorders and Disturbances, and Altered Sensorium) were pre-specified co-primary endpoints for statistical testing
- the FDA Guidance for Industry: *Clinical Studies Section of Labeling for Human Prescription Drug and Biological Products — Content and Format* (2006) states a “confidence interval and a p-value provide complementary information, and both should usually be provided when describing uncertainty of the treatment effect. A confidence interval provides a better numerical description of the uncertainty of the treatment effect and provides some information about its size. A p-value better conveys the strength of the finding (i.e., how likely it is that the observed treatment effect is a chance finding). However, it is generally better not to use a p-value alone”.
L1318: While the NPE categories reflect safety information which is generally conveyed in the Adverse Reactions section of labeling, these categories nevertheless reflect a pre-specified clinical outcome and thus it is reasonable to extend the above Clinical Studies Section of Labeling guidance to this situation.
L1319: From a statistical perspective, the inclusion of p-values in the labeling for the three NPE categories prespecified for statistical testing is not recommended for the following reasons:
L1320: - P-values are tools of statistical inference that do not account or incorporate clinical benefit into its inference. Therefore, the p-values cannot be used to determine the clinical benefit of the observed treatment differences.
- P-values are used for making decisions regarding the statistical significance of a result. However, p-values are not designed to influence clinical decision-making at the patient level. Therefore, the p-values should not be used by prescribers to determine treatment choices.
- While there is a FDA guidance regarding the Clinical Studies section of labeling (mentioned above), there is no FDA guidance that discusses the use of p-values in the Adverse Reactions section of labeling.
L1321: ## Lipid Analysis
L1322: Detailed lipid analyses were performed to evaluate the effect of DOR-containing therapy on fasting serum lipids. Assessing the superiority of DOR-containing therapy to the relevant comparator arms (i.e., DRV/r-containing therapy or EFV/FTC/TDF) by Week 48 fasting serum LDL-C and non-HDL-C mean change from baseline were Phase 3 trial prespecified hypotheses. Superiority of DOR-containing therapy over the comparator arm was assessed using ANCOVA models to compare the treatment groups, adjusted for baseline fasting lipid level and regardless of use of lipid lowering agents before or during the trial. Week 48 sensitivity analyses, which were not prespecified, excluded subjects on baseline lipid-lowering agents, and subjects initiating lipid-lowering therapy post-baseline had their last fasted on-treatment valued carried forward.
L1323: Across the Phase 3 trials, baseline lipid-lowering therapy use was 3% and on-treatment lipid-lowering therapy initiation was 1% (Table 69).
L1324: Table 69: Baseline and On-Treatment Lipid-Lowering Therapy, Week 48

L1327: ## Statistical Analysis
L1328: Both PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD included lipid-related endpoints in its testing hierarchy. Both trials assessed the statistical superiority of Week 48 change from baseline in 1) LDL-C and 2) non-HDL-C in the DOR-containing treatment arm compared to the active control arm. The trials also assessed the Week 48 change from baseline in total cholesterol, triglycerides, and HDL-C, although these endpoints were not pre-specified for statistical testing.
L1329: The original lipid analyses in PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD did not include all subjects in the FAS population. It only included subjects who had baseline and Week 48 data after implementing last observation carried forward (LOCF) for those with missing Week 48 data and those who modified lipid-lowering therapy during the trial.
L1330: Each exclusion criteria limits the number of subjects eligible for inclusion into the lipid analyses. (Refer to Table 144 in Section 15.6 for the number of subjects that meet the inclusion criterion and all preceding criteria for the LDL-C and non-HDL-C endpoints.) The exclusion of subjects without baseline observations or who were using lipid-lowering agents at baseline created a modified ITT population. A modified ITT population still benefitted from randomization as such exclusions were measured and determined at baseline. However, the exclusion of subjects with missing Week 48 observations is a post-baseline exclusion. Table 144 indicates a difference in the number of subjects who were in the FAS with baseline observations and not using lipid-lowering agents at baseline and the number of subjects who fit those criteria but who also have Week 48 observations. The difference indicates the presence of post-baseline exclusions. Therefore, the lipid analyses may not benefit from the protections that randomization provides.
L1331: At a Pre-NDA meeting with the applicant, the agency requested a sensitivity analysis for all lipid-related hypotheses that exclude subjects who were either on a statin or another lipid-modifying agent at baseline, or who started a statin or another lipid-modifying agent during the study, to be submitted with both NDAs. In the sensitivity analyses, subjects on lipid-lowering therapy at baseline are excluded and subjects that initiate lipid-lowering therapy post-baseline
L1332: have their last measurement prior to the start of the lipid-lowering therapy carried forward. The results of the sensitivity analyses are included in Section 6 of the applicant's proposed labeling. While the sensitivity analyses may be similar to the pre-specified lipid analyses, the sensitivity analyses were not pre-defined in the testing hierarchy.
L1333: Review of the distributions of the baseline characteristics found that they appear to be well-balanced between the DOR-containing arm and the active control arm in both trials. In addition, the distributions appear to be similar in the FAS population and the labeling population for all treatment arms. The findings do not suggest any measured confounding but they do not guarantee balanced distribution of all variables between treatment arms within the trials. (Refer to Table 145 and Table 146 in Section 15.6 for further information on baseline characteristics for the FAS and labeling populations.)
L1334: Table 68 depicts the results of both the original lipid analyses, which included subjects with baseline and Week 48 lipid measurements, as well as the sensitivity lipid analyses, which excluded subjects in the original analysis who used lipid-lowering therapy at baseline. The reviewer's analyses produced results consistent with the applicant's results. In the original analyses for PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, the treatment difference for all lipid endpoints except HDL-C was negative, favoring DOR over DRV+r. In the original analyses for PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, the treatment difference for all lipid endpoints except HDL-C was negative, favoring DOR/3TC/TDF over EFV/FTC/TDF. For both trials, the observed treatment differences for HDL-C were negative, favoring the active control arms. The one-sided p-values for the LDL and non-HDL hypotheses were less than 0.02499 in both trials. Therefore, subjects in the DOR arm experienced a statistically significantly larger decrease in LDL and non-HDL cholesterol, compared to subjects in the DRV+r arm. Likewise, subjects in the DOR/3TC/TDF arm experienced a statistically significantly larger decrease in LDL and non-HDL cholesterol, compared to subjects in the EFV/FTC/TDF arm. Of note, the clinical benefit of these findings has not been demonstrated.
L1335: Table 70 depicts the results of the sensitivity analyses in italics. These results are almost identical to the results of the original analyses for all lipid endpoints in both trials. Although the sensitivity analyses were not included in the hypothesis testing hierarchy, they produce the same information as the original analyses.

L1345: ## Lipid Analysis Conclusion
L1346: The detailed lipid analyses support inclusion of PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD Week 48 lipid data in DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF labeling. It is recommended to include baseline mean and mean change from baseline for LDL, non-HDL, cholesterol, triglycerides, and HDL values, and to include the treatment difference and CI information in labeling for the lipid categories prespecified for statistical testing (i.e., LDL, non-HDL). A decision was reached with the Division Director and signatory authority to include p-values in labeling for the two lipid categories pre-specified for statistical testing, based on the rationale applied to the NPE analyses, which is also considered acceptable by the Clinical Reviewer. For the statistical rationale for not including p-values please refer to the NPE analyses above.
L1347: It is also recommended to include Grade 3 lipid information in Section 6 labeling. As presented in Table 61, Grade 3 fasting serum lipid values were approximately &lt;1%, &lt;1% and &lt;1% for cholesterol, &lt;1%, 2.8% and 1.6% for LDL, &lt;1%, &lt;1% and 2.5% for triglycerides for the combined Phase 3 DOR arms, DRV/r arm, and EFV arm, respectively, in the Week 48 analysis.
L1348: ## Hepatobiliary Analysis
L1349: A detailed hepatobiliary analysis was performed to evaluate the potential for hepatotoxicity from DOR because hepatotoxicity has been identified as a safety signal for other NNRTIs. This section summarizes the following analyses: 1) review of hepatic laboratory abnormalities, including total bilirubin and an overview of potential drug-induced liver injury and Hy's Law cases; 2) review of hepatobiliary AEs; and 3) hepatic events in subjects coinfected with Hepatitis B or C.
L1350: ## Review of Hepatic Laboratory Abnormalities, Bilirubinemia
L1351: As noted in the section on laboratory findings (Table 61) rates of graded total bilirubin abnormalities were higher in the DOR group (6.5%) versus the comparator group (1.3%) in PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD (treatment difference: 5.2%, 95% CI: 2.4%, 8.3% by the exact method), and also higher in the DOR group (6.6%) versus the comparator group (&lt;1%) in PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD (treatment difference: 6.3%, 95% CI: 3.8%, 9.3% by the exact method). This bilirubinemia was primarily unconjugated, not associated with graded abnormalities in other
L1352: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L1353: liver tests (ALT, AST, or alkaline phosphatase), and not associated with treatment discontinuation.
L1354: As shown in Table 69, 95% of subjects with graded total bilirubin abnormalities had a maximum toxicity grade of 1 or 2, and 62% had graded abnormalities isolated to one study visit. The two grade 4 abnormalities had plausible alternative etiologies; acute hepatitis A virus infection (EFV/FTC/TDF) and bile duct stone (DOR/3TC/TDF). Accounting for relative frequency of study visits and blood draws, the graded total bilirubin abnormalities occurred throughout the study without an apparent temporal pattern in relation to start of therapy. Subjects with graded total bilirubin abnormalities from either the DOR or comparator treatment groups in the Phase 3 trials were disproportionately male (1/233 [&lt;1\%] female versus 54/1261 [4.3%] male subjects): the clinical relevance of this observation is unclear.

L1357: Graded events by isolated (1 study visit only), transient (more than one study visit but separated by time and not at consecutive visits) and sustained (2 or more consecutive study visits). *Transient and sustained grade 2 events had a maximum toxicity grade of 2, with transient or sustained events that were an increased grade from baseline but not necessarily all grade 2.
L1358: Mean bilirubin levels beginning 2 weeks after starting treatment were higher in subjects receiving DOR than in subjects receiving DRV/r (difference of ~0.1 mg/dL) or EFV (difference of ~0.2 mg/dL); however, this finding appears to be due to mean bilirubin levels decreasing after treatment initiation in the DRV/r and EFV groups, rather than mean bilirubin levels increasing after treatment initiation in the DOR groups. Analysis of bilirubin levels in subjects who received different daily doses of DOR in PN007 did not show any dose association with bilirubin levels.
L1359: No subjects met the complete definition for drug induced liver injury1 (DILI) in the Phase 3 trials or in PN007. Four subjects met DILI laboratory criteria, two DOR-treated subjects (PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD) and two EFV-treated subjects (PN007, PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD); however, all had alternative explanations for the increases in liver enzymes (2 had acute HCV infection and 2 had acute hepatitis A virus infection).
L1360: ## Review of Hepatobiliary AEs and Biliary AEs
L1361: TEAEs through Week 48 in the hepatobiliary disorders body system class in PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD were initially reviewed. Rates of Week 48 hepatobiliary disorder system organ class (SOC) AEs were low and similar between treatment groups [&lt;1\% (2/383) among both DOR and DRV/r recipients in PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD; 1.1% (4/364) versus &lt;1\% (3/364) among DOR/3TC/TDF and EFV/FTC/TDF recipients, respectively, in PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD]. However, across trials there was a numerical difference in biliary SAEs (5 in the pooled DOR group, versus 0 in the active comparator groups). Consequently, a specific biliary AE analysis to fully evaluate any potential biliary signals was conducted, using the following preferred terms to define a biliary AE: bile duct stone, biliary colic, biliary dyskinesia, cholecystitis, cholecystitis acute, and cholelithiasis. The safety pool was expanded to include the Phase 2 study PN007 and to include TEAEs that occurred after 48 weeks.
L1362: The identified biliary AEs are described in Table 70. DOR-treated subjects accounted for 8 of 10 subjects with biliary AEs, all five subjects with biliary SAEs, all six subjects with moderate to severe biliary AEs, and all five subjects requiring cholecystectomy. It is notable that at least one DOR-treated subject had a biliary SAE in each trial. None of the eight DOR-treated subjects with biliary TEAEs had graded bilirubin abnormalities except one subject with a bile duct stone (who had a single elevated bilirubin level of 12.3 at the time the stone was diagnosed), and none were considered related to study drug by the investigator.
L1363: 1 Hy's Law refers to the observation made by Dr. Hy Zimmerman that drug induced hepatocellular injury (i.e., aminotransferase elevation) accompanied by jaundice had a mortality of 10-50%. Hepatocellular injury sufficient to impair bilirubin excretion has been used by the FDA to identify drugs likely to cause severe liver injury. The definition used by the FDA as an indicator of clinical concern for drug-induced liver injury (DILI) includes the following: ALT or AST &gt; 3\times upper limit of normal (ULN), total bilirubin &gt; 2\times ULN without an initial increase in alkaline phosphatase, and no other explanations for the increases in liver enzymes (for example, viral hepatitis, pre-existing or acute liver disease, or another drug capable of causing the observed injury).
L1364: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR

L1368: A consult from the Division of Applied Regulatory Science (DARS) addressed the following question: Based on its structure and the structure of its metabolites, and its mode of elimination, what are plausible mechanisms whereby DOR might cause biliary AEs? The DARS consult concluded that no potential mechanism for DOR hepatotoxicity could be identified, and noted the following:
L1369: - DOR chemical structure evaluation did not reveal any features associated with hepatotoxicity.
- Although DOR is primarily metabolized by CYP3A4 in the liver, it does not significantly inhibit or induce any of the CYPs, nor does it significantly affect several hepatic transporters responsible for biliary events.
- Case analysis from the FDA Adverse Event Reporting System showed that NNRTIs were the least associated with hepatobiliary and gallbladder AEs compared to PIs and NRTIs, and that several of the co-medications used with DOR in the trials were significantly associated with hepatobiliary and gallbladder adverse events.
L1370: An information request to the Applicant requested a comprehensive assessment of the biliary AE findings, including epidemiologic, pharmacologic, and nonclinical data. The Applicant concluded the current available data for DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF do not suggest a relationship between DOR and biliary AEs and noted the following:
L1371: - Preclinical animal toxicity data do not suggest a relationship between DOR and biliary toxicity.
- Biliary excretion is not a significant pathway of elimination for DOR or its metabolites. DOR in vitro inhibition profile indicates increases of plasma unconjugated bilirubin via DOR inhibition of UGT1A1 or OATP1B1/3 are unlikely.
- Among subjects who experienced biliary AEs there was no evidence of elevated DOR PK, the times of onset were heterogeneous in relation to DOR initiation, most had predisposing risk factors including Hispanic ethnicity, obesity, hydrochlorothiazide use, and hyperlipidemia, and all had normal bilirubin levels prior to the biliary AE.
- Epidemiologic data suggest that the rates of gallbladder disease in the trials are similar to the background rate in the general US population
L1372: ## Hepatic Events in Subjects Coinfected with Hepatitis B or C
L1373: Hepatitis B virus (HBV) and hepatitis C virus (HCV) co-infections can make HIV-infected patients more susceptible to drug-related or other liver toxicities. There were only 44 total HBV or HCV co-infected subjects enrolled in the Phase 3 trials (21 subjects on DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF). Although the number of co-infected subjects is small, the data from these subjects do not indicate that treatment with DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF increases the risk of hepatic events in subjects co-infected with HBV or HCV. There were no hepatobiliary AEs reported in HBV or HCV co-infected DOR-treated subjects in the Phase 3 trials other than ALT increased and AST increased (reported in 4/21 subjects). Of the HBV or HCV co-infected subjects, none of the 21 DOR-treated subjects had AST or ALT toxicity grades higher than Grade 2, whereas 3 of 14 DRV/r-treated subjects had Grade 3-4 ALT +/- AST, and 1 of 9 EFV-treated subjects had a Grade
L1374: 3 ALT. No HBV or HCV co-infected subject had graded increased bilirubin laboratory toxicities from baseline.
L1375: ## Hepatobiliary Analysis Conclusion
L1376: The totality of information regarding hepatobiliary analysis supports including all graded total bilirubin laboratory information in the DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF labels. Total bilirubin abnormalities were seen more frequently in the DOR group versus the comparator group in both PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD. In addition, Grade 2-4 ALT, AST, and alkaline phosphatase laboratory information is recommended to provide general consistency with other approved HIV product labels. No additional labeling is recommended for hepatobiliary events.
L1377: - Regarding bilirubin laboratory analysis, in general bilirubinemia in DOR-treated subjects was Grade 1 or 2, not sustained, and not associated with treatment discontinuations or abnormalities in other liver laboratories.
- Regarding biliary AE analysis, overall observed rates of biliary AE in the clinical trials were low and similar to US population background rates. In addition, there is no preclinical suggestion of DOR-associated biliary toxicity, and the chemical structure of DOR does not suggest a mechanism whereby DOR might cause biliary toxicity.
L1378: Routine postmarketing pharmacovigilance is recommended for continued assessment of hepatobiliary events associated with DOR use.
L1379: ## **Rash and Hypersensitivity Reactions**
L1380: A detailed analysis of rash and hypersensitivity reactions was performed to evaluate the potential for skin reactions from DOR because rash and severe skin and hypersensitivity reactions have been identified as safety signals for other NNRTIs. Of note, the active comparator labels include WARNINGS AND PRECAUTIONS rash information.
L1381: ## *Rash Events*
L1382: Rash events were analyzed by pooling the preferred term of “drug eruption” and the preferred terms that contained the word “rash”, as specified in the table legend below, and similar to how rash events were pooled in the dolutegravir label.
L1383: DOR recipients in PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD had similar rates of overall rash events and rash ADRs (8% and 2%) as DRV/r recipients (9% and 3%), but DOR/3TC/TDF recipients in PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD had one third the rate of rash events and rash ADRs (6% and 2%) as EFV/FTC/TDF recipients (18% and 12%, Table 71). No DOR-treated subject had a rash SAE, and there were no reported severe drug eruptions such as Stevens-Johnson Syndrome, toxic epidermal necrolysis, or DRESS syndrome (Drug Reaction with Eosinophilia and systemic symptoms). Two DOR recipients in PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD discontinued treatment due to a rash event. One subject developed a severe, generalized, macular rash Day 13. Study treatment was stopped the next day, and the rash resolved by Day 19. The second subject developed a moderate, generalized, erythematous rash Day 4 treated with loratadine and diphenhydramine. The rash did not resolve, study treatment was stopped on Day 149, and the rash had not resolved by Day 163.
L1384: A longer median rash onset time was observed in the DOR groups (median 38-40 days) versus the active comparator groups (median 11-13 days); however, rash events in both the DOR and active comparator groups usually developed within 50 days of treatment initiation.

L1389: ## *Hypersensitivity Reactions*
L1390: Hypersensitivity reactions in PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD were analyzed using the MAED tool hypersensitivity SMQ narrow search; however, most identified hypersensitivity reaction events were rash events [64% (132/205)], including 88% (14/16) of hypersensitivity reaction ADRs in DOR-treated subjects. The two non-rash hypersensitivity reaction ADRs in DOR-treated subjects were not suggestive of a causal DOR relationship: 1) moderate urticaria in PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD Day 11-19 with negative rechallenge; and 2) mild allergic dermatitis in PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD Day 19-26 without study treatment interruption. No DOR-treated subjects reported angioedema or erythema multiforme or had a hypersensitivity SAE. One DOR-treated subject experienced a severe non-rash hypersensitivity reaction AE to concomitant medication (Levaquin).
L1391: ## *Rash and Hypersensitivity Reaction Analysis Conclusions*
L1392: Rash events occurred at similar rates in the DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF groups compared to the DRV/r group, but at substantially lower rates than the EFV/FTC/TDF group. Rash ADRs occurred &gt;5% among Phase 3 trial subjects in the EFV/FTC/TDF group; therefore, rash is recommended for DOR and DOR/3TC/FDC Section 6 ADVERSE REACTIONS labeling.
L1393: No specific hypersensitivity reaction events were identified in Phase 3 DOR-treated subjects: rash events comprised most identified hypersensitivity reaction narrow SMQ AEs.
L1394: Because no SAEs due to rash or hypersensitivity have been reported with DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF to date, neither rash or hypersensitivity reaction is currently recommended for the WARNINGS AND PRECAUTIONS section of the label. Routine postmarketing pharmacovigilance is recommended for continued assessment of rash and hypersensitivity associated with DOR use.
L1395: ## Alopecia
L1396: Because alopecia is an unusual AE for ARVs, and one subject discontinued DOR/3TC/TDF due to a moderate alopecia considered related to study treatment that subsequently resolved off treatment, alopecia was reviewed in more depth. Eight DOR-treated subjects (1%, 8/747) reported alopecia versus three subjects in the combined comparator groups (&lt;1%, 3/747; Table 72). Among the DOR-treated subjects with alopecia, four subjects (50%) had moderate or severe alopecia, three subjects (38%) had alopecia considered related to study treatment, six subjects (75%) had alopecia that did not completely resolve while still on study treatment, and one subject discontinued treatment due to the alopecia. Onset ranged Day 1 to 371, with no clear temporal pattern.
L1397: In response to an IR, the Applicant provided narratives for the subjects with alopecia and an assessment of whether alopecia was related to DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF use. The Applicant concluded alopecia was not related to DOR-use because there was no preclinical safety signal, there was lack of a temporal pattern, alopecia resolved or was resolving in 3/8 subjects while still on DOR-containing treatment, and may reflect background rates.
L1398: The totality of the information regarding alopecia including the low frequency of events and lack of a temporal pattern does not support additional DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF labeling at this time. Routine postmarketing pharmacovigilance is recommended for continued assessment of alopecia events associated with DOR use.
