L1403: ## 9.6. Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
L1404: Safety analyses by gender, race, ethnicity, and age (&lt; or ≥65 years of age) were performed for safety events among DOR recipients through 48 weeks in PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, and subjects originally randomized to 100 mg DOR in PN007. Of note, only 5 DOR recipients were ≥65 years of age, which compromised a meaningful interpretation of AE findings by age. No concerning patterns were identified in the safety analyses by race, gender, ethnicity, or age. The following findings are described in more detail because they were identified either in the context of specific safety analyses (graded bilirubin abnormalities) or because of numerical differences among demographic subgroups (dizziness, cardiovascular disorders):
L1405: - Graded total bilirubin abnormalities were observed more frequently in male versus female subjects, as described previously in Section 9.5 (subsection on Hepatobiliary Analysis).
- The TEAE of dizziness was reportedly disproportionately in Asian subjects both in the combined DOR group and the combined EFV group. The majority (207/251, 82.5%) of subjects with a TEAE of dizziness reported a maximum toxicity grade of 1 (mild).In the combined DOR group, 12/56 (21.4%) Asian versus 6/131 (4.6%) black versus 33/455 (7.3%) white subjects reported dizziness.
- In the combined EFV group, 41/62 (66.0%) Asian versus 17/76 (22.4%) black versus 78/231 (33.7%) white subjects reported dizziness.
- Of note, Asian subjects did not have higher DOR exposures versus other races to explain this difference.
- In Section 2.7.4 of the NDA submission (Summary of Clinical Safety), the Applicant reported that there was a higher proportion of AEs in the Cardiovascular Disorders system organ class (SOC) in Asian subjects compared to other races, for both the combined DOR group and combined EFV group.
L1406: - Among Asian subjects in the Phase 2 and 3 trials, 7% (5/71) in the combined DOR group and 3% (2/67) in the EFV group reported cardiovascular disorder TEAEs. These events in DOR-treated Asian subjects were mild [palpitations (3), sinus bradycardia, tachycardia] and none were SAEs, considered related to study treatment, or led to discontinuation.
- Among white and black or African American subjects in the Phase 3 trials, 1% (7/543) and 2% (3/169) in the combined DOR group and 1% (3/257) and 2% (2/85) in the EFV group reported cardiovascular TEAEs, respectively.
L1407: While there is a numerical increased proportion of cardiovascular disorder SOC events reported in Asian DOR-treated subjects, the totality of the available information including small numbers, mild and non-serious nature of the reported TEAEs, and lack of higher DOR exposures in Asian subjects versus other races does not support additional DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF labeling at this time. Routine postmarketing pharmacovigilance is recommended for continued assessment of any cardiovascular events associated with DOR use.
L1408: ## 9.7. Specific Safety Studies/Clinical Trials
L1409: No additional trials have been conducted to evaluate specific safety concerns.
L1410: ## 9.8. Additional Safety Explorations
L1411: ## Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
L1412: Clinical trial findings do not suggest the potential of DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF for carcinogenicity. Most of the neoplasms in PN007, PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD among DOR-treated subjects were benign cutaneous neoplasms, and all identified malignancies were reported as not related to study drug. Three cases of squamous cell carcinoma (skin, tongue, anal), one case of basal cell carcinoma, two cases of lymphoma (B-cell, Hodgkin's disease), and one case of Kaposi's sarcoma were reported among DOR-treated subjects, which have been associated with HIV infection. In the SUR three additional malignancies were reported in PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD and PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD DOR-treated subjects: prostate cancer, squamous cell carcinoma (anal) and basal cell carcinoma. Nonclinical toxicology studies identified an imbalance in thyroid adenoma and carcinoma at the highest dose (7 times the DOR AUC exposure at the RHD) in a 2-year rat carcinogenicity study (see Section 5 for further details): no thyroid adenomas or carcinomas were reported in the Phase 2 or 3 DOR-containing trials.
L1413: ## Pregnancy
L1414: As stated in Section 5, DOR exposure was not associated with effects on fertility and DOR maternal exposure was not associated with any adverse effects on pre- and postnatal development. In pregnant rabbits, skull malformations were observed at DOR exposure approximately 8.5 times the projected clinical exposure: these malformations were not considered test-article related, and this finding was not observed in the rat reproductive toxicology studies.
L1415: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L1416: Women who were pregnant and/or lactating were excluded from enrollment into DOR-containing clinical trials; however, six subjects became pregnant during the Phase 2/3 trial period and discontinued due to pregnancy. The outcomes for these pregnancies were: a vaginal live birth with no congenital anomalies for two pregnancies (DOR 50 mg group in PN007, DOR group in PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD); spontaneous abortion considered not drug related for three pregnancies (1 in the DOR group in PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD; 1 in the DOR/3TC/TDF group in PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, 1 in the EFV/FTC/TDF group in PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD); and pending (pregnancy ongoing) for 1 pregnancy, (EFV/FTC/TDF group in PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD).
L1417: During the SUR period, there were five additional pregnant subjects. In PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD, three subjects (1 subject in the DOR treatment group and 2 in the DRV/r treatment group) were pregnant. The subject in the DOR treatment group discontinued Day 589, following an elective abortion on Day 554. In PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, two subjects (both in the DOR/3TC/TDF group) discontinued due to pregnancy: one subject discontinued Day 327 (pregnancy outcome is pending), and one subject discontinued Day 493 (pregnancy outcome was a spontaneous abortion on Day 507; the investigator considered this outcome not related to study drug or study procedure).
L1418: No adequate and well-controlled trials of DOR have been conducted in the pregnant population, and no adequate human data are available to establish whether or not DOR poses a risk to pregnancy outcomes.
L1419: ## **Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound**
L1420: The potential for drug abuse, withdrawal, or rebound with DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF was not evaluated but is not anticipated.
L1421: There were no reported overdoses with DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF in any of the Phase 1 trials. Accidental DOR overdose was reported by Week 48 in one DOR-treated PN007 subject (200 mg for two days) and one DOR-treated PN018 DRIVE-FORWARD subject (300 mg at once): no other AEs were reported in these subjects. There is no known specific treatment for overdose with DOR-containing therapy: in the event of an overdose, the patient should be monitored and standard supportive treatment applied as required.
L1422: ## **Patient-Reported Outcome Endpoints**
L1423: In PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD, subjects completed a WPAI at Day 1, Week 4, Week 8, Week 16, and Week 48 (or the discontinuation visit). The WPAI has been used in studies of HIV-infected patients and utilizes a one week recall period. As HIV infection has been shown to have an impact on productivity, the WPAI: General Health Questionnaire (WPAI:GH) was included as an exploratory measurement to describe the impact of these treatments. This patient-reported outcome questionnaire was designed to assess the quantitative impact of health conditions on loss of time and impaired productivity for functional activities such as work-for-pay, school work, and work around the house. There were no statistically significant treatment differences between the DOR/3TC/TDF and EFV/FTC/TDF treatment groups in regards to absenteeism,
L1424: presenteeism, overall work productivity impairment due to health, or activity impairment due to health across all study time points. Because the WPAI:GH was an exploratory endpoint, the study was not specifically powered to detect a treatment difference and is not proposed to be included in labeling.
L1425: ## 9.9. Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1426: ## Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L1427: Neither DOR nor the FDC tablet DOR/3TC/TDF are available on the US market or in any foreign market. Therefore, no postmarketing experience is available for either DOR or DOR/3TC/TDF.
L1428: 3TC has been approved for the treatment of HIV-1 infection since 1995, and TDF has been approved for the treatment of HIV-1 infection since 2001. Identified safety concerns described in the 3TC and TDF labels are included in the labeling for DOR/3TC/TDF.
L1429: ## Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L1430: The safety of DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF are expected to be similar to the individual drug products and aligned with what was observed in the NDA applications. Routine pharmacovigilance will be conducted to monitor for unlabeled or unexpected adverse events.
L1431: ## 9.10. Integrated Assessment of Safety
L1432: The safety data submitted with these NDAs demonstrate a favorable DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF safety profile: no major safety issues unique to DOR were identified in this review.
L1433: PN021 DRIVE-AHEAD provided evidence of statistically significantly lower proportions of subjects experiencing dizziness, sleep disorders and disturbances, and altered sensorium while using DOR/3TC/TDF, compared to EFV/FTC/TDF. These findings are supported by the analysis of NPEs in the Phase 2 trial PN007.
L1434: Both Phase 3 trials demonstrated statistically significant differences for Week 48 changes from baseline of LDL-C and non-HDL-C, favoring DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF over DRV+r and EFV/FTC/TDF, respectively.
L1435: The following adverse reactions occurred in at least five percent of DOR-treated participants in the Phase 3 clinical trials: nausea, fatigue, headache, diarrhea, abdominal pain, abnormal dreams, and dizziness. Grade 1 and 2 total bilirubin elevations occurred more frequently in DOR-containing arms. In general, these elevations were not sustained and not associated with treatment discontinuations or abnormalities in other liver laboratories. Nonclinical toxicology studies identified an imbalance in thyroid adenoma and carcinoma at the highest dose (7 times the DOR AUC exposure at the RHD) in a 2-year rat carcinogenicity study (see Section 5 for further details): no thyroid adenomas or carcinomas were reported in the Phase 2 or 3 DOR-containing trials.
L1436: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L1437: Safety considerations associated with DOR-containing treatment can be adequately described in the label: no risk management beyond standard pharmacovigilance is warranted based on this review.
L1438: ## 10. Advisory Committee Meeting and Other External Consultations
L1439: No Advisory Committee meeting was held for these applications.
L1440: ## 11.Pediatrics
L1441: No studies in the pediatric population have been submitted in the NDA applications. A single initial Pediatric Study Plan (iPSP), intended to support evaluation of both DOR and DOR/3TC/TDF in pediatric subjects, was agreed by the FDA on 3/20/2015. An amended iPSP was agreed by the FDA on 5/24/2017, which proposed a program of formulation development, clinical pharmacology, and clinical trials.
L1442: The Applicant has the following deferral requests for DOR:
L1443: - A deferral of pediatric assessments of DOR in children aged 4 weeks to 23 months, on the grounds that:DOR will be ready for approval and use in adult patients before pediatric studies are complete in this age group;
- Additional safety and PK data in older children and in adults infected with virus harboring transmitted NNRTI resistance mutations are needed to support the initiation of studies in this age group;
- An age-appropriate formulation needs to be identified and shown to be biocomparable to the DOR tablet in adults prior to its administration in this age group.
- A deferral of pediatric assessments of DOR in children aged at least 2 years and weighing less than 35 kg, on the grounds that:DOR will be ready for approval and use in adult patients before pediatric studies are complete in this age group;
- A benefit must first be established in the adult population in the setting of switch before administration to pediatric patients in this age/weight group;
- An age-appropriate formulation needs to be identified and shown to be biocomparable to the adult DOR tablet in adults prior to its administration in this age/weight group.
- A deferral of pediatric assessments of DOR in children aged &lt;18 years and weighing at least 35 kg, on the grounds that DOR will be ready for approval and use in adult patients before pediatric studies are complete in this age/weight group.
L1444: The Applicant has the following deferral requests for DOR/3TC/TDF:
L1445: - A deferral of pediatric assessments of DOR/3TC/TDF in children aged at least 2 years and weighing less than 35 kg, on the grounds that:DOR/3TC/TDF will be ready for approval and use in adult patients before pediatric studies are complete in this age/weight group;
- A benefit must first be established in the adult population in the setting of switch before administration to pediatric patients in this age/weight group;
- An age-appropriate formulation needs to be identified and shown to be biocomparable to the adult DOR/3TC/TDF tablet in adults prior to its administration in this age/weight group.
L1446: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L1447: - A deferral of pediatric assessments of DOR/3TC/TDF in children aged &lt;18 years and weighing at least 35 kg, on the grounds that:DOR/3TC/TDF will be ready for approval and use in adult patients before pediatric studies are complete in this age/weight group;
- Additional safety and PK data to support administration of the 100 mg dose of DOR in adolescents are needed to support initiation of studies of DOR/3TC/TDF in this age/weight group.
L1448: The Applicant has requested (1) a waiver of pediatric assessments of DOR in children aged 0 to &lt;28 days, on the grounds that there is no additional significant therapeutic benefit conferred by DOR in this segment of the pediatric population and, based on a lack of reliable/definitive diagnosis of HIV-1 infection in this age group, the product is unlikely to be used in a substantial number of patients in this category, and (2) a waiver of pediatric assessments of DOR/3TC/TDF in children aged less than 2 years, on the grounds that there is no additional significant therapeutic benefit conferred by DOR/3TC/TDF in this segment of the pediatric population.

L1452: - Information highlighted below are significant changes made to the full prescribing information from the sponsor proposed label submitted on October 23, 2017 for PIFELTRO and DELSTRIGO labels.
- HIGHLIGHTS and TABLE OF CONTENTS were revised for consistency with the rest of the Prescribing Information.
- “Treatment-naïve” was revised to “no prior antiretroviral treatment history” throughout the PI for consistency with newer HIV labels.
- DELSTRIGO label was updated with information from 3TC and TDF labels for consistency.
L1453: (b) (4)
L1454: ## 1 INDICATIONS AND USAGE
L1455: The indications statement was revised to specify that PIFELTRO and DELSTRIGO are indicated for adult patients.
L1456: Revised PIFELTRO indications statement: PIFELTRO™ is indicated in combination with other antiretroviral agents for the treatment of HIV-1 infection in adult patients with no prior antiretroviral treatment history.
L1457: Revised DELSTRIGO indications statement: DELSTRIGO™ is indicated as a complete regimen for the treatment of HIV-1 infection in adult patients with no prior antiretroviral treatment history.
L1458: ## 4 CONTRAINDICATIONS
L1459: Enzalutamide and mitotane were added as examples of strong CYP3A inhibitors that are contraindicated to be coadministered with PIFELTRO and DELSTRIGO.
L1460: ## 6 ADVERSE REACTIONS
L1461: ## 6.1 Clinical Trials Experience
L1462: ## Common Adverse Reactions:
L1463: **PIFELTRO:** Table 1 which displays [REDACTED] (b) (4) was modified to display adverse reactions for all grades in at least 5% of subjects in both DRIVE-FORWARD and DRIVE-AHEAD.
L1464: **DELSTRIGO:** Table 1 was modified to include adverse reactions for all grades in at least 5% of subjects in DRIVE-AHEAD. The adverse reactions listed in Table 1 for both PIFELTRO and DELSTRO labels differ as PIFELTRO also included adverse reactions from DRIVE-FORWARD.
L1465: For the rationale to include all grades, refer to Section 9.4 Treatment Emergent Adverse Events and Adverse Reactions.
L1466: (b) (4)
L1467: **PIFELTRO:** The revised labeling contains the discontinuation rate of study drug due to adverse events for each arm in DRIVE-FORWARD and DRIVE-AHEAD.
L1468: **DELSTRIGO:** The revised labeling contains the discontinuation rate of study drug due to adverse events for DRIVE-AHEAD.
L1469: For rationale, refer to Section 9.5 and 9.10.
L1470: ## **Neuropsychiatric Adverse Events:**
L1471: **PIFELTRO and DELSTRIGO:** Table 2 which displays neuropsychiatric adverse events (NPE) in DRIVE-AHEAD was revised to include the pre-specified NPEs of sleep disorders and disturbances, dizziness, and altered sensorium with treatment difference displayed with confidence interval. Subjects who reported one or more NPE and subjects who reported depression and suicide/self-injury NPEs are described in text without treatment difference because these events were not pre-specified for statistical testing. Information about DRIVE-AHEAD NPEs characterized by severity, onset, and those leading to treatment discontinuation was added as well. For rationale for labeling with respect to display of NPE, treatment difference, 95% CI and p-value for the pre-specified and non-prespecified NPEs, refer to Section 9.5 subsections Neuropsychiatric Analysis and Neuropsychiatric AE Analysis Conclusion.
L1472: Similarly, refer to Section 9.5, subsection Lipid Analysis, for rationale for labeling with respect to display of pre-specified and non-pre-specified lipid parameters.
L1473: ## **Laboratory Abnormalities:**
L1474: (b) (4)
L1475: **PIFELTRO and DELSTRIGO:** Table 3 which displays laboratory abnormalities was revised to retained ULN cutoff pertaining to grade 2-4 events and to include total bilirubin with limit 1.1 - &lt;1.6 ULN and fasted lipid values for cholesterol, LDL, and triglycerides corresponding to grade \ge 3 limits.
L1476: Table 4 which displays [REDACTED] (b) (4) was revised to add baseline and change in lipid levels and included treatment difference and confidence interval for pre-specified lipid levels for LDL and non-HDL cholesterol. For rationale, refer to Section 9.5 (subsection on Lipid Analysis).
L1477: ## 6.2 Postmarketing Experience

L1482: Based on PBPK modeling of the persistence of the CYP3A induction effect, recommendation to allow at least 4 weeks of cessation of drug prior to initiation of PIFELTRO or DELSTRIGO was added for enzalutamide, anticonvulsants, antimycobacterials, mitotane, and St. John's wort.

L1484: ## 7.2 Effect of PIFELTRO on Other Drugs
L1485: ## 7.3 Effects of DELSTRIGO on Other Drugs

L1490: ## 12.3 Pharmacokinetics
L1491: Results summarizing negative clinical drug interaction studies for doravirine was added to include in vitro data on inhibition and induction of CYP enzymes, and inhibition of UGT1A1 and major efflux and update transporters.
L1492: Table 7 which displays clinically significant drug interactions was modified [REDACTED] (b) (4)
L1493: ketoconazole and ritonavir. Although the AUC for DOR increased approximately 3 to 3.5 fold when coadministered with ketoconazole and ritonavir, the increase in DOR exposure was not considered clinically significant. The available safety data from phase 2 and 3 trials was sufficient to support the increased DOR exposures.

L1551: ## 15.2. Financial Disclosure
L1552: The applicant adequately disclosed financial interests/arrangements with clinic investigators as recommended in the guidance for industry, *Financial Disclosure by Clinical Investigators*, and by 21 CFR 54.4. None of the 280, 584, and 573 investigators for PN007, PN018, or PN021 respectively are employed by the applicant, although three investigators are married to Merck employees. Two of the investigators (&lt;1%) have financial interests or arrangements with the applicant, three subinvestigators (all for PN018) did not return the requested information about financial disclosures, and the remaining investigators (&gt;99%) have no financial interests or arrangements with the applicant, as defined in 21 CFR 54.2.
L1553: The investigator financial disclosures do not raise questions about the integrity of the data. The primary efficacy endpoint (proportion of subjects with HIV-1 RNA &lt;50 copies/mL at Week 48) is an objective laboratory measurement that is assessed centrally and not vulnerable to investigator bias. In addition, all three trials were randomized, active-controlled, and double-blind, which would minimize the potential for investigator bias to play a role. Finally, &lt;1% of investigators had financial interests or arrangements with the applicant, and these investigators with financial interests enrolled &lt;1.5% of the subjects.
L1554: In conclusion, the likelihood that trial results were biased based on financial interests is minimal and should not affect the approvability of the application.

L1570: ## 15.5. Clinical Pharmacology
L1571: ## 15.5.1. In Vitro Studies
L1572: ## Plasma Protein Binding
L1573: The binding of [^{3}\text{H}]MK-1439 to plasma proteins was determined by equilibrium dialysis using an HTDialysis apparatus (PK003). [^{3}\text{H}]MK-1439 was added to rat, dog, and human plasma at final concentrations of 0.1 and 1 \mu\text{M}. Phosphate buffered saline (pH 7.4) was added to one side of the dialysis plate and an equal volume of plasma containing [^{3}\text{H}]MK-1439 was added to the other side of the plate in triplicate experiments. The plate was incubated at 37°C under 5% CO_2 for 5 hours. To determine the amount of [^{3}\text{H}]MK-1439 in each sample (disintegration per minute, dpm), triplicate 50 \mu\text{L} aliquots were counted in 5 mL scintillant using a liquid scintillation counter. The unbound fraction in plasma was calculated as follows:
L1574: \text{Fraction unbound} = \frac{\text{DPM in buffer chamber}}{\text{DPM in plasma chamber}}
L1575: Binding was independent of concentration between 0.1 and 1 \mu\text{M} where mean unbound fractions were 24.9%, 25.0, and 33.7 for humans, rats, and dogs, respectively.
L1576: A separate study (PK016) was conducted at higher concentrations of MK-1439 (3 \mu\text{M} and 5 \mu\text{M}) by using a Harvard 96-well equilibrium dialysis plate. The concentrations of MK-1439 were analyzed by LC-MS/MS. The results from this study were similar to the previous observed in PK003.
L1577: The Cmax and Cmin of MK-1439 is 2.3 \mu\text{M} and 0.93 \mu\text{M}, respectively. Therefore, these two studies have covered the clinically relevant concentrations. Unbound fraction of 24% was used in MK-1439 PBPK analysis, which is acceptable.
L1578: The protein binding of the major metabolite of MK-1439, M9, was also evaluated (the applicant's report PK015) in human plasma and it was moderate and did not vary between 0.1 and 1 \mu\text{M} M9. The unbound fraction of M9 (8%-9%) was 3-fold lower than that of MK-1439.
L1579: ## Blood-to-Plasma Partitioning
L1580: The distribution of [^{3}\text{H}]MK-1439 between red blood cells and plasma of rats, dogs, and humans was determined in freshly drawn blood at concentrations of 0.1, 1.0, and 10 \mu\text{M}. The appropriate amount of [^{3}\text{H}]MK-1439 was added to 0.5 mL samples of blood in triplicate, and incubated for 30 minutes at 37°C to reach equilibrium. The incubated blood was centrifuged at 4300 rpm for 30 minutes to separate the red blood cells from the plasma. A 100 \mu\text{L} aliquot from each sample was then added to 5 mL of scintillant and analyzed by liquid scintillation counting to determine the concentration of [^{3}\text{H}]MK-1439 in the plasma (dpm in plasma). As a surrogate to experimentally determine the initial whole blood concentration of [^{3}\text{H}]MK-1439 (dpm in "whole blood"), the appropriate amount of compound was added to 0.5 mL samples of plasma in triplicate. A 100 \mu\text{L} aliquot from each sample was then counted to represent the "whole blood" concentration. The blood to plasma ratio (B:P) was calculated using the following equation:
L1581: B:P = \frac{DPM \text{ in "whole blood"}}{DPM \text{ in plasma separated from blood}}
L1582: The observed blood to plasma concentration ratios ranged from 0.90 to 1.10 and showed no clear concentration dependence. These results indicate no preferential distribution of MK-1439 into blood cells. Therefore, blood and plasma clearance are similar.
L1583: The blood-to-plasma concentration ratios for M9 was also determined in rats, dogs and humans using the same method (the applicant's report PK015). The blood-to-plasma partitioning ratio for metabolite M9 was in general lower across species compared to MK-1439, with values of 0.7 to 0.9 in rats, dogs, and humans (human: 0.7).
L1584: ## Metabolism of [^{3}\text{H}]MK-1439 and Characterization of Human CYPs Involved in the Oxidative Metabolism of [^{3}\text{H}]MK-1439
L1585: The metabolism of [^{3}\text{H}]MK-1439 was investigated in vitro in liver microsomes and hepatocytes from humans. Relatively low turnover was observed but the major metabolite identified in human liver microsomes resulted from oxidative metabolism of the triazole (M9). No significant antiviral activity was observed for M9.
L1586: Incubation of MK-1439 (10 \mu\text{M}) with a series of recombinant human CYPs, CYP1A1, 1A2, 1B1, 2A6, 2B6, 2C8, 2C9*1, 2C9*2, 2C9*3, 2C18, 2C19, 2D6*1, 2E1, 2J2, 3A4, 3A5, and 3A7, demonstrated that oxidation of MK-1439 was carried out primarily by CYP3A4/5 (applicant's non-clinical report PK003).
L1587: To confirm the involvement of CYP3A in the metabolism of MK-1439, studies using an inhibitory anti-CYP3A monoclonal antibody (mAb) were conducted. Incubation of MK-1439 (10 \mu\text{M}) with either human liver microsomes or recombinant CYP3A4 (rCYP3A4) and rCYP3A5 enzymes in the presence of anti-CYP3A mAb demonstrated near complete inhibition of oxidative metabolism. Based on these data, it was concluded that the oxidative metabolism of MK-1439 was mainly catalyzed by CYP3A4 and CYP3A5 (applicant's non-clinical report PK003).
L1588: The kinetic constants for the oxidation of MK-1439 to the metabolite M9 were catalyzed by recombinant cytochrome P450 (CYP) 3A4 and CYP3A5. K_m values for one-site and two-site binding models were determined for both enzymes. The one-site binding K_m for this reaction was similar for both enzymes (20.9 vs. 31.1 \mu\text{M}). Likewise, the high affinity, low capacity K_m of the two-site binding model (K_{m1}) was similar for both enzymes as well (3.6 vs. 5.4 \mu\text{M}). The V_{\text{max}} was approximately 15-20-fold higher for CYP3A4 using either model, so that the intrinsic clearance (C_{\text{Lint}}) ranges for rhCYP3A4 and rhCYP3A5 were 0.110-0.142 and 0.0048-0.0049 \mu\text{L}/\text{min}/\text{pmol CYP}, respectively (applicant's non-clinical report PK017).
L1589: The glucuronidation of MK-1439 was investigated in rat, dog, and human liver microsomes fortified with uridine 5'-diphospho-glucuronic acid (UPDGA). No glucuronidation products were detected after 2 hrs incubation of MK-1439 with liver microsomes fortified with UPDGA for all species.
L1590: The metabolism of M9 was assessed in human hepatocytes. M9 was not metabolized in human hepatocytes following a 2-hr incubation.
L1591: ## Inhibition of CYP Isozymes by MK-1439
L1592: The reversible inhibitory effects of MK-1439 on human liver microsomal activity for CYP1A2, 2B6, 2C8, 2C9, 2C19, 2D6, and 3A4 were evaluated. Pooled human liver microsomes (0.25 mg/mL) were incubated at 37°C in a reaction mixture (0.2 mL final volume) containing the appropriate CYP probe substrate and MK-1439 (0.05 to 100 µM) in 100 mM potassium phosphate buffer (pH 7.4) with 3.14 mM MgCl2 and an NADPH-generating system (2.82 mM glucose-6-phosphate, 1.25 mM NADP, and 1.34 units/mL glucose-6-phosphate dehydrogenase). The enzyme reactions, their respective substrate concentrations (near their *Km* values), and incubation times are summarized in Table 76. The reactions were **APPEARS THIS WAY ON ORIGINAL** terminated by adding 200 µL of methanol for the CYP2D6 and CYP3A4 assays only, or 50 µL of 50% acetonitrile/2.5% formic acid/0.1% methanol (v/v in water) for all other assays. Stop solutions contained the appropriate internal standard. CYP2C8 samples were diluted 1:3 with 10% acetonitrile/0.5% formic acid (v/v in water). The samples were centrifuged for 10 minutes at 3500 rpm before being transferred to a clean 96-well plate and analyzed by LC-MS/MS. The IC50 values were calculated using SigmaPlot v11.0(b) (4) by nonlinear regression.

Refer to Guidance for Industry--- In Vitro Metabolism and Transporter-Mediated Drug-Drug Interaction Studies for the definition of R1 and R1,gut. In vivo DDI study with midazolam shows MK-1439 did not have a clinically meaningful effect on the pharmacokinetics of the CYP3A substrate midazolam.

L1600: Time-Dependent Inhibition of CYP3A4 was evaluated in pooled human liver microsomes (1 mg/mL) were preincubated for 5 to 45 min at 37°C with MK-1439 (10 and 50 µM) in 100 mM potassium phosphate buffer (pH 7.4) containing 1 mM EDTA, 6 mM MgCl2, and an NADPH-generating system. Mifepristone was included as a positive control. The first order rate constants (*k*obs) for inactivation were calculated from the negative slope of the lines by linear regression analysis of the natural logarithm of the remaining activity as a function of time. As shown in Figure 14, **MK-1439 did not cause time-dependent inhibition of CYP3A4 activity** at 10 and 50 µM (*k*obs &lt;0.010 min-1 at both concentrations as compared to 0.084min-1 for the positive control mifepristone (10 µM)).

L1603: ## Induction of CYP3A4 and CYP1A2 in Human Hepatocytes by MK-1439
L1604: The potential for MK-1439 to up-regulate the activity of drug metabolizing enzymes and transporters via ligand-activated nuclear receptors, including the Arylhydrocarbon receptor (AhR), the Constitutive Androstane Receptor (CAR) and the Pregnane X Receptor, was evaluated in cryopreserved plated human hepatocytes from 3 donors. The hepatocytes were treated for 48 hours with vehicle control, MK-1439 (0.1 to 20 \mu\text{M}), or the positive control inducers omeprazole (50 \mu\text{M}), phenobarbital or rifampicin (10 \mu\text{M}) for AhR, CAR, and PXR, respectively. The activation of these receptors was determined by concentration dependent increases in messenger RNA (mRNA) and activity of CYP1A2, 2B6, and 3A4 by monitoring phenacetin O-demethylation, bupropion hydroxylation and testosterone 6\beta-hydroxylation, respectively.
L1605: Results indicated that CYP1A2 mRNA or enzyme activity was not altered significantly by MK-1439 at concentrations of up to 20 \mu\text{M}. The positive control omeprazole induced CYP1A2 mRNA by 21.3- to 32.1-fold and enzyme activity by 8.9- to 22-fold across 3 hepatocyte lots
L1606: MK-1439 did not induce CYP2B6 mRNA or enzyme activity while the positive control phenobarbital induced mRNA by 14- to 18.5-fold and enzyme activity by 7.2- to 13.7-fold across 3 hepatocyte lots.
L1607: MK-1439 did not induce CYP3A4 mRNA or enzyme activity at 0.1-5 \mu\text{M}. An increase in
L1608: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L1609: CYP3A4 mRNA was observed at 10 µM (1.2- to 4.0-fold) and 20 µM (1.4- to 4.2-fold) (Table 78). This effect was small (3.1 to 20.3% across hepatocytes lots, relative to the positive control rifampicin at 10 µM) and had no corresponding effect on CYP3A4 enzyme activity
L1610: These in vitro results indicated that MK-1439 is not likely to cause drug interactions via activation of AhR, CAR, or PXR.

L1617: ## Inhibition of UGT1A1
L1618: Pooled human liver microsomes (0.5 mg/mL) were incubated with 20 µM estradiol, 5 mM UDPGA, 9 mM MgCl2 and 25 µg/mL alamethicin in 200 µL of 81 mM HEPES buffer, pH 7.0 at 37°C for 20 min. Estradiol glucuronidation was quantified in the absence and presence of increasing concentrations of either MK-1439 (0.78 to 100 µM) or nicardipine (a known inhibitor of UGT1A1, 0.078 to 10 µM). Under these conditions, MK-1439 was not an inhibitor of UGT1A1 (IC50&gt;100 µM), but nicardipine inhibited the glucuronidation of 17β-estradiol with an IC50 of 3.2 ± 0.27 µM.
L1619: ## Drug Transporters Involved in the Disposition of MK-1439
L1620: Efflux of MK-1439 by BCRP: Bi-directional transport of [3H] MK-1439 (0.1 to 1 µM) was measured across monolayers of Madin-Darby canine kidney (MDCKII) and MDCKII cells stably expressing human BCRP (MDCKII-BCRP) at 37°C, in the absence and presence of the BCRP inhibitor Ko143 (2 µM). Bi-directional transport of [3H] prazosin (5 µM) was measured in parallel as a positive control.
L1621: The Papp of MK-1439 in control MDCK-II cells was 10.4 to 11.5 × 10-6 cm/s at 0.1 to 1 µM while the positive control prazosin had a Papp of 22.7 × 10-6 cm/s. The ratio of basolateral-to-
L1622: apical/apical-to-basolateral (B-A/A-B) transport was 1.1 in control cells and 1.9 in BCRP-expressing cells. The B-A/A-B ratio in the BCRP-expressing cells was not decreased in the presence of the BCRP inhibitor Ko143. In the same experiment, the BA/AB ratio for [^3H] prasozin was 4.7 in BCRP-transfected cells and decreased to 1.4 in the presence of Ko143, indicating the functionality of the assay. Thus, results from these studies indicated that MK-1439 is not a substrate for BCRP.
L1623: **Efflux of MK-1439 by P-gp:** Bi-directional transport of MK-1439 (0.1 to 1 \muM) was measured across monolayers of porcine renal epithelial cells (LLC-PK1) and LLC-PK1 cells stably expressing human MDR1 (LLC-MDR1) at 37°C. Bidirectional transport of verapamil (1 \muM) was measured in parallel as a positive control. The experiment was performed in triplicate. Over a range of concentrations (0.1, 0.5, and 1 \muM), the B-A/A-B ratio for MK-1439 in P-gp transfected cells was 3.5 to 4.7 (comparable to verapamil, Table 79), compared to 0.9 to 1 in non-transfected cells indicating that MK-1439 is a substrate of P-gp. The mean apparent permeability coefficient (Papp) for 0.1 \muM MK-1439 was 25 x 10-6 cm/sec, and did not change significantly at 0.5 and 1 \muM, indicating good passive permeability (Table 79). The applicant indicated drug interactions with P-gp inhibitors are not anticipated to be significant due to the high permeability of MK-1439. Although based on Guidance for Industry — Waiver of In Vivo Bioavailability and Bioequivalence Studies for Immediate-Release Solid Oral Dosage Forms Based on a Biopharmaceutics Classification System, the absolute bioavailability of MK-1439 is less than 85% (64%) and is not considered to be highly permeable.
L1624: **Table 79: Transport of MK-1439 in Control or P-Glycoprotein Transfected Cells**
L1625: Compound (\muM)	Papp BA/Papp AB Ratio	Papp x 10-6 (cm/s)
LLC-PK1

L1626: **Uptake by OATP1B1 and OATP1B3:** [^3H]MK-1439 (1 \muM) was evaluated as a substrate of the Organic Anion Transporting Polypeptide (OATP)1B1 and OATP1B3 in MDCKII cells stably expressing OATP1B1 or OATP1B3. Rapid, time-dependent uptake of MK-1439 into control cells was observed, likely due to good passive permeability. A small increase in uptake into OATP1B1, but not into OATP1B3, expressing cells, relative to control cells, was observed. This uptake was not inhibited by the OATP1B1 and 1B3 inhibitor bromosulphophthalein (BSP, 100 \muM). Similar studies conducted in HEK-293 cells demonstrated that uptake into OATP1B1- or OATP1B3-expressing cells was similar to that in control cells, indicating that MK-1439 was not a substrate of either transporter. In the same studies, uptake of the OATP1B1 substrate [^3H]estradiol 17\beta-glucuronide (E217\betaG), and the OATP1B3 substrate [^3H]cholecystokinin-8 (CCK8), was effectively inhibited by BSP.
L1627: Follow-up studies in cryopreserved human hepatocytes demonstrated uptake of [^3H]MK-1439 (1 \muM) to be reduced by a maximum of 1.4-fold in the presence of a cocktail of transporter inhibitors (10 \muM cyclosporin A, 10 \muM rifamycin SV, 100 \muM rifampin and 50 \muM quinidine) designed to
L1628: fully inhibit OATP1B1, OATP1B3, NTCP, and OCT1, as compared to in the absence of inhibitors. In the same studies, the uptake of 1 \muM [^3H] E217\betaG, 5 nM [^3H] CCK8, 1 \muM [^3H] taurchocholic acid (TCA) and 1 \muM [^{14}C] tetraethylammonium (TEA), known substrates of OATP1B1, OATP1B3, NTCP (Na^+-taurocholate co-transporting polypeptide) and OCT1, respectively, was reduced by 4.0 to 12.5-fold in the presence of the cocktail, compared to in the absence. These data suggest a weak, but not profound transporter mediated uptake. As observed in the OATP1B1 and 1B3 transfected cells, good passive permeability appeared to be the major driver for the uptake of MK-1439 in hepatocytes, with a weak transporter mediated component.
L1629: ## Inhibition of Drug transporters by MK-1439
L1630: **BCRP:** The ATP-dependent uptake of [^3H]methotrexate ([^3H]MTX; 10 \muM) was measured in membrane vesicles containing human BCRP in the absence or presence of MK-1439 (0 to 75 \muM) or the positive control inhibitor Ko143 (5 \muM). The IC50 of MK-1439 for BCRP was 51 \muM (Table 7). MK-1439's maximal concentrations at the clinical dose of 100 mg are anticipated to be below 4 \muM, with a corresponding unbound concentration below 1 \muM. The in vitro IC50 value (51 \muM) obtained in experiments with transfected cells expressing BCRP was more than 50-fold above DOR free concentrations in plasma (Table 5). Therefore, systemic drug interactions via this transporter are unlikely. However, DOR could inhibit the intestinal efflux of substrates of this transporter and increase their exposure. The theoretical gut lumen concentrations of MK-1439 (400 \mug/mL or 940 \muM, assuming complete dissolution of the 100 mg dose in 250 mL of intestinal fluid) at the therapeutic dose exceed 10-fold of the in vitro IC50. However, the solubility of DOR is limited. In vitro, the solubility of MK-1439 was 120 \mug/mL (282 \muM), so that actual concentrations in the gut lumen are unlikely to exceed this value.
L1631: **P-gp:** Inhibition of intestinal P-gp was not adequately tested. Attempts to test MK-1439 inhibition of P-gp at 300 \muM in vitro resulted in 8.7% inhibition; however, MK-1439 precipitated in the assay media. Thus, although inhibition of gut P-gp cannot be excluded, it is likely to be limited by MK-1439's solubility (282 \muM) and be of no clinical relevance.
L1632: **BSEP:** The ATP-dependent uptake of [^3H]taurocholic acid ([^3H]TCA; 1 \muM) was measured in membrane vesicles containing human BSEP in the absence or presence of MK-1439 (0 to 50 \muM) or the positive control inhibitor atorvastatin (100 \muM). Atorvastatin strongly inhibited ATP-dependent 1 \muM [^3H]TCA uptake into BSEP vesicles, but MK-1439 did not inhibit BSEP at concentrations up to 50 \muM in vitro (Table 7). It is unlikely for MK-1439 to interact with other drugs at the clinical dose via inhibition of this transporter.
L1633: **OATP1B1:** Uptake of [^3H]pitavastatin (0.1 \muM) was measured in Madin-Darby canine kidney (MDCKII) and MDCKII cells stably expressing human OATP1B1(MDCKII-OATP1B1) cells in the absence or presence of MK-1439 (0 to 75 \muM) or the control inhibitor cyclosporine A (CsA; 10 \muM). Results indicated that MK-1439 inhibited OATP1B1 with an IC50 value of 39 \muM (Table 80), while 10 \muM cyclosporin effectively inhibited the uptake of pitavastatin. Based on a bioavailability of approximately 64%, a mean unbound Cmax of 0.54 \muM and an unbound hepatic inlet concentration (Imax,u) of approximately 1.1 \muM, MK-1439 is unlikely to inhibit OATP1B1 in vivo
L1634: (R&lt;1.1). I_{\max,u} after an oral 100 mg dose was estimated assuming that the fraction absorbed was 0.67 (F_a), the rate of absorption was 1.4 \text{ hr}^{-1}, and the C_{\max} was 2.3 \mu\text{M}, with a hepatic blood flow of 90 L/hr and unbound fraction of 0.24.
L1635: **OATP1B3:** Uptake of [^3\text{H}]sulfobromophthalein (0.1 \mu\text{M}) was measured in Madin-Darby canine kidney (MDCKII) and MDCKII cells stably expressing human OATP1B3(MDCKII-OATP1B3) cells in the absence or presence of MK-1439 (0 to 75 \mu\text{M}) or the control inhibitor cyclosporine A (\text{CsA}; 10 \mu\text{M}). As described above, I_{\max,u} of MK-1439 is approximately 1.1 \mu\text{M} and R is less than 1.1. Therefore, **MK-1439 is unlikely to inhibit OATP1B3 in vivo.**
L1636: **OAT1, OAT3, and OCT2:** Uptake of [^3\text{H}]cidofovir (1 \mu\text{M}) was measured in Madin-Darby canine kidney (MDCKII) cells and MDCKII cells stably expressing human OAT1 (MDCKII-OAT1) in the absence or presence of MK-1439 (0 to 75 \mu\text{M}) or the positive control inhibitor probenecid (1 \text{ mM}). Uptake of [^3\text{H}]estrone sulfate (1 \mu\text{M}) was measured in Madin-Darby canine kidney (MDCKII) cells and MDCKII cells stably expressing human OAT3 (MDCKII-OAT3) in the absence or presence of MK-1439 (0 to 75 \mu\text{M}) or the positive control inhibitor probenecid (1 \text{ mM}).
L1637: Uptake of [^{14}\text{C}]metformin ([10 \mu\text{M}]) was measured in Chinese hamster ovary (CHO-K1) and CHO-K1 cells stably expressing human OCT2 (CHO-K1-OCT2) cells in the absence or presence of MK-1439 (0 to 75 \mu\text{M}) or the positive control inhibitor quinidine (100 \mu\text{M}).
L1638: Table 80 shows the in vitro \text{IC}_{50} values obtained in experiments with transfected cells expressing the OAT1, OAT3, and OCT2. Based on Draft Guidance for Industry— In Vitro Metabolism- and Transporter-Mediated Drug-Drug Interaction Studies, the investigational drug has the potential to inhibit these transporters in vivo if the I_{\max,u}/\text{IC}_{50} value is \ge 0.1 for OAT1, OAT3, or OCT2. Therefore, **drug interactions via OAT1, OAT3 or OCT2 is unlikely.**
L1639: **MATE1 and MATE2K:** The inhibitory effect of DOR on the uptake of 5 \mu\text{M} [^{14}\text{C}]metformin was evaluated in CHO-K1 cells transfected with MATE1 and in MDCK-II cells transfected with MATE2K. At 50 \mu\text{M}, the highest concentration tested, 28 and 39% inhibition was observed for MATE1 and MATE2K, respectively (Table 80). Based on Draft Guidance for Industry— In Vitro Metabolism- and Transporter-Mediated Drug-Drug Interaction Studies, the investigational drug has the low potential to inhibit MATEs in vivo if the I_{\max,u}/\text{IC}_{50} value is &lt; 0.02. Therefore, **the risk for interaction of MK-1439 with substrates of these transporters is low.**

L1642: ## 15.5.2. In Vivo Bioavailability and Drug Interaction Studies
L1643: ## Protocol P037: A Study of the Comparative Fed and Fasted Bioavailability of MK-1439 in Healthy Subjects
L1644: Study #	P037	Study Period	December 12, 2014 - December 22, 2014
Clinical Facility	(b) (4)
L1645: **Design:** An open-label, single-dose, randomized, two-period, two-treatment, two-sequence, crossover study in healthy, non-smoking, male and female volunteers.
L1646: **Analysis:** The pharmacokinetic parameters AUC0-last, AUC0-inf, Cmax, Tmax, C24, T½, CL/F, and Vd/F were estimated using a non-compartmental approach. Linear mixed-effects modeling in SAS was utilized to estimate the geometric mean ratios (Fed/Fasted) of MK-1439 exposure PK parameters and their corresponding 90% confidence intervals.
L1647: **Dosing Schedule:** Treatment A consisted of one tablet (100 mg) of MK-1439 administered after an overnight fast of at least 10 hours. Treatment B consisted of one tablet administered 30 minutes after the start of a high fat, high-calorie breakfast. For specific details about the constituents of the meal, please refer to Table 11-2, page 36 of the CSR. A washout period of 7 days (\pm 3 hours) was used and is considered appropriate based on the half-life of MK-1439 (\sim15 hours).
L1648: **PK Sampling:** 11 samples were collected within a 72-hour period. The sampling scheme is considered appropriate based on the Tmax (\sim2 hours) and half-life values.
L1649: **Bioanalytical Method:** The bioanalytical site is (b) (4) The assay, precision and accuracy of standard curve and QC runs, and the incurred sample reanalysis results are acceptable. The study samples were assayed within the long-term stability period established for all analytes.
L1650: Study Population:
Enrolled	14	Completed	14	Discontinued Due to AE	0	PK Population	14
Note: 14 adverse events (AEs) involving 5 subjects in the study were observed. All AEs were judged to be mild in severity. No concomitant medications were reported to be taken by any subjects.

L1652: ## Protocol P026: A Comparative Bioavailability Study of MK-1439A in Healthy Subjects under Fasted Conditions
L1653: Study #	P026	Study Period	February 26, 2015 - March 08, 2015
Clinical Facility	(b) (4)
L1654: **Objective:** To evaluate the comparative bioavailability between MK-1439A (100 mg MK-1439/300 mg 3TC/300 mg TDF) fixed dose combination (FDC) tablets compared to the individual component tablets taken concomitantly in the form of MK-1439 100 mg tablets, Epivir® 300 mg tablets, and Viread® 245 mg tablets; after a single-dose in healthy subjects under fasted conditions.
L1655: **Note:** The Sponsor utilized Viread® in the form of tenofovir disoproxil (as fumarate) as distributed in Europe. Per EMA's Summary of Product Characteristics1, each film-coated tablet containing 245 mg of tenofovir disoproxil (as fumarate) is equivalent to 300 mg of tenofovir disoproxil fumarate (TDF).
L1656: **Design:** An open-label, single-dose, randomized, two-period, two-treatment, two-sequence, crossover study in healthy, non-smoking, male and female volunteers. The inclusion and exclusion criteria are standard for a healthy study population.
L1657: **Analysis:** The pharmacokinetic parameters AUC_{0-\text{last}}, AUC_{0-\infty}, C_{\text{max}}, T_{\text{max}}, T_{1/2}, CL/F, and Vd/F were estimated for MK-1439, lamivudine (3TC), and tenofovir (TFV). C_{24} was also assessed for MK-1439. Linear mixed-effects modeling in SAS was utilized to estimate the geometric mean ratios of MK-1439, 3TC, and TFV exposure PK parameters and their corresponding 90% confidence intervals when administered as an FDC compared to the individual components administered concomitantly.
L1658: **Dosing Schedule:** Treatment A consisted of 100 mg MK-1439/300 mg 3TC/300 mg tenofovir (as TDF) in the form of an FDC. Treatment B consisted of one MK-1439 100 mg tablet, one Epivir® 300 mg tablet and one Viread® 245 mg tablettenofovir disoproxil (as fumarate). Both were administered after an overnight fast of at least 10 hours and for at least 4 hours following drug administration. A washout period of 7 days (\pm 3 hours) was used and is considered appropriate based on the half-life values of MK-1439 (~15 hours), TFV (17 hours), and 3TC (5-7 hours).
L1659: **PK Sampling:** 11 samples were collected within a 72-hour period for all components. The sampling scheme is considered appropriate based on the T_{\text{max}} values of MK-1439 (~2 hours), TFV (1 hour), and 3TC (~ 1 hour), as well as their half-life values. Non-zero pre-dose concentrations at the beginning of Period 2 were observed for Subjects 17 for MK-1439, and Subject 21 for MK-1439, 3TC, and TFV. However, all were less than 3% of the subject's corresponding C_{\text{max}} value and were included in the PK dataset.
L1660: **Bioanalytical Method:** The bioanalytical site is [REDACTED] (b) (4) for 3TC and TFV, whereas MK-1439 samples were analyzed at [REDACTED] (b) (4). The assay precision and accuracy of the quality control and calibration curve, and the incurred sample reanalysis results are acceptable. The study samples were assayed within the long-term stability period established for all analytes.
L1661: ## **Study Population:**
L1662: Enrolled	24	Completed	24	Discontinued Due to AE	0	PK Population	24
L1663: Note: 17 AEs involving 10 subjects were observed. The most common AE was somnolence. One AE was judged to be moderate in severity, while all other AEs were mild. No serious adverse events were reported.

L1692: ## Protocol P044: A Study of the Pharmacokinetics of the Intravenous Microdose Formulation of MK-1439 in Healthy Adults

Clinical Facility	(b) (4)
L1694: **Design:** A single-dose, single-period, open-label trial of MK-1439 in healthy male and female subjects. Each subject received an IV dose of 100 µg MK-1439 administered via infusion syringe over a period of 15 minutes.
L1695: **Analysis:** The pharmacokinetic parameters AUC0-last, AUC0-inf, Cmax as Ceoi (concentration at the end of infusion), T½, CL, and Vdss were estimated using a non-compartmental approach.
L1696: **Dosing Schedule:** A single IV dose of MK-1439, 100 µg, via infusion syringe over a period of 15 minutes. The Applicant utilized 100 µg as the dose per the recommendations of the guidance for IV microdose studies which suggests using 1/1000 of the target clinical dose.
L1697: **PK Sampling:** 17 Blood samples were collected predose and from 5 minutes to 72 hours post start of infusion. The sampling is more frequent compared to other studies and is appropriate to estimate the exposure PK parameters and clearance considering that MK-1439 is administered intravenously.
L1698: **Bioanalytical Method:** The bioanalytical site is Merck (West Point, PA). The assay, its precision and accuracy, and the incurred sample reanalysis results are acceptable. The study samples were assayed within the long-term stability period established.

L1708: ## Protocol P001V01: A Single and Multiple Dose Study to Evaluate the Safety and Pharmacokinetics of Doravirine (MK-1439) and to Evaluate the Effect of Multiple Doses of Doravirine on Midazolam
L1709: ## Information Regarding the Clinical Trial Site and Duration of the Trial
L1710: (b) (4)
L1711: The trial was conducted by
L1712: ## Critical Trial Information
L1713: (Only the MDZ cohort is reviewed here.)
L1714: This was a 3-part study to assess the initial safety, tolerability, and PK of single and multiple oral doses of DOR in healthy male subjects and to compare the tablet formulation of DOR used in Parts I and II of this study with a capsule formulation (Part III). All subjects received drug under fasting conditions (with the exception of the panels where the effect of food was preliminarily evaluated). All inclusion and exclusion criteria, including prohibited concomitant medications appeared standard for a healthy volunteer study and is acceptable.
L1715: Panel	Dose Assignment
C†	30 mg once-daily x 10-days
D†	60 mg once-daily x 10-days
E‡	120 mg once-daily x 14-days (Days 1-14) and 2.0 mg Midazolam HCL syrup (single doses Days -1 and 13)§
F†	240 mg once-daily x 10-days
† Within Panels C, D, and F, 6 subjects were randomized to receive doravirine and 2 subjects to receive matching placebo according to a computer-generated allocation schedule.
‡ Within Panel E, 8 subjects were randomized to receive doravirine and 2 subjects to receive matching placebo according to a computer-generated allocation schedule.
§ On Day -1 and Day 13, all 10 subjects received open-label midazolam.
L1716: **NOTE:** A subsequent single ascending dose (SAD) and multiple ascending dose (MAD) study was conducted upon completion of this one thus, the SAD and MAD portion of this study will *not* be reviewed. However, Panel E of the MAD assessment (Part II) will be reviewed for the midazolam (MDZ) interaction assessment. In addition, Part III of this study was not crucial for development of DOR and will not be reviewed.
L1717: ## Sample Collection and Bioanalysis
L1718: ## Sample Collection
L1719: Plasma samples for MDZ concentration assessments were collected up to 24 hrs post-dose.
L1720: ## Bioanalytical method
L1721: Acceptable precision and accuracy for all standard curve and QC runs. All samples were analyzed within the long-term storage stability duration of 185 days.
L1722: ## Results
L1723: A total of 9 subjects completed Part II, Panel E of the study. No concomitant medications were reported to be taken by any subjects in this panel. The most common AEs reported following treatment with doravirine or placebo were headache and back pain. Most AEs were considered by the Investigator to be of mild or moderate intensity; however, one subject (b) (6) experienced 3 laboratory AEs which were mild to severe in intensity and not considered related to study drug (significantly elevated AST from Days 6 through 9; CPK from Day 1 until Day 2 and Days 5 through 13; and myoglobin on Day 7).

L1730: ## **Protocol P006: A Single and Multiple Dose Study to Evaluate the Safety, Tolerability, and Pharmacokinetics of Doravirine (MK-1439)**
L1731: ## **Information Regarding the Clinical Trial Site and Duration of the Trial**
L1732: The trial was conducted by [REDACTED] from 11/10/11 to 6/1/12.
L1733: ## **Critical Trial Information**
L1734: This was a double-blind, randomized, placebo-controlled, single-rising and multiple dose study to evaluate the PK, safety, and tolerability of DOR in healthy adult male subjects after single dose administration and multiple dose administration for up to 10 days using the oral [REDACTED] tablet (tablet used in pivotal phase 3 trial, Study 018). Two panels (A and B) of 8 subjects each received DOR or matching placebo in a 3:1 ratio, respectively, in up to 3 treatment periods. All subjects received drug under fasting conditions in all periods. See table below for the breakdown of doses received in each period of Panels A and B. There was at least a 7-day washout period between study drug administration at the beginning of each period. All inclusion and exclusion criteria, including prohibited concomitant medications appeared standard for a healthy volunteer study and is acceptable.

L1753: ## Protocol P008: A Study to Investigate the Absorption, Metabolism, Excretion, and Mass Balance of MK-1439
L1754: ## Information Regarding the Clinical Trial Site and Duration of the Trial
L1755: The trial was conducted by [REDACTED] (b) (4) from 6/3/13 to 6/26/13.
L1756: ## Critical Trial Information
L1757: This was a single-dose, open-label, 1-period study to investigate the absorption, metabolism, excretion, and mass balance of MK-1439 in healthy adult male subjects (N=6). On Day 1, a single average oral dose of approximately 351 mg (\sim 211 \mu Ci) ^{[14C]}MK-1439 (actual doses ranged from 349 mg – 353 mg [210 – 212 \mu Ci]) was administered to each subject with 240 mL of water after an overnight fast. ^{[14C]}MK-1439 was supplied as a [REDACTED] (b) (4) at the clinical research unit. A dose of 350 mg was used to approximate clinically efficacious exposures derived from a 100-150 mg tablet dose.
L1758: Blood, urine, and fecal samples were collected to measure total radioactivity (plasma, urine, and fecal samples), MK-1439 concentrations (plasma samples only), and metabolic profiling (plasma, urine, and fecal samples) for at least 168 hours postdose (Day 8). All inclusion and exclusion criteria, including prohibited concomitant medications appeared standard for a healthy volunteer study and is acceptable.
L1759: ## Sample Collection and Bioanalysis
L1760: ## Sample Collection
L1761: Plasma, urine, and fecal samples were collected up to 168 hrs post-dose.
L1762: ## Bioanalytical method
L1763: Acceptable precision and accuracy for all standard curve and QC runs. All samples were analyzed within the long-term storage stability duration of 185 days.
L1764: ## Results
L1765: The only reported concomitant medication taken by any subject (while on study) was magnesium hydroxide and it was determined that it would not affect study assessment.
L1766: Total mean recovery of radioactivity following a single oral dose of ^{[14C]}MK-1439 was approximately 101% (95% CI: 97.5% - 105%), with approximately 10.8% excreted in urine and 90.4% in feces (84.1% as unchanged drug). The primary components in urine were M9 and unchanged MK-1439, accounting for approximately 6.7% and 2.2% of the administered dose, respectively.
L1767: MK-1439 was the major circulating component of total radioactivity detected in plasma, accounting for approximately 76% and 78% of the plasma total radioactivity AUC0-48 and Cmax, respectively. The primary components in urine were M9, accounting for 6.7% of the dose, and MK-1439, accounting for 2.2% of the radioactive dose. M8, M10, and M18 were also detected in urine, but combined represented approximately 0.5% of the administered dose. The mean plasma MK-1439 to total radioactivity AUC ratio was approximately 0.77.
L1768: Total radioactivity in plasma was below the LLOQ of 94 ng equivalents/g (221 nM equivalents) in all subjects by 120 hours postdose, and plasma MK-1439 concentrations fell below the LLOQ of 1.00 ng/mL (2.35 nM) in half the subjects (3) by 168 hours postdose. Total radioactivity in urine and feces samples was present at quantifiable levels up to the last collection interval for each subject.
L1769: ## Conclusions
L1770: Mass balance was achieved in all subjects with 101% of the radioactive dose being recovered over an 8-day collection period. Most of the MK-1439 dose was excreted in feces (90.4%) with the majority as unchanged drug (84.1%). The majority of the absorbed dose was excreted in urine both as unchanged drug (2.2%) and metabolites (7.2%). Thus, the predominant mechanism of elimination of the absorbed dose was metabolism (primarily forming the oxidative metabolite M9). All of the metabolites detected in the excreta and in circulation have also been observed in either rats or dogs.
L1771: ## Protocol P029: A Study of the Comparative Fed and Fasted Bioavailability of MK-1439A in Healthy Subjects
L1772: ## Information Regarding the Clinical Trial Site and Duration of the Trial
L1773: The trial was conducted by [REDACTED](b) (4) from 1/26/15 to 2/5/15.
L1774: ## Critical Trial Information
L1775: This was an open-label, single-dose, randomized, two-period, two-treatment, crossover, comparative fed and fasted bioavailability study conducted using the same tablet formulation of doravirine (DOR)/lamivudine (3TC)/tenofovir disoproxil fumarate (TDF) 100/300/300 mg that was used in phase 3 trials. The treatments were as follows:
L1776: ## Treatment A
L1777: - one FDC tablet administered 30 minutes after the start of a high-fat, high calorie breakfast.
L1778: ## Treatment B
L1779: - one FDC tablet administered after an overnight fast of at least 10 hours.
L1780: The washout between drug administration for each subject was 7 days (\pm 3 hours). The content of the high-fat meal was: 997 total kcal with 50.6% coming from fat, 34.1% coming from carbohydrates, and 14.4% coming from protein.
L1781: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807

L1789: A total of 13 subjects completed the study. There were 16 AEs involving 6 subjects in the study. One AE was judged to be moderate in severity while all the others were mild. L1790: **NOTE:** Subject concentration data from period I were excluded from the PK and statistical analyses due to the uncertainty of a potential for a drug interaction between DOR/3TC/TDF and amoxicillin.

L1802: ## Conclusions
L1803: MK-1439 AUC0-inf and Cmax were similar after administration with a high fat meal or in the fasted state, while C24 was increased by 26% after a high-fat meal. 3TC AUC0-inf was similar after administration with a high-fat meal or in the fasted state, while Cmax was decreased by 19% after a high-fat meal. Tenofovir AUC0-inf was increased by 27% and Cmax was decreased by 12% after administration with a high-fat meal compared to the fasted state. None of these changes are considered clinically relevant and the DOR/3TC/TDF tablet can be taken with or without food, as directed in the phase 3 trials.
L1804: ## Protocol P003: A Study to Evaluate the Effect of Multiple Doses of Tenofovir on the Single Dose Pharmacokinetics of Doravirine (DOR, MK-1439)
L1805: **Study Design:** This was an open-label, 2-period, fixed-sequence study in eight young healthy male subjects. In Period 1, all subjects received a single oral dose of 100 mg DOR. In Period 2, the same subjects received a single daily dose of 300 mg tenofovir disoproxil fumarate (TDF) for 18 days with coadministration of a single dose of 100 mg DOR on Day 14. Tenofovir has no effect on DOR AUC0-inf and C24, but reduced DOR Cmax by 20%, which is not considered clinically significant.

L1818: ## Protocol P002: A Study to Evaluate the Effect of Multiple Doses of Ritonavir on the Single Dose Pharmacokinetics of Doravirine (DOR, MK-1439)
L1819: **Study Design:** This was an open-label, fixed-sequence, 2-period study in eight (8) healthy young male subjects 18 to 50 years of age. In Period 1, all subjects received a single oral dose of 50 mg of DOR. There was a washout period of at least 7 days between study drug administration in Period 1 and the first dose of study drug in Period 2. In Period 2, the same subjects received 100 mg of ritonavir twice daily (b.i.d.) for 20 days. On Day 14, all subjects received the morning (AM) dose of ritonavir in combination with a single oral dose of 50 mg DOR. Subjects consumed a meal within 30 minutes prior to or after drug administration except the Day 1 AM dose of DOR and Day 14 AM dose of ritonavir and DOR, when the drug products were administered under fasted conditions.

L1826: **Results:** Consistent with the in vitro data, ritonavir, a CYP3A/P-gp inhibitor, significantly increased DOR concentrations.

L1830: ## **Protocol P010: A Study to Evaluate the Effect of Multiple Doses of Ketoconazole on the Single Dose Pharmacokinetics of Doravirine (DOR, MK-1439) in Healthy Subjects**
L1831: **Study Design:** This was an open-label, fixed-sequence, 2-period study in ten (10) healthy subjects 18 to 50 years of age. In Period 1, all subjects received a single oral dose of 100 mg of DOR. There was a washout period of at least 7 days between study drug administration in Period 1 and the first dose of study drug in Period 2. In Period 2, the same subjects received oral doses of 400 mg ketoconazole QD for 10 days, with co-administration of a single oral dose of 100 mg DOR on Day 2.
L1832: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L1833: There was a 7-day washout between the single oral dose administration of Period 1 and the first oral dose administration of Period 2. Subjects were required to fast for at least 10 hours before dosing on Day 1 of Period 1 and Day 2 of Period 2, and for at least 4 hours thereafter. In Period 2 (except on the morning of Day 2, when DOR was co-administered with ketoconazole following an overnight fast), subjects were required to fast for at least 1 hour prior to and at least 2 hours post ketoconazole dosing.

L1843: ## **Protocol P011: A Study to Assess the Effects of Single and Multiple Doses of Rifampin on the Single-Dose Pharmacokinetics of doravirine (DOR, MK-1439) in Healthy Adult Subjects**
L1844: **Study Design:** This was an open-label, 2-period, fixed-sequence study to assess the effects of single and multiple doses of rifampin on the single-dose pharmacokinetics of DOR. Eleven (11) healthy non-tobacco using male adult subjects were enrolled in this study. For Treatment A (Period 1), subjects received a single oral dose of 100 mg DOR on Day 1. For Treatment B (Period 2), subjects received a single oral dose of 600 mg rifampin co-administered with a single oral dose of 100 mg DOR on Day 1. For Treatment C (Period 2), subjects received multiple oral doses of 600 mg rifampin QD for 15 days (Days 4 to 18) co-administered with a single oral dose of 100 mg DOR on Day 17. The washout period was at least 7 days between dosing in Period 1 and first dosing in Period 2. All study medications were administered under fasted conditions.
L1845: **Formulation:**
DOR 100-mg pre-market formulation oral compressed tablets (OCT), Lot # WL00047831;

The PK samples for DOR were collected at predose, 0.5, 1, 1.5 2, 3, 6, 12, 24, 48, and 72 hours (prior to Treatment C dosing) following Treatment B dosing on Day 1 of Period 2. The PK samples for DOR were also collected at predose, 0.5, 1, 1.5 2, 3, 6, 12, 24, 36, and 48 hours following Treatment C dosing on Day 17 of Period 2.
L1847: **Bioanalytical Methods:** Plasma samples collected for DOR concentrations were analyzed by Merck Sharpe &amp; Dohme, Corp. (Oss, The Netherlands). L1857: Note: One subject, Subject (b) (6), was discontinued from the study on Day 17 of Period 2 due to a failed drug screen at check-in on Day 16 of Period 2.
L1858: **Reviewer's Comment:** These results indicate that a single dose of rifampin decreased DOR volume of distribution. According to A. Grover and L.Z. Benet (Effect of Drug Transporters on Volume of Distribution, The AAPS Journal, Vol. 11, No. 2, June 2009), "interactions at uptake transporters at the liver" can "lead to a significant decrease in volume of distribution". The results from single dose rifampin DDI study show that the inhibitors of OATP1B1/3 may have an effect on DOR volume of distribution, which leads to increased Cmax and reduced terminal half-life. However, because DOR has a relative large safety margin, this effect is not considered clinically significant.
L1859: ## **Conclusion:**
L1860: - Single-dose co-administration of rifampin with a single oral dose of DOR results in a 40% increase in Cmax but no significant effect on the AUC and C24hr of DOR, suggesting that DOR may be a substrate for OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 uptake transporters and increased Cmax is due to decreased volume of distribution. Modulation of OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 is not likely to have clinically meaningful effects on the pharmacokinetics of DOR.
- When DOR is co-administered with multiple oral doses of rifampin, DOR AUC and C24hr are decreased 88% and 97%, respectively, confirming that DOR is a sensitive CYP3A and P-gp substrate.
- The C24hr of DOR is &lt; 54 nM when a single oral dose of 100 mg DOR is co-administered with multiple oral doses of rifampin, suggesting that the clinical efficacy of DOR may be
L1861: reduced when co-administered chronically with rifampin or other strong CYP3A inducers, thus, coadministration of strong CYP3A inducers with DOR should be contraindicated.
L1862: ## **Protocol P020: An Open-label, Multiple-Dose Study to Investigate the Effect of Switching from Efavirenz Therapy to MK-1439 on the Pharmacokinetics of MK-1439**
L1863: **Study Design:** This was an open-label, 3-period, fixed-sequence, multiple-dose study to investigate the effect of switching from efavirenz therapy to MK-1439 on the pharmacokinetics of MK-1439. Twenty (20) healthy male and female subjects were enrolled. In Period 1, subjects received multiple oral doses of 100 mg MK-1439 once daily (QD) from Days 1 to 5. In Period 2, subjects received multiple oral doses of 600 mg efavirenz QD from Days 1 to 14. In Period 3, subjects received multiple oral doses of 100 mg MK-1439 QD from Days 1 to 14. There was a 7-day washout between the last dose of MK-1439 in Period 1 and the first dose of efavirenz in Period 2. There was no washout between Periods 2 and 3. All drugs were administered under fasted conditions.
L1864: ## **Formulation:**

L1867: **PK Sampling:** In Period 1, blood samples for determination of MK-1439 plasma concentration were collected predose on Days 1 to 5 and at specified time points up to 24 hours (0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 6, 12, and 24 hours) on Day 1 and Day 5. In Period 2, blood samples for determination of efavirenz plasma concentration were collected predose on Day 1. In Period 3, blood samples for MK-1439 plasma concentration were taken for 24 hours (0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 6, 12, and 24 hours) on Day 1 and Day 14, and predose samples for MK-1439 and efavirenz were collected on Days 1 - 14. A sample for efavirenz concentration was also collected 24 hours following the MK-1439 dose on Day 14. In addition, blood samples for CYP2B6 genotype determination were also obtained from consented subjects.

The standard curve and QC data indicated that the plasma assay method for DOR was precise and accurate. All samples were analyzed in the timeframe supported by frozen stability storage data.

L1879: Figure 16 presents the GM and 90% CIs of the plasma MK-1439 C24 and efavirenz concentrations (Cefv) versus day during the multiple-dose administration of 100 mg MK-1439 with efavirenz pretreatment. Subject (b) (6) was excluded from the primary Cefv analysis since this subject is a slow metabolizer (CYP2B6*6/*6). In addition, the plasma efavirenz Cefv values on Day 15 for Subjects (b) (6) were not included in the Applicant's primary Cefv analysis since the concentrations are questionable. On Day 15 of Period 3 at the 336 hour, samples collected for efavirenz for Subject (b) (6) were possibly switched according to the pharmacokinetic laboratory analysis. The identity of the samples cannot be confirmed or denied based on the available data and are considered missing for both subjects at this time point. Secondary Cefv analyses was conducted with the following scenarios: including Subject (b) (6) but excluding the questionable concentrations for Subjects (b) (6), and excluding Subject (b) (6) but including the questionable concentrations. The results of all 3 analyses are comparable. Figure 16 shows that as MK-1439 concentrations increased, efavirenz concentrations were decreased.

L1882: ## **Conclusion:**
L1883: - Following multiple-dose efavirenz pretreatment relative to no pretreatment, single-dose MK-1439 AUC and Cmax were reduced by approximately 62% and 35%, respectively.
- Following multiple-dose efavirenz pretreatment relative to no pretreatment, multiple-dose Day 14 AUC and Cmax were reduced by approximately 32% and 14%, respectively.
- Following multiple-dose efavirenz pretreatment relative to no pretreatment, MK-1439 C24 was initially reduced by ~85% after a single dose, and gradually recovered, but was still reduced by ~50% after 14 days of MK-1439 dosing.
- Efavirenz concentrations decreased steadily following cessation of dosing but remained detectable in all subjects for 9 days following cessation of therapy.
L1884: ## **Protocol P035: A Study to Evaluate the Effect of Multiple Doses of Rifabutin on the Single-Dose Pharmacokinetics of Doravirine (MK-1439) in Healthy Subjects and Study Report 04R3RC: Nonparametric Superposition (NPS) Projection of Steady State Pharmacokinetics of MK-1439 Co-administered with Rifabutin**
L1885: **Study Design:** This was an open-label, 2-period, fixed-sequence trial to evaluate the effect of multiple doses of rifabutin on the single-dose PK of DOR. Eighteen healthy subjects were enrolled. In Period 1, subjects received a single 100 mg dose of DOR followed by a 7-day washout. In Period 2 subjects received 300 mg QD of rifabutin on Day 1 through Day 16. On Day 14, a single dose of DOR was co-administered with rifabutin. Doravirine was administered under fasted conditions.
L1886: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L1887: With the exception of Period 2, Day 14 (doravirine PK day), rifabutin was administered 30 minutes after a meal to improve GI tolerability. On Day 14, rifabutin was administered under fasted conditions to reduce variability in doravirine PK.
L1888: Individual steady state DOR plasma concentration-time profiles for DOR co-administered with rifabutin for QD and BID administration were projected using the non-parametric superposition (NPS) module in Phoenix WinNonlin® Version 6.4. Individual plasma concentration-time profiles of DOR following a 100 mg single oral dose of DOR coadministered with 300 mg QD rifabutin were used as the input for the NPS.
L1889: ## **Formulation:**
L1890: Doravirine 100 mg Tablets (Merck Sharp &amp; Dohme Corp., USA), Lot No.: WL00059426;
Mycobutin® (rifabutin) 150 mg Capsules, USP (Pharmacia &amp; Upjohn Co., Division of Pfizer Inc., USA), Lot No.: X287A

L1908: **Reviewer's Comment:** Rifabutin is a moderate inducer of CYP3A. Co-administration of doravirine with rifabutin at steady-state reduced the AUC0-inf and C24 of doravirine by approximately 50% and 68%, respectively. However, the peak concentrations of doravirine were not affected by coadministration of rifabutin. The NPS prediction for doravirine PK following doravirine 100 mg single dose or doravirine Day 1 PK following doravirine 100 mg QD + rifabutin 300 mg QD is generally consistent with the observed doravirine PK results in Study P035. 100 mg BID DOR co-administered with 300 mg QD rifabutin achieves similar C24, AUC0-24, and Cmax values to a dose of 100 mg QD DOR in the absence of a CYP3A inducer.
L1909: The effect of rifabutin on doravirine concentrations is not as high as the effect of rifampin (reduced doravirine AUC by 88%), and increase doravirine frequency from QD to BID when coadministered with rifabutin may be a viable treatment alternative for HIV patients co-infected with *Mycobacterium tuberculosis* infection with dose adjustment.
L1910: ## **Conclusion:**
L1911: - Doravirine AUC0-inf was decreased by 50% and C24 was decreased by 68% when doravirine was co-administered with multiple doses of rifabutin compared to doravirine administered alone. Doravirine Cmax was similar when either administered alone or with multiple doses of rifabutin.
- Based on NPS projections, 100 mg BID DOR co-administered with 300 mg QD rifabutin achieves similar C24, AUC0-24, and Cmax values to a dose of 100 mg QD DOR in the absence of a CYP3A inducer.
L1912: ## **Protocol P036: A Study to Evaluate the Effect of MK-1439 at Steady-State on the Pharmacokinetics of Atorvastatin in Healthy Subjects**
L1913: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L1914: **Study Design:** This was an open-label, 2-period, fixed-sequence, PK drug interaction trial to evaluate the effect of DOR on the PK of atorvastatin in healthy male and female subjects. Sixteen subjects were enrolled in this trial. In Period 1, subjects received a single dose of 20 mg atorvastatin. In Period 2 subjects were administered 100 mg DOR QD from Days 1 to 8 and a single dose of 20 mg atorvastatin on Day 5. There was a 72-hour washout between Periods 1 and 2. Subjects fasted from at least 8 hours prior to and until at least 4 hours after atorvastatin drug administration in both periods.
L1915: ## **Formulation:**
L1916: MK-1439 100 mg Tablets (Merck Sharp &amp; Dohme Corp., USA), Lot No.: WL00059342;
Atorvastatin - Lipitor® 20 mg Tablets [REDACTED](b) (4) Lot No.: J07799
L1917: **PK Sampling:** In each period, 13 samples were collected for atorvastatin concentrations at the following time points: prior to dosing (0-hour) and at 0.25, 0.5, 1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 12, 24, 36, 48 and 60 hours after atorvastatin drug administration. In Period 2, the 24- and 48- samples after atorvastatin 20 mg tablet drug administration were collected within 5 minutes prior to the dosing for MK-1439 100 mg Tablets.
L1918: **Bioanalytical Methods:** The standard curve and QC data indicated that the plasma assay method for DOR was precise and accurate. All samples were analyzed in the timeframe supported by frozen stability storage data.

The AEs were not considered related to study drugs.
L1934: As shown in Figure 19, there was a greater variability associated with Cmax ratio as compared to AUC0-inf and AUC0-last.
L1935: **Figure 16: Individual Ratios, GMR and 90% CI for Atorvastatin Plasma PK Parameters Following the Administration of a Single Dose of 20 mg Atorvastatin Alone or Co-administered with the 5th Dose of 100 mg MK-1439 QD (Source: Applicant's study report)**
L1936: **Reviewer's Comment:** Atorvastatin is a substrate of CYP3A, OATP1B1, MDR1, BCRP and P-gp. The study shows that MK-1439 has minimal impact on the disposition of atorvastatin, which is consistent with in vitro studies and other in vivo DDI studies, where MK-1439 is shown to have no effect on CYP3A-mediated metabolism or OATP1B1, MDR1, P-gp or BCRP transporters. It is not clear why Cmax is lower when MK-1439 is coadministered with atorvastatin. It may be partially due to a greater variability associated with Cmax ratio as compared to the ratios of other PK parameters. This difference in Cmax for atorvastatin is not expected to be clinically significant.
L1937: **Conclusion:** MK-1439 has no clinically significant effect on atorvastatin concentrations.
L1938: ## **Protocol P016: A 2-way Steady State Interaction Study of MK-1439 and Dolutegravir**
L1939: **Study Design:** This was an open-label, 3-period, fixed-sequence trial to evaluate the PK of MK-1439 and dolutegravir when co-administered in healthy male and female subjects. Twelve subjects were enrolled. In Period 1, subjects received 50-mg dolutegravir QD for 7 days. In Period 2, subjects were administered 200-mg MK-1439 QD for 7 days. In Period 3, subjects were administered 50-mg dolutegravir + 200-mg MK-1439 QD for 7 days. There was at least a 7-day washout between Period 1 and Period 2. There was no washout between Period 2 and Period 3.
L1940: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation – NDA 210806 and NDA 210807
## Doravirine (DOR) and DOR/3TC/DOR
L1941: Dosing in the morning of Day 7 (profile day) was performed following a fasting period of \ge 10 hours in each period. On Day 7 of each treatment period, a standard lunch and dinner (no breakfast) were provided at 4 and 7 hours after dosing. During Day 1 to Day 6 dosing, standardized meals (including breakfast approximately 1 hour after dosing) was served.
L1942: ## **Formulation:**
L1943: Product Name	Product Potency	Dosage Form	Lot-No.
MK-1439	100 mg	Tablet	WL00054839
Dolutegravir	50 mg	Tablet	WL00054840
L1944: **PK Sampling:** In each period, blood samples were collected for MK-1439 and/or dolutegravir concentrations prior to dosing (0-hour) on Day 7 and at 0.5, 1, 1.5, 2, 3, 6, 12, and 24 hours following drug administration. In addition, predose samples were collected on Day 5 and 6.
L1945: **Bioanalytical Methods:** The standard curve and QC data indicated that the plasma assay methods for MK-1439 and dolutegravir were precise and accurate. All samples were analyzed in the timeframe supported by frozen stability storage data.
